# Rigveda Samhita — Volume 15 (Kannada → English translation)

### Printed p. 2

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 3, Sū. 24.* (Pīṭhike, continued from the preceding page.)

Besides describing their great deeds with qualifying words, the deeds done by Bṛhaspati are described in the Ṛks addressed to Brahmaṇaspati, and the deeds of Brahmaṇaspati in the Ṛks addressed to Bṛhaspati, by mutual exchange. Therefore, although the names are different, the sage Gṛtsamada seems to have held the idea that these two deities are one and the same deity. As time went on this idea changed, and it seems to have given room to the idea that Bṛhaspati is one and Brahmaṇaspati another. Room for such changes is seen also in the case of other deities. For instance, in "mahān indraḥ" the word महान् (*mahān*) is an epithet of Indra, yet it is laid down that Indra is one deity and Mahān Indra another deity. In the same way this idea also fits the deities Agni, Agniḥ Śuciḥ, Agniḥ Pāvakaḥ, etc. This matter will be discussed in the last part of this Introduction.

The sage Yāska has said as follows about Brahmaṇaspati in his Nirukta:

> ब्रह्मणस्पतिः । ब्रह्मणस्पतिर्ब्रह्मणः पाता ना पालयिता ना ॥

> *brahmaṇaspatiḥ | brahmaṇaspatirbrahmaṇaḥ pātā nā pālayitā nā ‖*

(Reference as printed: ಸಿ. 10-14.)

Here the word ब्रह्म (*brahma*) (brahma) means food, wealth and hymn of praise (Ni. 3-9). Therefore ब्रह्मणः पाता (*brahmaṇaḥ pātā*) means "the protector of food", that is, the lord or master of food (nourishment). To show how Brahmaṇaspati is lord of food, Yāska gives as an example:

> अश्मास्यमवतं ब्रह्मणस्पतिर्मधुधारमभि यमोजसातृणत् ।
> तमेव विश्वे पपिरे स्वर्दृशो बहु साकं सिसिचुरुत्समुद्रिणं ॥

> *aśmāsyamavataṃ brahmaṇaspatirmadhudhāramabhi yamojasātṛṇat |*
> *tameva viśve papire svardṛśo bahu sākaṃ sisicurutsamudriṇaṃ ‖*

(Reference as printed: ಮಿ. ಸಂ. 2-24-4.)

giving this Ṛk as the example, and for it gives the explanation:

> अशनवंतमास्यंतनवंतमनातितं ब्रह्मणस्पतिर्मधुधारमभियमोजसा बले-
> नाभ्यतृणत्तमेव सर्वे पिबंति रश्मयः सूर्यदृशो बह्वेनं सह
> सिंचंत्युत्समुद्रिणमुदकवंतं ॥

> *aśanavaṃtamāsyaṃtanavaṃtamanātitaṃ brahmaṇaspatirmadhudhāramabhiyamojasā bale-*
> *nābhyatṛṇattameva sarve pibaṃti raśmayaḥ sūryadṛśo bahvenaṃ saha*
> *siṃcaṃtyutsamudriṇamudakavaṃtaṃ ‖*

(Reference as printed: ಸಿ. 10-13.)

The main purport is this: with the help of the sun's rays the clouds drink the water of the sea and cause that water to fall specially on the earth as rain; from the rain come the herbs and trees, and from the herbs and trees food and other nourishment is obtained; therefore Brahmaṇaspati is the lord of food. In the same way, when the word ब्रह्म (*brahma*) is taken to mean hymn of praise or wealth, one must say that Brahmaṇaspati is the lord of the mantras or of wealth.

### Printed p. 3

*Running head: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, A. 2, A. 7, Va. 1 (as printed), p. 3.*

The commentator (Sāyaṇa) has stated this very meaning when commenting on the Ṛk quoted above.

Mantras addressed to Brahmaṇaspati are numerous in the Ṛgveda. In those mantras it is said that Brahmaṇaspati gives wealth and food. Many people doubt whether this Brahmaṇaspati is a separate deity or a changed form of Agni. Since other deities such as Agni also give wealth and food, some argue that Brahmaṇaspati is a special form of Agni. Yāska is silent on this point, that is, he has said nothing clearly. Even in the Ṛks addressed to Brahmaṇaspati there is no authority whatever for saying that this deity is a changed form of Agni. Therefore it may be said freely that Brahmaṇaspati is a separate deity, like Indrāgnī.

In his Nirukta, explaining the derivation of the word Bṛhaspati, Yāska says:

> बृहस्पतिः बृहतः पाता वा पालयिता वा ।

> *bṛhaspatiḥ bṛhataḥ pātā vā pālayitā vā |*

(Reference as printed: ಸಿ. 10-11.)

On this the commentator Durgācārya says: "Why Bṛhaspati? 'Bṛhataḥ pātā or pālayitā': the protector, guardian of this great world or of water; पालयिता (*pālayitā*) also means protector only — the roots differ, the meaning is one." That is, he is the preserver of the great world or of water, the one who protects. He says that although for expressing the sense of the word पति (*pati*) (pati) there are words formed from different roots, पाता (*pātā*) and पालयिता (*pālayitā*), the meaning is only one.

In many places, explaining बृहतः पाता (*bṛhataḥ pātā*), Sāyaṇa gives for the word बृहतः (*bṛhataḥ*) the meaning बृहतः यज्ञाख्यकर्मणः (*bṛhataḥ yajñākhyakarmaṇaḥ*) or वृष्टाख्यकर्मणः (*vṛṣṭākhyakarmaṇaḥ*), that is, "of the rite in the form of sacrifice" or "of the rite in the form of granting rain". Since Bṛhaspati, as priest of the gods, performs the sacrificial rite, and since he brings about the rain that men need, both these epithets are natural. Yāska gives as an example the Ṛk describing Bṛhaspati's priesthood and his granting of rain:

> यद्देवापिः शंतनवे पुरोहितो होत्राय वृतः कृपयन्नदीधेत् ।
> देवश्रुतं वृष्टिवनिं रराणो बृहस्पतिर्वाचमस्मा अयच्छत् ॥

> *yaddevāpiḥ śaṃtanave purohito hotrāya vṛtaḥ kṛpayannadīdhet |*
> *devaśrutaṃ vṛṣṭivaniṃ rarāṇo bṛhaspatirvācamasmā ayacchat ‖*

(Reference as printed: ಮ. ಸಂ. 10-98-7.)

### Printed p. 4

*Running head: 4 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 3, Sū. 24 (this header is merged into the text of p. 3 in the supplied text; no separate page marker was supplied for p. 4).*

In the explanation of this Ṛk it is said: "Bṛhaspati was the Brahman; he gave speech to him", that is, Bṛhaspati became the priest called Brahman in the sacrifice performed by the king named Śantanu, taught him a mantra in the form of words, and when the sacrificer praised with that mantra, rain came. Besides this, in the Yajurveda and other texts:

> बृहस्पतिर्देवानां पुरोहित आसीच्छंडामर्कावसुराणां ।

> *bṛhaspatirdevānāṃ purohita āsīcchaṃḍāmarkāvasurāṇāṃ |*

(Reference as printed: ತೈ. ಸಂ. 6-4-10-1.)

It is said that Bṛhaspati was the priest of the gods, and that Śaṇḍa and Marka were priests of the asuras.

> ब्रह्म वै देवानां बृहस्पतिर्ब्रह्मणैवैनमभिचरति ।

> *brahma vai devānāṃ bṛhaspatirbrahmaṇaivainamabhicarati |*

(Reference as printed: ತೈ. ಸಂ. 2-2-9-1.)

> तस्मै विशः स्वयमेवा नमंते यस्मिन् ब्रह्मा राजनि पूर्व एति ।

> *tasmai viśaḥ svayamevā namaṃte yasmin brahmā rājani pūrva eti |*

(Reference as printed: ಋ. ಸಂ. 4-50-8.)

> अवस्यवे यो वरिवः कृणोति ब्रह्मणे राजा तमवंति देवाः ॥

> *avasyave yo varivaḥ kṛṇoti brahmaṇe rājā tamavaṃti devāḥ ‖*

(Reference as printed: ಋ ಸಂ. 4-50-9.)

> सोमं राजानमवसेग्निं गीर्भिर्हनामहे ।
> आदित्यान्विष्णुं सूर्यं ब्रह्माणं च बृहस्पतिं ॥

> *somaṃ rājānamavasegniṃ gīrbhirhanāmahe |*
> *ādityānviṣṇuṃ sūryaṃ brahmāṇaṃ ca bṛhaspatiṃ ‖*

(Reference as printed: ಋ. ಸಂ. 10-141-3.)

In these and similar Ṛks Bṛhaspati is described as an officiating priest (ṛtvij).

In the Ṛgveda Bṛhaspati has a prominent place. Besides being praised in eleven hymns, he is also praised in two hymns (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-49; 7-97) together with Indra as a dual deity. The word Bṛhaspati occurs about 120 times in the Ṛgveda. In many places Bṛhaspati and Brahmaṇaspati are praised interchangeably in one and the same mantra. Even so, Bṛhaspati and Brahmaṇaspati are different deities.

Some Western scholars, and some among modern scholars too, have expressed the opinion that both are one deity, because in Ṛ. Saṃ. hymn 2-23 Bṛhaspati and Brahmaṇaspati are praised interchangeably. But the ancient Veda commentators such as Yāska, Śaunaka and Sāyaṇa have said clearly that Bṛhaspati is one and Brahmaṇaspati another. Of the 19 Ṛks of the above-mentioned hymn Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-23, only 6 Ṛks, namely 1, 5, 9, 11, 17, 19, praise Bṛhaspati; the remaining 13 Ṛks are addressed to Brahmaṇaspati. Yāska too has given different derivations for the word Bṛhaspati and the word Brahmaṇaspati. Therefore Bṛhaspati is one and Brahmaṇaspati another.

### Printed p. 5

*Running head: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, A. 2, A. 7, Va. 1 (as printed), p. 5.*

Bṛhaspati has a distinction wholly different from the other deities. While the other deities are all special forms of nature or of a power of nature, Bṛhaspati seems to belong to the field of man's moral and religious life. The Ṛgveda has many examples that this deity, protector of praise, hymn or prayer, exerts his influence on the decision, resolve or power of the minds of the gods. In the 98th hymn of the 10th Maṇḍala of the Ṛgveda there is an account of a history concerning the royal sons of the Kuru line, Devāpi and Śantanu. The writers of the Nirukta describe thus how very important Bṛhaspati's part in it is:

> अत्रेतिहासमाचक्षते। देवापिश्चार्ष्ट्षषेणः शंतनुश्च कौरवा भ्रातरा बभूवतुः।
> स शंतनुः कनीयानभिषेचयांचक्रे । देवापिस्तपः प्रतिपेदे । ततः
> शंतनो राज्ये द्वादश वर्षाणि देवो न ववर्ष । तमूचुब्राह्मणा
> अधर्मस्त्वया चरितो ज्येष्ठं भ्रातरमंतरित्याभिषेचितं तस्मात्ते देवो न
> वर्षतीति । स शंतनुर्देवापिं शिशिक्ष राज्येन । तमुवाच देवापिः
> पुरोहितस्तेऽसानि याजयानि च त्वेति ॥

> *atretihāsamācakṣate| devāpiścārṣṭṣaṣeṇaḥ śaṃtanuśca kauravā bhrātarā babhūvatuḥ|*
> *sa śaṃtanuḥ kanīyānabhiṣecayāṃcakre | devāpistapaḥ pratipede | tataḥ*
> *śaṃtano rājye dvādaśa varṣāṇi devo na vavarṣa | tamūcubrāhmaṇā*
> *adharmastvayā carito jyeṣṭhaṃ bhrātaramaṃtarityābhiṣecitaṃ tasmātte devo na*
> *varṣatīti | sa śaṃtanurdevāpiṃ śiśikṣa rājyena | tamuvāca devāpiḥ*
> *purohitaste 'sāni yājayāni ca tveti ‖*

(Ni. 2-11)

Explanation: In former times, in the Kuru line, the king named Ṛṣṭiṣeṇa had two sons, Devāpi and Śantanu. Of them Devāpi was the elder and Śantanu the younger. After their father Ṛṣṭiṣeṇa died, his elder son Devāpi wished to become king. But his younger brother Śantanu gave him no opportunity, desired to be the ruler of the kingdom himself, drove out his elder brother Devāpi, and began to rule himself. The elder brother Devāpi, distressed, went to the forest and passed his time practising austerity. While matters stood so, no rain fell in his country for 12 years and a great famine came. Then King Śantanu, reflecting what should be done about it, asked the advice of the Brāhmaṇas. The Brāhmaṇas said to the king: according to the Dharmaśāstra the king's eldest son is the one entitled to the kingdom; therefore the rule should have been exercised by your elder brother Devāpi. But you gave him no opportunity, drove him out and seized the kingdom. Because you are thus practising adharma, the rain-god does not rain in your kingdom. King Śantanu repented of the wrong he had done, called his elder brother Devāpi, who was practising austerity in the forest, and handed the kingdom over to him. But Devāpi said that he did not want the kingdom, that he would practise austerity in the forest and take refuge in the path of renunciation, and that Śantanu should perform a sacrifice to remove the drought that had arisen in the kingdom, in which he (Devāpi) would take up the priesthood and help bring about rain. The king agreed, and Devāpi took the part of the ṛtvij called Hotṛ in his sacrifice.

### Printed p. 6

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 3, Sū. 24, p. 6.*

Bṛhaspati became the ṛtvij called Brahmā. In that sacrifice, performed for the sake of causing rain, Devāpi the Hotṛ addressed Bṛhaspati, who was Brahmā: "O Bṛhaspati, you have great influence over the minds of the gods; therefore teach me the mantras that win them over." Being so asked, Bṛhaspati agreed, and taught the Hotṛ Devāpi mantras containing the prayers of the deities needed for causing rain. When he made offerings to the deities with those mantras, rain fell abundantly, it is said. Therefore this hymn is also named the "rain-desiring hymn" (vṛṣṭikāma-sūkta). This matter, with some variations, is also told in Purāṇic works such as the Viṣṇu Purāṇa 4-20-7, the Mahābhārata Ādiparva, and the Bhāgavata Purāṇa 9-22-14 to 17.

In the 108th hymn of the 10th Maṇḍala of the Ṛg-saṃhitā there is another legend. The Asuras called Paṇis had carried off the cows of the gods (the water-bearing clouds) and hidden them in an impregnable fortress. Indra sent Saramā, the divine bitch, to search for them. Saramā came to the Paṇis, engaged them in dispute, and asked them to give back the stolen cows. The Paṇis said: "What is the power of Indra? We are great heroes and our weapons are very sharp; so the gods cannot conquer us, nor can the gods learn the place where the cows are kept. Therefore, if Saramā leaves the side of the gods and joins our company, we will give her whatever wealth she wants." Though they urged her in many ways, Saramā did not agree; she returned from there and told the gods all this. Then Bṛhaspati by his own power found out the place where the cows were hidden. Afterwards the gods subdued them and brought the cows back. Such is the legend.

In many such acts of daring Bṛhaspati's part is very important. In particular, for helping men with the rain they need, Bṛhaspati's help is essential. It is this kind of prayer that is seen especially in the hymns addressed to Bṛhaspati.

Let us examine closely what Bṛhaspati's qualities and achievements are, as described in the Ṛgveda. Bṛhaspati was born of Heaven and Earth, and is nourished by their greatness (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-93-8). But in one place (2-23-17) it is said that Tvaṣṭṛ produced him. He is named the father of the gods (2-26-3); and it is also said (10-72-8) that the gods were produced from him, as sparks of fire fly from the blow of a smith's hammer.

He is called Brahmā (the ṛtvij named Brahmā) (10-141-3). He has a connection with the ṛtvijs (7-10-4; 10-14-3). He is also named Aṅgirasa (4-40-1; 4-73-1; 10-47-6).

### Printed p. 7

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 1 (as printed, the Aṣṭaka numeral being "೮"), Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 7.*

He composes and utters words of praise; he is their lord and impeller (2-23-1, 2; 1-40-5; 10-98-7); by the mantra alone he accomplishes all his aims (2-24-3); he mounts the shining chariot called sacrifice and sets out to conquer the enemies of the gods and of the worshippers (2-23-3). He is the nourisher and protector of the faithful, their guide; he rescues them from calamities (2-23-4). He grants them wealth and the like (2-23-9). He is called the father of all the gods; he is endowed with all divine qualities (3-62-4; 4-50-6). He is of all forms; all things that everyone desires are in him (7-10-4, 7-97-4); he bears a golden spear (7-97-7); together with Indra he is also leader of the armies (10-103-8). He is the increaser of life (10-100-5); plants have sprung from him (10-97-15 and 19); his vehicle is said to be tawny horses (Ni. 2-28).

When Trita, thrown into a well by demons and calling the gods to his help, cried out in distress, Bṛhaspati heard it and released him from that perilous state (1-105-17); and together with Indra Bṛhaspati made the water that had been wrapped in darkness flow again (2-23-18 ; 1-56-5): so it is said.

Bṛhaspati by his own power broke open the stone door of the well full of honey. The gods drank that honey and poured down rain abundantly (2-24-3, 4). When Bṛhaspati split the fortresses of Vala with fire-spitting rays, the herds of cows became visible; he drove the cows out as one breaks an egg; seeing the honey (water) enclosed in rock, he split it with his lion-roar and brought it out (10-68-4 to 9). Bṛhaspati brought the cows out from the rocks; he seized the cows of Vala and made them his own (10-68-5).

Bṛhaspati is endowed with divinity (2-23-2); he is the own of all the gods (3-62-4; 4-50-6); and among the gods he has the most divinity; in the form of a god he gathers into himself all the gods and all things (2-24-11; 8-61-18). Being capable, he keeps the two ends of the earth far apart (4-50-1). The rising of the sun and moon one after the other is also his inimitable deed (10-68-10). In the Taittirīya Saṃhitā (of the Yajurveda) a connection of Bṛhaspati with certain stars is stated: Bṛhaspati is the deity of the Puṣya star (Tai. Saṃ. 4-4-10-1). In the Purāṇas and similar works Bṛhaspati is described as Vācaspati and as the presiding deity of the planet Bṛhaspati (Jupiter).

Some scholars have held that the deities Bṛhaspati and Brahmaṇaspati are transformed forms of Agni. A scholar named M. Langlois, in his work the translation of the Ṛgveda,

### Printed p. 8

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 3, Sū. 24, p. 8.*

Part I, pp. 249, 254, 578, has supported with many examples that these two deities are transformed forms of Agni. A scholar named Wilson, too, in the introduction to the first part of his translation of the Ṛgveda, has expressed the same opinion; and the scholar Max Muller also (says):

Brihaspati and Brahmanaspati are both varieties of Agni, the priest and the purohit of the Gods and men, and as such he is invoked together with the Maruts &c.

That is: Bṛhaspati and Brahmaṇaspati are both transformed forms of Agni, standing in the position of ṛtvij and purohita to gods and men; from this point of view they are invoked together with the Marut deities in many places. We give one or two examples stating this identity of Agni with Bṛhaspati and Brahmaṇaspati.

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> त्वम॑ग्न॒ इंद्रो॑ वृष॒भः स॒ताम॑सि॒ त्वं विष्णु॑रुरुगा॒यो न॑म॒स्यः॑ ।
> त्वं ब्र॒ह्मा र॑यि॒विद्ब्र॑ह्मणस्पते॒ त्वं वि॑धर्तः सचसे॒ पुरं॑ध्या ॥

> *tvamagna iṃdro vṛṣabhaḥ satāmasi tvaṃ viṣṇururugāyo namasyaḥ |*
> *tvaṃ brahmā rayividbrahmaṇaspate tvaṃ vidhartaḥ sacase puraṃdhyā ‖*

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-1-3, printed as "ಪು. ಸಂ.")

"O Agni, you are Indra, the best of the good; you are Viṣṇu, worshipful, of vast body; O Brahmaṇaspati, you are the wealthy ṛtvij called Brahmā": thus Agni is described as the form of various deities.

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> तं शुभ्रमग्निमवसे हवामहे वैश्वानरं मातरिश्वानमुक्थ्यं ।
> बृहस्पतिं मनुषो देवतातये विप्रं श्रोतारमतिथिं रघुष्यदं ॥

> *taṃ śubhramagnimavase havāmahe vaiśvānaraṃ mātariśvānamukthyaṃ |*
> *bṛhaspatiṃ manuṣo devatātaye vipraṃ śrotāramatithiṃ raghuṣyadaṃ ‖*

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-26-2)

"We invite for our protection the bright Agni, friend of the human race; Mātariśvan, worthy to be spoken of in mantras; the wise one, who praises the gods, who is like a guest, who is swift: Bṛhaspati too we invite to come to the sacrificial rites connected with men," and so on.

Sāyaṇācārya, while commenting on Ṛk 5-43-12, says of the word Bṛhaspati:

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> अथवा इयमाग्नेया। बृहतः परिवृढस्य कर्मणः स्वामाति बृहस्पतिरग्निरुच्यते।
> तथा नीलवर्णधूमपृष्ठत्वसदनसादनहिरण्यवर्णत्वादिलिंगैरप्यग्निरेव बृहस्पतिः ॥

> *athavā iyamāgneyā| bṛhataḥ parivṛḍhasya karmaṇaḥ svāmāti bṛhaspatiragnirucyate|*
> *tathā nīlavarṇadhūmapṛṣṭhatvasadanasādanahiraṇyavarṇatvādiliṃgairapyagnireva bṛhaspatiḥ ‖*

That is: "Or this mantra may be said to be addressed to Agni. Because he is lord of the great, vast sacrificial work in hand, Agni is named Bṛhaspati. Likewise, black smoke is his back; the sacrificial altar is his seat; and by marks such as golden colour Bṛhaspati should be called Agni himself."

### Printed p. 9

*Running head: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 1, p. 9.*

Now let us examine the examples in which Agni is spoken of as distinct from Bṛhaspati and Brahmaṇaspati.

> सोषामविंदत्स स्वः १ सो अग्निं सो अर्कॆण वि बबाधे तमांसि ।
> बृहस्पतिर्गो वपुषो वलस्य निर्मज्ञानं न पर्वणो जभार ॥

> *soṣāmaviṃdatsa svaḥ 1 so agniṃ so arkèṇa vi babādhe tamāṃsi |*
> *bṛhaspatirgo vapuṣo valasya nirmajñānaṃ na parvaṇo jabhāra ‖*

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-68-… [the reference is printed twice, the first time broken off: "(ಋ. ಸಂ. ೧೦-೬೮", then "(ಋ, ಸಂ. ೧೦-೬೮-೯)"]; the second is 10-68-9.)

In this ṛk it is said that Bṛhaspati obtained Uṣas, Svar (heaven) and Agni, and by a single mantra drove away the darkness; thus Bṛhaspati and Agni are stated to be different.

> इंद्रं नो अग्ने वसुभिः सजोषा रुद्रं रुद्रेभिरा वहा बृहंतं ।
> आदित्येभिरदितिं विश्वजन्यां बृहस्पतिमृक्वभिर्विश्ववारं ॥

> *iṃdraṃ no agne vasubhiḥ sajoṣā rudraṃ rudrebhirā vahā bṛhaṃtaṃ |*
> *ādityebhiraditiṃ viśvajanyāṃ bṛhaspatimṛkvabhirviśvavāraṃ ‖*

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-10-4, as printed "ಮ. ಸಂ. ೭-೧೦-೪".)

In this ṛk Agni is entreated to bring Indra together with the Vasus, Rudra together with the Rudras (Marut deities), Aditi, mother of all people, together with the Ādityas, and Bṛhaspati together with the Aṅgiras seers who praise him. Besides these, there are many ṛks in the Ṛgveda that indicate that Bṛhaspati or Brahmaṇaspati is one and Agni another: 3-20-5, 4-40-1, 5-51-12, 7-41-1, 7-44-1, 9-5-11, 10-35-11, 10-65-1, 10-130-4, 10-141-3, and others. Therefore the doctrine that Agni is one, Bṛhaspati another and Brahmaṇaspati another is supported in the Ṛgveda. (The text prints "ಋಗ್ವದದಲ್ಲಿ" for "Ṛgveda" in the last sentence, as supplied.)

## Sūkta 24 (Maṇḍala 2)

*The twenty-fourth sūkta.*

**Sāyaṇa's introduction to the sūkta** (Sanskrit, transcribed; see the Anuvāda below for the sense):

> अथ ऋषिच्छंदोदेवताविनियोगपुरःसरं सप्तमो व्याख्यायते ॥ गार्त्स-
> मदस्य द्वितीयमंडलस्य तृतीयेऽनुवाके दश सूक्तानि । तत्रैकं सूक्तं व्याकृतं ।
> सेमामिति षोडशर्चं द्वितीयं सूक्तं । अत्रानुक्रम्यते । सेमां षोडशांत्या
> त्रिष्टप् द्वादशी च सैंद्री चेति । मंडलद्रष्टा गृत्समद ऋषिः । द्वादशीषोडश्यौ
> त्रिष्टुभौ । शिष्टाश्चतुर्दश जगत्यः । ब्राह्मणस्पत्यं हेत्युक्तत्वात् तुह्यादिपरिभाषयेद-
> मादीनि त्रीणि सूक्तानि ब्राह्मणस्पत्यानि । अस्य सूक्तस्य द्वादश्यैंद्री इंद्रदेवत्या ।
> चशब्दाद्ब्राह्मणस्पत्या च ॥ सामान्यविनियोगः पूर्वमेवोक्तः । विशेषतस्तु
> लिंगादवगंतव्यः ॥

*Gist:* *semām* is the second sūkta of Anuvāka 3 of Maṇḍala 2, of sixteen ṛks, seen by Gṛtsamada; the deity is Brahmaṇaspati (Ṛk 12 also Indra); Ṛks 12 and 16 are *triṣṭubh*, the other fourteen *jagatī*.

### Printed p. 10

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 3, Sū. 24, p. 10.*

**Anuvāda (Kannada).** The Anuvāda of the sixth adhyāya of the second aṣṭaka has been given. Now the seventh adhyāya will be explained, with the subjects and discussions. In the third anuvāka of this second maṇḍala, seen by the seer Gṛtsamada, there are ten sūktas in all. Of these the first sūkta was explained earlier, that is, at the end of the sixth adhyāya. Now the second sūkta, *semām*, will be explained. There are sixteen ṛks in this sūkta. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: "*semāṃ ṣoḍaśāntyā triṣṭub dvādaśī ca saindrī ceti*." The seer of this sūkta is Gṛtsamada himself, the seer of the maṇḍala; the twelfth and sixteenth ṛks are in the *triṣṭubh* metre; the remaining fourteen ṛks are in the *jagatī* metre. As stated in the Anukramaṇikā, Brahmaṇaspati is the deity of these three sūktas, that is, of this sūkta and the next two; the twelfth ṛk of this sūkta has Indra as deity, and because the word *ca* is used in the Anukramaṇikā, this ṛk has both Indra and Brahmaṇaspati as deities. The general application (*viniyoga*) of this sūkta has been stated earlier. The special application can be understood from the sense of each mantra.

**Sūkta—24**

Maṇḍala 2 ‖ Anuvāka 3 ‖ Sūkta 24 ‖
Aṣṭaka 2 ‖ Adhyāya 7 ‖ Varga 1 ‖
Number of ṛks in the Sūkta: 16 ·
Ṛṣi: Gṛtsamada · Devatā: Ṛks 1–11 and 13–16 Brahmaṇaspati; Ṛk 12 Brahmaṇaspati and Indra · Chandas: Ṛks 1–11 and 13–15 *jagatī*; Ṛks 12 and 16 *triṣṭubh*.

#### Ṛk 1

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> सेमाम॑विड्डि॒ प्रभृ॑तिं॒ य ईशि॑षे॒ऽया वि॑धेम॒ नव॑या म॒हा गि॒रा ।
> यथा॑ नो मी॒ढ्वान्स्व॑ते॒ सखा॒ तव॒ बृह॑स्पते॒ सीष॑धः॒ सोत नो॒॑
> मतिं ॥ १ ॥

> *semāmaviḍḍi prabhṛtiṃ ya īśiṣe 'yā vidhema navayā mahā girā |*
> *yathā no mīḍhvānsvate sakhā tava bṛhaspate sīṣadhaḥ sota no*
> *matiṃ ‖ 1 ‖*

### Printed p. 11

*Running head: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 1, p. 11.*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

(The supplied text prints a stray line "॥ ದಶರಾತ್ರಿ ॥" at the head of the Pada block; kept as supplied, flagged below.)

> ॥ दशरात्रि ॥
> सः : इमां । अविद्धि । प्रभृतिं । यः । ईशिषे । अया । विधेम । नवया ।
> महा । गिरा ।
> यथा । नः । माध्वान् । स्तवते । सखा । तव । बृहस्पते । सीसधः । सः । उत । नः ।
> मतिं । १ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे ब्रह्मणस्पते यस्त्वमीशिषे सर्वस्य जगत ईश्वरो भवसि स त्वमिमामस्म-
> दीयां प्रभृतिं प्रकर्षेण बृतां स्तुतिमविड्डि । प्राप्नुहि ॥ अवतेः प्राप्तर्थस्य
> लोटैतद्रूपं ॥ वयं च त्वामया अनया ॥ छांदसो वर्णलोपः ॥ नवया
> नूतनया प्रत्यग्रया महा महत्या गिरा वेदरूपया स्तुतिलक्षणया वाचा विधेम !
> परिचरेम ॥ विधतिः परिचरणकर्मा ॥ उत आपि च नोऽस्माकं मध्यॆ मीढ्वान् सेक्ता
> स्तुतेर्वषिता । कर्तॆति यावत् । तव सखा मित्रभूतः सन् यथा येन प्रकारेण
> यत्कामनया स्तवते । त्वां स्तौति । हे बृहस्पते बृहतो ब्रह्मणो मंत्रस्य
> स्वामिन् स त्वं नोऽस्माकं मतिं तदभिमतं फलं सीषधः । साधय ॥ छांदसे लुजि
> चज्येतद्रूपं

*Gist:* O Brahmaṇaspati, lord of all the world, accept our new, great praise; as the praiser, your friend, praises you, so fulfil our desire.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

(O Brahmaṇaspati) यः (*yaḥ*)—you who; ईशिषे (*īśiṣe*)—are lord of the whole world; सः (*saḥ*)—such as you; इमां (*imāṃ*)—this, recited by us; प्रभृतिं (*prabhṛtiṃ*)—and excellently composed, praise; अविद्धि (*aviddhi*)—accept; नवया (*navayā*)—new; महा (*mahā*)—and great; अया गिरा (*ayā girā*)—with this word in the form of praise; विधेम (*vidhema*)—we will serve you; उत (*uta*)—and; नः (*naḥ*)—among us; मिढ्वान् (*miḍhvān*) (as printed in the Pratipadārtha; the Pada has ಮಾಧ್ವಾನ್)—the praiser, showerer of praise; तव सखा (*tava sakhā*)—being your friend; यथा (*yathā*)—in what manner; स्तवते (*stavate*)—he praises you (accordingly); बृहस्पते (*bṛhaspate*)—O lord of mantras; सः (*saḥ*)—you; नः (*naḥ*)—our; मतिं (*matiṃ*)—desired object; सीषधः (*sīṣadhaḥ*)—fulfil.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Bṛhaspati, you who are lord of the whole world, accept the praise recited by us and excellently composed. With this new and great word of praise we serve you. O lord (printed "ಒಡನೆಯೇ", sic) of mantras, as the praiser among us praises you, accordingly fulfil our desired object.

### Printed p. 12

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 3, Sū. 24, p. 12.*

**Printed English translation**

Inasmuch, Brihaspati, as you rule (over all), accept this our propitiation : we worship you with this new and solemn hymn : since this, your friend, our benefactor, celebrates you, fulfil our desires.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

सः इमां (*saḥ imāṃ*)—Here the two words सः इमां (*saḥ imāṃ*) have joined, and instead of स इमां (*sa imāṃ*) the form is सेमां (*semāṃ*); that is, the visarga after the *sa* has been dropped. Likewise, in this same mantra the words सः उत (*saḥ uta*) have joined to become सोत (*sota*). Such examples are many in the Ṛgveda.

सेदग्ने अस्तु सुभगः सदानुर्यस्त्वा नित्येन हविषा य उक्षैः । (*sedagne astu subhagaḥ sadānuryastvā nityena haviṣā ya ukṣaiḥ |*) (Pu. Saṃ. 4-4-7; reference as supplied; no gloss is given in the source)

सेद्यभवो यमवध यूयमिंद्रश्च मर्त्यं । (*sedyabhavo yamavadha yūyamiṃdraśca martyaṃ |*) (Pu. Saṃ. 4-37-6; reference as supplied; no gloss is given in the source)

In these verses (printed "ಮಕ್ಕಳಲ್ಲಿ", "in these children", sic as supplied) the words सः इत् (*saḥ it*) have joined to become सेत् (*set*).

सैनानीकेन गुविदत्रो अस्मे यष्टा देवा आयुजिष्ठः स्वस्ति । (*sainānīkena guvidatro asme yaṣṭā devā āyujiṣṭhaḥ svasti |*) (Ma. Saṃ. 2-9-6; reference as supplied; no gloss is given in the source)

In this ṛk the words सः एना (*saḥ enā*) have joined to become सैना (*sainā*). Since such usages do not accord with grammar, it is customary to call them Vedic (*chāndasa*) usages.

प्रभृतिं (*prabhṛtiṃ*)—प्रकर्षण बृतां स्तुतिं (*prakarṣaṇa bṛtāṃ stutiṃ*): the praise that we bear (hold), that is, make, in a very excellent manner. The word is formed from the root भृञ् भरणे (*bhṛñ bharaṇe*) preceded by the prefix प्र (*pra*). In the Ṛgveda this root is commonly used in the sense of praising in a special manner. Therefore the commentator explains the word *prabhṛti* as praise made in a special manner.

ईशिषे (*īśiṣe*)—सर्वस्य जगत ईश्वरो भवसि (*sarvasya jagata īśvaro bhavasi*): "you are the lord of the whole world", is the commentator's explanation. That is, all the gods, human beings, animals and the plant kingdom are under his control; therefore it is natural to say that you are the master of all. This idea is stated in such ṛks as Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-24-11; 4-50-1; 10-68-10; 10-97-15

### Printed p. 13

*Running head: [8. 2. 8. 7. 1.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 13 (as supplied).*

and so on. Since the word *bṛhaspati* means lord of the mantras, Bṛhaspati accomplishes all his works through mantras alone: splitting the clouds, sending rain, driving away darkness, destroying enemies, and so on, he does by mantras only. Therefore the Ṛgveda has the very sentence बृहस्पतिर्हंत्यमित्रमर्कॆः (*bṛhaspatirhaṃtyamitramarkèḥ*) (Bṛhaspati destroys the enemy by the power of mantra alone).

अया (*ayā*)—अनया (*anayā*). The *n* of the word अनया (*anayā*) has been dropped, giving आया (*āyā*) [sic as supplied]. This is a Vedic usage.

अया नवया महा गिरा विधेम (*ayā navayā mahā girā vidhema*)—अनया नूतनया महत्या वेदरूपया स्तुतिलक्षणया वाचा परिचरेम (*anayā nūtanayā mahatyā vedarūpayā stutilakṣaṇayā vācā paricarema*): "we serve with this new, great word in the form of the Veda, having the nature of praise". The sense is: O Bṛhaspati, the praise we are now making is newly composed by us, that is, not seen (revealed) by earlier ṛṣis; and it is such as extols your qualities, which are महत्तूरितवाद (*mahattūritavāda*) [?] (the supplied word is unclear). With such praise-sentences in the form of the Veda we praise you.

मीढ्वान् (*mīḍhvān*)—सेक्ता स्तुतेर्वषिता (*sektā stutervaṣitā*). Although the word *mīḍhvān* is used of Indra and other gods in the sense कामानां वर्षिता (*kāmānāṃ varṣitā*), "one who showers desired objects", here it is used as an epithet of the sacrificer who praises. "One who showers praise" means the sacrificer who praises Bṛhaspati in a special manner.

तव सखा (*tava sakhā*)—मित्रभूतः (*mitrabhūtaḥ*): one who does good like a friend. Bṛhaspati is one who does favour, like a friend, to the sacrificer who praises him. That doing good or favour is the chief quality of a friend is stated by a Vedic sentence itself: न स सखा यो न ददाति सख्ये (*na sa sakhā yo na dadāti sakhye*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-117-4): the man who gives nothing to his friend, that is, who does not help in time of trouble, is no friend at all. Here, because Bṛhaspati gives desired objects to the sacrificer who praises him, he is praised with the word *sakhā* (friend):

यो रेवान् यो अमीवहा वसुवित्सुष्टिवर्धनः । (*yo revān yo amīvahā vasuvitsuṣṭivardhanaḥ |*)
स नः सिषक्तु यस्तुरः ॥ (*sa naḥ siṣaktu yasturaḥ ‖*)
(Ma. 1-18-2)

That is, Bṛhaspati is the giver of wealth, nourishment and riches to him who praises him, and the remover of disease and affliction. Such a Bṛhaspati comes quickly to give desired objects to the sacrificer who praises him.

### Printed p. 14

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 3, Sū. 24, p. 14.*

एवा पित्रे विश्वदेवाय वृष्टे यज्ञैर्विधेम नमसा हविर्भिः । (*evā pitre viśvadevāya vṛṣṭe yajñairvidhema namasā havirbhiḥ |*)
बृहस्पते सुप्रजा वीरवंतो वयं स्याम पशयो रयाणां ॥ (*bṛhaspate suprajā vīravaṃto vayaṃ syāma paśayo rayāṇāṃ ‖*)
(Pu. Saṃ. 4-50-7)

That is: Bṛhaspati, who is like a father because he protects us, who is to be praised by all, and who grants desired objects, we serve with sacrificial rites, with offerings of oblations and with praise-sentences. O Bṛhaspati, grant that we may obtain heroic sons, grandsons and so on, and become lords of wealth and other goods. Since in many ṛks of this kind it is said that sacrificers obtain wealth and the rest by praising Bṛhaspati, he is described and praised here as a friend who does favours.

#### Ṛk 2

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> यो नन्त्वा॒न्यन॑म॒न्नो ज॑सो॒ताद॑र्दर्म॒न्युना॒ शंब॑राणि॒ वि ।
> प्राच्या॑वय॒दच्यु॑ता॒ ब्रह्म॑ण॒स्पति॒रा चावि॑श॒द्वसु॑मंतं॒ वि पर्व॑तं ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> यॆः । नन्न्तानि । अनमत् । नि । ओजसा । उत । आदर्दः । मन्युना । शंबराणि । वि ।
> प्र । अच्यवयत् । अच्युता । ब्रह्मणः । पतिः । आ । च । अविशत् । वसुमंतं । वि ।
> पर्वतं ॥ २ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> यो ब्रह्मणस्पतिर्नंत्यानि नमनीयानि राक्षसादीन्योजसा बलेन न्यनमत् ।
> नितरामनमयत् प्रह्वीभूतान्यकरोत् । उत अपि च स ब्रह्मणस्पतिर्मन्युना मनुना
> क्रोधेन वा ॥ मन्युर्मन्यतेर्दिप्तिकर्मणः क्रोधकर्मणो वधकर्मणो वा ।
> नि. १०-२९ । इति यास्कः ॥ शंबराणि ॥ मेघनामैतत् ॥ मेघान् व्यदर्दः ।
> वर्षणार्थं विदारितवान् ॥ द्वै विदारण इत्यस्माद्यजलुगंताल्लज्येतद्रूपं ॥ तदनंतर-
> मच्युता अच्युतानि नैश्चल्येन मेघॆऽवस्थितान्युदकानि प्राच्यावयत् प्रकर्षेणागम-

> *yo brahmaṇaspatirnaṃtyāni namanīyāni rākṣasādīnyojasā balena nyanamat |*
> *nitarāmanamayat prahvībhūtānyakarot | uta api ca sa brahmaṇaspatirmanyunā manunā*
> *krodhena vā ‖ manyurmanyaterdiptikarmaṇaḥ krodhakarmaṇo vadhakarmaṇo vā |*
> *ni. 10-29 | iti yāskaḥ ‖ śaṃbarāṇi ‖ meghanāmaitat ‖ meghān vyadardaḥ |*
> *varṣaṇārthaṃ vidāritavān ‖ dvai vidāraṇa ityasmādyajalugaṃtāllajyetadrūpaṃ ‖ tadanaṃtara-*
> *macyutā acyutāni naiścalyena meghè 'vasthitānyudakāni prācyāvayat prakarṣeṇāgama-*

### Printed p. 15

*Running head: [A. 2 A. 6 V. 1.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 15 (as supplied).*

**Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya** (continued)

> यत् । मेघान्निरगमयदित्यर्थः । तथा वसुमंतं गोरूपधनवंतं पर्वतं
> वलनाम्नासुरेण बिलद्वारे विधानार्थं निहितं शिलोच्चयं आ च अभिमुख्येन चेत् ॥
> चशब्दश्चेदर्थः । समुच्चयार्थत्वे तु चवायोगे प्रथमेति पूर्वस्याच्यावय-
> दित्यस्याद्युदात्तत्वं स्यात् अस्य च सर्वानुदात्तत्वं । चेदर्थे तु निपातैर्यद्यदिहंतेति
> निघातप्रतिषेधः सिध्यति ॥ व्यविशत् । विशेषेण प्रविशति । तदानीं तद्दोरूपं
> धनं प्राप्नोदित्यर्थः ॥

> *yat | meghānniragamayadityarthaḥ | tathā vasumaṃtaṃ gorūpadhanavaṃtaṃ parvataṃ*
> *valanāmnāsureṇa biladvāre vidhānārthaṃ nihitaṃ śiloccayaṃ ā ca abhimukhyena cet ‖*
> *caśabdaścedarthaḥ | samuccayārthatve tu cavāyoge prathameti pūrvasyācyāvaya-*
> *dityasyādyudāttatvaṃ syāt asya ca sarvānudāttatvaṃ | cedarthe tu nipātairyadyadihaṃteti*
> *nighātapratiṣedhaḥ sidhyati ‖ vyaviśat | viśeṣeṇa praviśati | tadānīṃ taddorūpaṃ*
> *dhanaṃ prāpnodityarthaḥ ‖*

Gist: the Ṛk's last part says that he came near the mountain holding the wealth in the form of cattle, which Vala had placed at the cave-mouth, and entered it, obtaining that wealth.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

*yaḥ brahmaṇaspatiḥ*—that same Brahmaṇaspati who; *ojasā*—by strength; *nantāni*—the asuras who deserved punishment; *ni anamat*—punished and made humble; *uta*—and; *manyunā*—in wrath; *śambarāṇi*—the clouds; *vi adardaḥ*—(for the sake of rain) split; *acyutā*—the waters that were without motion (in the cloud); *prācyāvayat*—made to flow; *vasumantaṃ*—holding wealth in the form of cattle; *parvataṃ*—the mountain; *ā ca*—and approached (facing it); *vi aviśat*—entered fully.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The renowned Brahmaṇaspati punished by his strength the asuras who deserved punishment and made them humble. And in wrath he split the clouds and so helped the falling of rain. He made the waters, which stood motionless in the cloud, flow. He approached the mountain that holds wealth in the form of cattle and entered it.

**Printed English**

(You are) that Brahmanaspati who, by his might, has humbled those who deserved humiliation; who in his wrath rent the clouds asunder ; who sent down the undescending (waters) and made way into the mountain (cave), opulent (in cattle).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

*yaḥ brahmaṇaspatiḥ nantvāni ojasā ni anamat* (as supplied)—ब्रह्मणस्पतिः नमनीयानि राक्षसादीनि बलेन नितरामनमयत् (*brahmaṇaspatiḥ namanīyāni rākṣasādīni balena nitarāmanamayat*) (Brahmaṇaspati made the demons and others, who were to be bent, bow down utterly by strength). That Brahmaṇaspati made the wicked or the asuras, who had to be restrained (because they were puffed up and intoxicated), walk bowed down by his own power; that is, he suppressed their ego and pride and made them subject to the rules of Bṛhaspati [sic as supplied: *bṛhaspati*]. Here the word *nantvāni* is formed from the root *ṇamu prahvatve* [sic as supplied] and is used in the sense "to be bent down", that is, "(people) to be punished". Since those who come under punishment are wicked doers of evil deeds, the commentator has given the word *nantvāni* the meaning "demons, asuras".

### Printed p. 16

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 3, Sū. 24, p. 16.*

उत मन्युना शंबराणि वि अदर्दः (*uta manyunā śaṃbarāṇi vi adardaḥ*)—स ब्रह्मणस्पतिः मन्युना क्रोधेन वा मेघान् वर्षणार्थं विदारितवान् (*sa brahmaṇaspatiḥ manyunā krodhena vā meghān varṣaṇārthaṃ vidāritavān*) (that Brahmaṇaspati split the clouds, by *manyu* or by anger, for the sake of rain). And that Brahmaṇaspati split the clouds for rain by his power of mantra or by anger. The idea is that he angrily split the clouds, which had held the waters back in themselves without raining, and made them rain.

*manyunā*—For the word *manyu* Yāska gives the derivation मन्युर्मन्यतेर्दिप्तिकर्मणः क्रोधकर्मणो वधकर्मणो वा मन्यंत्यस्मादिषवः (*manyurmanyaterdiptikarmaṇaḥ krodhakarmaṇo vadhakarmaṇo vā manyaṃtyasmādiṣavaḥ*) (Ni. 10-29): that is, he says that the word *manyu*, formed from the root *manyati*, has the sense of illumining, of anger, or of slaying. He also says that *manyu* may be the name of a deity of that name, and in support of this cites the Ṛk—

⟦ತ್ವಯಾ ಮನ್ಯೋ ಸರಥಮಾರುಜಂತೋ ಹರ್ಷಮಾಣಾಸೋ ಧೃಷಿತಾ ಮರುತ್ವಃ ।
ತಿಗ್ಮೇಷವ ಆಯುಧಾ ಸಂಶಿಶಾನಾ ಅಭಿ ಪ್ರ ಯಂತು ನರೋ ಅಗ್ನಿರೂಪಾಃ ॥⟧
(Pu. Saṃ. 10-84-1)

The deity Manyu is praised in the 83rd and 84th sūktas of the 10th Maṇḍala.

*śambarāṇi*—The word *śambara* is read among the names of the cloud (Ni. 2-21). For the word *śambara*, formed from the root *śamu upaśame*, the commentary says: शमयति नाशयति आसुरानिति शंबो वज्रः । शंबोऽस्य प्रहर्तृत्वेनास्ति । रो मत्त्वर्थीयः । प्रहरति हि वज्रः इंद्रप्रेरितो मेघान् (*śamayati nāśayati āsurāniti śaṃbo vajraḥ | śaṃbo 'sya prahartṛtvenāsti | ro mattvarthīyaḥ | praharati hi vajraḥ iṃdraprerito meghān*) (*śamba* is the thunderbolt, because it pacifies, that is destroys, the asuras; it is his as striker; the suffix *ra* is in the sense of possession; for the thunderbolt, impelled by Indra, strikes the clouds). Because it destroys asuras and others, Indra's thunderbolt is called *śamba*; since that thunderbolt, impelled by Indra, splits the clouds for rain, the cloud is called *śambara*. Or: यद्वा संपूर्वात् वृणोतेः इत्यपि संबरः सन् वर्णव्यतयेन शंबरः । संवियते मेघेनाकाशं इति शंबरः (*yadvā saṃpūrvāt vṛṇoteḥ ityapi saṃbaraḥ san varṇavyatayena śaṃbaraḥ | saṃviyate meghenākāśaṃ iti śaṃbaraḥ*) (or, from *vṛṇoti* with the prefix *saṃ*, it is *sambara*, and by interchange of letter *śambara*; the sky is covered by the cloud, hence *śambara*). That is, the word *sambara* may be formed from the root *vṛṇoti*, "to cover", with the prefix *saṃ*; by a change of letter *sambaraḥ* becomes *śambaraḥ*; since the sky is covered by the cloud, the cloud is called *śambara*. Or *śambara* may also mean water; the word *śambara* is also read among the 101 [as supplied] names of water (Ni. 2-24). Since such water is in this cloud, the cloud is called *śambara*.

### Printed p. 17

*Running head: [A. 2. A. 7. V. 1.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 17 (as supplied).*

अच्युता प्र अच्यवयत् (*acyutā pra acyavayat*)—तदनंतरमच्युता अच्युतानि नैश्चल्येन मेघेवस्थितान्युदकानि प्रकर्षणागमयत् । मेघान्निरगमयदित्यर्थः (*tadanaṃtaramacyutā acyutāni naiścalyena meghevasthitānyudakāni prakarṣaṇāgamayat | meghānniragamayadityarthaḥ*) (afterwards he made the unmoved waters, standing motionless in the cloud, come forth; the sense is that he made them come out of the clouds). He made all the waters, which were gathered motionless in the clouds, move; that is, he made them fall on the earth in the form of rain.

वसुमंतं पर्वतं आ च वि अविशत् (*vasumaṃtaṃ parvataṃ ā ca vi aviśat*)—गोरूपधनवंतं पर्वतं वलनाम्मासुरेण द्वारे सिधानार्थं निहितं शिलोच्चयं अभिमुख्येन विशेषेण प्रविशति । तदानीं तद्दोरूपं धनं प्राप्नोदित्यर्थः (*gorūpadhanavaṃtaṃ parvataṃ valanāmmāsureṇa dvāre sidhānārthaṃ nihitaṃ śiloccayaṃ abhimukhyena viśeṣeṇa praviśati | tadānīṃ taddorūpaṃ dhanaṃ prāpnodityarthaḥ*) (as supplied; the readings *vala-nāmmāsureṇa* and *sidhānārtham* differ from the bhāṣya's own printing on p. 15). The asura named Vala stole the cows of the gods, hid them in a cave of the mountain, and, so that they should not escape, closed the mouth of that cave with a great rock. Brahmaṇaspati, by his power of mantra, found the mountain where the cows were hidden, removed the rock at the cave-mouth, went to the cows and freed them from bondage: this is the main idea. Moreover, since the word *parvata* also means cloud (Ni. 2-21), the words *vasumantaṃ parvataṃ* may also be given the meaning "the cloud holding wealth in the form of water". In that case the sentence may be understood: Brahmaṇaspati entered, by his power of mantra, the cloud full of waters and impelled it to pour down rain.

### Printed p. 18

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 3, Sū. 24, p. 13 (as supplied; the page is printed 18).*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> देवानामिंद्रादीनां मध्यॆ देवतमायातिशयेन दानादिगुणयुक्ताय ॥
> षष्ठ्यर्थे चतुर्थी ॥ ईदृशस्य ब्रह्मणस्पतेस्तत्कर्त्वं कर्तव्यं कर्म । किं पुनस्तदिति
> चेदुच्यते । दृळ्ता दृढानि पर्वतादीन्यश्रथन् ॥ श्रंथ विमोचनप्रतिहर्षणयोः ॥
> अस्य बलेन श्रथितानि विश्लिष्टान्यभवन् । तथा वीळिता संस्तंभितानि दृढीकृतानि वृक्षादीनि ॥
> वीळयतिश्च व्रीळयतिश्च संस्तंभकर्माणाविति यास्कः । नि. ५-१६ ॥ अव्रदंत ।
> मृदून्यभवन् ॥ प्रदतिर्मृदूर्भावकर्मा । अपि च स ब्रह्मणस्पतिर्वलेनापहृता गा
> उदाजत् ॥ अज गतिक्षेपणयोः ॥ देवान्प्रत्युदगमयत् । तथा ब्रह्मणात्मीयेन
> मंत्रेण वलं सर्वस्यावारकमसुरमभिनत् । मंत्रस्य सामर्थ्येनाभैत्सीत् ।
> तदनंतरं वलेनोत्पादितं तमो ध्वांतमगूहत् ॥ गुह संवरणे ॥ गूढमदृश्यम-
> करोत् । तदनंतरं स्वरादित्यं व्यचक्षयत् । तमसो निराकरणेन तत्समदर्शयत् ॥
> चष्टिः पश्यतिकर्मा ॥

> *devānāmiṃdrādīnāṃ madhyè devatamāyātiśayena dānādiguṇayuktāya ‖*
> *ṣaṣṭhyarthe caturthī ‖ īdṛśasya brahmaṇaspatestatkartvaṃ kartavyaṃ karma | kiṃ punastaditi*
> *ceducyate | dṛḷtā dṛḍhāni parvatādīnyaśrathan ‖ śraṃtha vimocanapratiharṣaṇayoḥ ‖*
> *asya balena śrathitāni viśliṣṭānyabhavan | tathā vīḷitā saṃstaṃbhitāni dṛḍhīkṛtāni vṛkṣādīni ‖*
> *vīḷayatiśca vrīḷayatiśca saṃstaṃbhakarmāṇāviti yāskaḥ | ni. 5-16 ‖ avradaṃta |*
> *mṛdūnyabhavan ‖ pradatirmṛdūrbhāvakarmā | api ca sa brahmaṇaspatirvalenāpahṛtā gā*
> *udājat ‖ aja gatikṣepaṇayoḥ ‖ devānpratyudagamayat | tathā brahmaṇātmīyena*
> *maṃtreṇa valaṃ sarvasyāvārakamasuramabhinat | maṃtrasya sāmarthyenābhaitsīt |*
> *tadanaṃtaraṃ valenotpāditaṃ tamo dhvāṃtamagūhat ‖ guha saṃvaraṇe ‖ gūḍhamadṛśyama-*
> *karot | tadanaṃtaraṃ svarādityaṃ vyacakṣayat | tamaso nirākaraṇena tatsamadarśayat ‖*
> *caṣṭiḥ paśyatikarmā ‖*

Gist: the Ṛk's deed is ascribed to Brahmaṇaspati: he loosened the firm mountains and made the rooted trees pliant, drove out the cows seized by Vala, split Vala by the mantra, hid the darkness and made the sun shine. Yāska is cited (Ni. 5-16) for *vīḷayati* and *vradati*.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

देवानां (*devānāṃ*)—among the gods, Indra and others; देवतमाय (*devatamāya*)—of Brahmaṇaspati, the most excellent god; कर्तृं (*kartṛṃ*) (as supplied; the Saṃhitā has कर्त्वं (*kartvaṃ*))—the extraordinary deed, which is famed as told below (namely); दृळ्हा (*dṛḷhā*) (the bhāṣya and Special Topics print दृळ्ता (*dṛḷtā*))—the firm mountains and the like; अश्रथन् (*aśrathan*)—he made loose; नीळिका (*nīḷikā*) (as supplied; the Saṃhitā and bhāṣya have वीळिता (*vīḷitā*))—the firmly rooted trees and the like; अव्रदंत (*avradaṃta*)—he made them soft; गाः (*gāḥ*)—the cows (taken away by Vala); उदाजत् (*udājat*)—he brought them out of the cave and made them go towards the gods; ब्रह्मणा (*brahmaṇā*)—by the power of his own mantra; वलं (*valaṃ*)—the asura named Vala; अभिनत् (*abhinat*)—he frightened and drove away (or destroyed); तमः (*tamaḥ*)—the darkness; अगूहत् (*agūhat*)—he scattered and made vanish; स्वः (*svaḥ*)—the sun; व्यचक्षयत् (*vyacakṣayat*)—he made shine.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

Among the gods, Indra and others, the extraordinary deed of Brahmaṇaspati, the most excellent god, is famed as told below. Namely: he loosened the firm mountains and the like. He made the firmly rooted trees and the like soft. He made the cows seized by Vala go towards the gods. By his mantra he frightened and drove away the asura Vala, and destroyed him. He scattered the darkness and made it vanish. He made the sun shine.

### Printed p. 19

*Running head: [A. 2 A. 7. V. 1.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 19 (as supplied).*

**Printed English**

That was exploit, (performed) for the most divine of the gods, by which the firm (shut gates) were thrown open, the strong (barriers) were relaxed, (by him) who set the cows at liberty : who by the (force of the) sacred prayer, destroyed Vala : who dispersed the darkness and displayed the light.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this Ṛk the praise of the deeds done by Brahmaṇaspati is described. It also says that Brahmaṇaspati did all these feats by the power of his mantra alone.

देवानां देवतमाय-इंद्रादीनां मध्यॆ अतिशयेन दानादिगुणयुक्ताय! (*devānāṃ devatamāya-iṃdrādīnāṃ madhyè atiśayena dānādiguṇayuktāya!*) Among all the gods Brahmaṇaspati is the most excellent. That is, among Indra and the other gods this Brahmaṇaspati is the one endowed in the highest degree with qualities such as generosity; the meaning is that he grants the desired objects to those who praise him more abundantly than Indra and the other gods. Or, if the word *devatama* is taken to mean "most excellent of the gods", the words देवानां देवतमाय (*devānāṃ devatamāya*) suggest the sense "one who is like the most excellent even among the gods", "god of gods"; just as one who is the foremost among kings is called "king of kings", so Brahmaṇaspati is described here by the words देवानां देवतमाय (*devānāṃ devatamāya*). Here the dative देवतमाय (*devatamāya*) is used in the sense of the genitive; such is the commentator's explanation.

कर्त्वं—कर्तव्यं कर्म । (*kartvaṃ—kartavyaṃ karma |*) The deed that is done; that is, the feat that Brahmaṇaspati accomplished by the power of his mantra.

दृळ्ता अश्रथन्, वीळिता अव्रदंतॆ (*dṛḷtā aśrathan, vīḷitā avradaṃtè*) (as supplied)—Some of Brahmaṇaspati's feats are described in this Ṛk. Among them are making the firm mountains and the like loose, and making soft the trees and the like that are well rooted and have grown firm. Here the verb अव्रदंतॆ (*avradaṃtè*) (as supplied) is formed from the root *vrand* [as supplied: व्रंद् (*vraṃd*)] and is used in the sense "to be soft". For the word व्रंदी (*vraṃdī*) formed from this root, Yāska gives the explanation व्रंदी व्रंदतेर्मृदुभावकर्मणः (*vraṃdī vraṃdatermṛdubhāvakarmaṇaḥ*) (Ni. 5-16) [sic as supplied: the hyphen of the line-break is printed व्रंदते- (*vraṃdate-*)]. In this connection the Ṛk—

⟦ನಿ ಯದ್ವೃಣಕ್ಷಿ ಶ್ವಸನಸ್ಯ ಮೂರ್ಧನಿ ಶುಷ್ಣಸ್ಯ ಚಿದ್ವಂದಿನೋ ರೋರುವದ್ವನಾ ।
ಪ್ರಾಚೀನೇನ ಮನಸಾ ಬರ್ಹಣಾವತಾ ಯದದ್ಯಾ ಚಿತ್ಕೃಣವಃ ಕಸ್ತ್ಯಾ ಪರಿ ॥⟧
(Mu. Saṃ. 1-54-5)

### Printed p. 20

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 3, Sū. 24, p. 20.*

having been cited (by Yāska), he explains it thus: निवृणक्षि यच्छसनस्य मूर्धनि शब्दकारिणः शुष्ण स्यादित्यस्य च शोषयितू रोरूयमाणो वनानीति वा वधेनेति वा । अव्रदंत वीळिता इत्यपि निगमो भवति । वीळयतिश्च व्रीळयतिश्च संस्तंभकर्माणौ पूर्वेण संप्रयुज्येते । (*nivṛṇakṣi yacchasanasya mūrdhani śabdakāriṇaḥ śuṣṇa syādityasya ca śoṣayitū rorūyamāṇo vanānīti vā vadheneti vā | avradaṃta vīḷitā ityapi nigamo bhavati | vīḷayatiśca vrīḷayatiśca saṃstaṃbhakarmāṇau pūrveṇa saṃprayujyete |*) Having given this explanation, he also uses the present Ṛk, अव्रदंत वीळिता (*avradaṃta vīḷitā*), as an example. Of the wind that moves in the atmosphere and the sun, who is denoted by the word *vrandin* because he makes fruits and the like soft, Indra is greater than both, because he pours down water. Yāska himself says that applying the epithet *vrandin* to the sun, because he makes fruits and the like tender, is not so clear; and citing this very Ṛk he gives, for वीळिता (*vīḷitā*) here, the sense that he softened or loosened the trees with firm roots, अव्रदंत (*avradaṃta*). And: वीळयतिश्च व्रीळयतिश्च संस्तंभकर्माणौ (*vīḷayatiśca vrīḷayatiśca saṃstaṃbhakarmāṇau*) means that वीळिता (*vīḷitā*), formed from the root वीळयति (*vīḷayati*) or व्रीळयति (*vrīḷayati*), of the sense "to make stand firm like a pillar", is used together with the word अव्रंदत (*avraṃdata*) (as supplied; elsewhere अव्रदंत (*avradaṃta*)); so the meaning, making what is firm soft, is clearly explained.

गाः उत् आजत्, ब्रह्मणा वलं अभिनत् (*gāḥ ut ājat, brahmaṇā valaṃ abhinat*)—Brahmaṇaspati, by the power of his mantra, split the asura Vala, and drove out from the mountain cave the cows of the gods that he had hidden in bondage. This is the main idea.

तमः अगूहत्, स्वः वि आचक्षयत् (*tamaḥ agūhat, svaḥ vi ācakṣayat*)—He drove away the darkness that Vala had spread, and made the sun visible to all and shining. The asura Vala produced darkness and covered the sun so that he should not be seen by all. Bṛhaspati destroyed that darkness and made the sun shine; such is the idea.

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> *tameva viśve papire svardṛśo bahu sākaṃ sisicurutsamudriṇaṃ ‖ 4 ‖*

*Running head: [A. 2 A. 7. V. 1.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 21 (as supplied).*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अश्वआस्यं । अवतं । ब्रह्मणः । पतिः । मधुधारं । अभि ! यं । ओजसा । अतृणत् ।
> तं । एव । विश्वे । पपिरे । स्वःदृशः । बहु । साकं । सिसिचुः । उत्सं । उद्रिणं ॥ ४ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> ब्रह्मणस्पतिर्ब्रह्मणो मंत्रस्य पतिः पालयिता देवोSश्मास्यमश्नवद्दृढत-
> रास्यं । यास्कस्त्वाह । अशनवंतमास्यंदनवंतं । नि. १०-१३ । इति । व्यापनवंतमा-
> सेचनवंतमिति तस्यार्थः । मधुधारं मधुर्मादयित्रि उदकधारा यस्य
> तादृशमवतमवस्तात्ततं विस्तृतं यं मेघमोजसा बलेनाभ्यतृणत् ॥ उत्कृदिर्
> हिंसानादरयोः ॥ अहिंसीत् । वर्षणार्थमवधीदित्यर्थः । तमेव मेघं विश्वे
> सर्वॆ स्वर्दृशः । स्वरादित्यः । तस्य संबंधिनो द्रष्टारो रश्ययः पपिरे । घर्मकाले
> पिबंति । मेघन वृष्टस्योदकस्य पानात्तमेव पिबंतीत्युच्यते । ते च रश्मयो
> वर्षासूत्समुत्सेचनवंतमुद्रिणमुदकवंतं मेघं साकं सह युगपदेव बहु
> सिसिचुः । घर्मकाले यत्पीतं ततोSपि बहुतरं सहस्रगुणितं सिंचंतीत्यर्थः ॥

> *brahmaṇaspatirbrahmaṇo maṃtrasya patiḥ pālayitā devoSśmāsyamaśnavaddṛḍhata-*
> *rāsyaṃ | yāskastvāha | aśanavaṃtamāsyaṃdanavaṃtaṃ | ni. 10-13 | iti | vyāpanavaṃtamā-*
> *secanavaṃtamiti tasyārthaḥ | madhudhāraṃ madhurmādayitri udakadhārā yasya*
> *tādṛśamavatamavastāttataṃ vistṛtaṃ yaṃ meghamojasā balenābhyatṛṇat ‖ utkṛdir*
> *hiṃsānādarayoḥ ‖ ahiṃsīt | varṣaṇārthamavadhīdityarthaḥ | tameva meghaṃ viśve*
> *sarvè svardṛśaḥ | svarādityaḥ | tasya saṃbaṃdhino draṣṭāro raśyayaḥ papire | gharmakāle*
> *pibaṃti | meghana vṛṣṭasyodakasya pānāttameva pibaṃtītyucyate | te ca raśmayo*
> *varṣāsūtsamutsecanavaṃtamudriṇamudakavaṃtaṃ meghaṃ sākaṃ saha yugapadeva bahu*
> *sisicuḥ | gharmakāle yatpītaṃ tatoSpi bahutaraṃ sahasraguṇitaṃ siṃcaṃtītyarthaḥ ‖*

Gist: Brahmaṇaspati, the lord of the mantra, split with his strength the hard, water-streaming cloud that hangs downward; the sun's rays drink its water in the hot season and in the rains pour it down again in abundance.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

ब्रह्मणस्पतिः (*brahmaṇaspatiḥ*)—Brahmaṇaspati. अश्मास्यं (*aśmāsyaṃ*)—hard like a stone; मधुधारं (*madhudhāraṃ*)—full of water; अवतं (*avataṃ*)—hanging down, spread out and swaying; that यं (*yaṃ*)—which cloud; ओजसा (*ojasā*)—by his own strength; अभि अतृणत् (*abhi atṛṇat*)—he split open and divided! तमेव (*tameva*)—that very cloud (the water enclosed in the cloud); विश्वे (*viśve*)—all; स्पर्दृशः (*spardṛśaḥ*) (as supplied)—the rays of the sun; पपिरे (*papire*)—drink (in the hot season). (Again in the rainy season) उत्सं (*utsaṃ*)—in the form of a stream; उद्रिणं (*udriṇaṃ*)—full of water, that is the rain; साकं (*sākaṃ*)—gathering together; बहु (*bahu*)—abundantly; सिसिचुः (*sisicuḥ*)—they pour down.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The cloud that is hard like a stone, full of water and hanging down, which Brahmaṇaspati split and divided by his strength: all the water enclosed in that cloud the sun's rays drink up in the summer. Again, in the rainy season, all of them gathering together pour down abundantly the rain, which is in the form of a stream and full of water.

### Printed p. 22

*Running head: [22] Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 2. A. 3. Sū. 24.] (as supplied; the Maṇḍala figure is printed 2 in the supplied text).*

**Printed English**

That stone-like, solid, down-hanging, water-laden (cloud), which Bramhmanspathi has with his strength divided, the universal solar rays have drunk up: but they have again shed together the showering and water-spreading (rain).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

अश्मास्यं (*aśmāsyaṃ*)—the text gives अश्ववद्दृढतरास्यं (*aśvavaddṛḍhatarāsyaṃ*) (as supplied; the bhāṣya has अश्नवद्दृढतरास्यं (*aśnavaddṛḍhatarāsyaṃ*)); Sāyaṇa's explanation is "very firm like a stone". Yāska, explaining this word, takes the present Ṛk itself as his example and explains: अशनवंतमास्यंदनवंतमवातितं (*aśanavaṃtamāsyaṃdanavaṃtamavātitaṃ*) (Nir. 10-13, printed "ಸಿ ೧೦-೧೩"). That is: व्यापनवंतमासेचनवंतमिति तस्यार्थः (*vyāpanavaṃtamāsecanavaṃtamiti tasyārthaḥ*), that is, a cloud that is pervaded with water and pours down rain. And for this Ṛk he gives the explanation:

अशनवंतमास्यंदनवंतमवतितं ब्रह्मणस्पतिर्मधुधारमभियमोजसा बलेनाभ्यतृणत् तमेव सर्वॆ पिबंति रश्मयः सूर्यदृशो बह्वेनं सह सिंचंत्युत्समुद्रिणमुदकदंतं (*aśanavaṃtamāsyaṃdanavaṃtamavatitaṃ brahmaṇaspatirmadhudhāramabhiyamojasā balenābhyatṛṇat tameva sarvè pibaṃti raśmayaḥ sūryadṛśo bahvenaṃ saha siṃcaṃtyutsamudriṇamudakadaṃtaṃ*) (Nir. 10-13).

The main idea is: this Brahmaṇaspati struck, with his power, the cloud that is full of water and hard as a stone, and made that cloud pour its sweet water downward on the earth. The rays of the sun drink up (suck up) that very water, that is, the water lying on the earth, and again pour it on the earth in the form of rain. When the sun's sharp rays fall upon the sea, rivers, lakes and other reservoirs on the earth, a part of the water in them is heated by the heat of the rays, spreads in the atmosphere as vapour and appears in the form of cloud. That this cloud again rains the water it holds upon the earth is known to all. Modern science also supports this. Therefore the origin of cloud and the causes of rain stated here accord with experience and with science.

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे ऋत्विग्यजमाना वो युष्मदर्थं सना सनातनानि ता तानि का चित् कानि

> दिना मासशब्दस्य मास्भावः । स्वतवस्थासोषसां च त इष्यते । पा. ७-४-४८-२. ।
> इति तत्वं ॥ शरद्भिः संवत्सरैश्च वरंत । निवृण्वंति । तत्काले वर्षणार्थं मेघद्वारा-
> ण्युद्वाटयंतीत्यर्थः। कानि पुनस्तानि। ब्रह्मणस्पतियार्यानि वयुना॥ ज्ञाननामैतत् ॥
> ज्ञानानि मंत्रविषयाणि चकार । कृतवान् । तदनंतरमयतंता अयतमानाव-
> प्रयत्नावुभौ लोक् दौश्च पृथिवी च अन्यदन्यदित् अन्यदन्यदेव पृथिवी वृष्टिजलं
