> *agacchata ‖*

Gist: Brahmaṇaspati's mantra (or wrath), meant to do a great deed, proved true by his will; he drove out the cows and divided them among the gods, and they flowed apart like rivers.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

mahi—great. karma—the work. kariṣyataḥ—of him who will accomplish. brahmaṇaspateḥ—of Brahmaṇaspati. manyuḥ—the mantra, an instrument of meditation (or his wrath). yathāvaśam—according to his wish. satyaḥ—true in effect (not in vain). anubhavat [?]—became. yaḥ—the Brahmaṇaspati who. gāḥ—the cows (carried off by the Paṇis). udājat—released and brought out from the cave. saḥ—that same Brahmaṇaspati. dive—to the gods of the world of the gods. vi abhajat ca—divided and distributed (then that herd of cows). mahīva rītiḥ—like the currents of rivers such as the Gaṅgā, splitting into branches and flowing apart. śavasā—by their own strength. pṛthak—(to the houses of the gods) separately. āsarat—went.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The mantra, an instrument of meditation, of Brahmaṇaspati who accomplishes great works, became fruitful in truth according to his wish. The same Brahmaṇaspati who released from the cave the cows carried off by the Paṇis divided them among the gods in the world of the gods. Then that herd of cows, like the currents of rivers such as the Gaṅgā breaking into branches and flowing apart, went by their own strength separately to the houses of the gods.

**Printed English translation**

The purpose of Brahmaspathi, engaging in a great work, has been successful, according to his wish ; for he it was who recovered (the stolen) cattle for (the dwellers in) heaven ; and distributed them ; so that of their own power they took different directions, like (the branches of) a mighty river.

### Printed p. 45

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

ब्रह्मणस्पतेः मन्युः सत्यः (*brahmaṇaspateḥ manyuḥ satyaḥ*): here the word मन्यु (*manyu*) is explained in two ways, as मननसाधनो मंत्रः क्रोधो वा (*mananasādhano maṃtraḥ krodho vā*), a mantra or (क्रोध (*krodha*)) anger; since Brahmaṇaspati is one who performs great works, the mantra of this god, or his anger as well, is true. And सः यथा कामयते तथा करोति (*saḥ yathā kāmayate tathā karoti*): this god acts as he wishes, that is, he performs his actions (duties) with complete independence; so it is explained.

यः गाः उदजत्, सः दिवे विभजच्च (*yaḥ gāḥ udajat, saḥ dive vibhajacca*): this Brahmaṇaspati, पणिभिरपहृताः गाः उदगमयत् (*paṇibhirapahṛtāḥ gāḥ udagamayat*), brought back the cows seized by the demons, bringing them out of the cave and protecting them; द्युलोकाय तत्रत्येभ्यो देवेभ्यः विभक्ताश्च अकरोत् (*dyulokāya tatratyebhyo devebhyaḥ vibhaktāśca akarot*): he divided them and gave them to the gods dwelling in heaven. This narrative has been told earlier.

महीव रीतिः शवसा पृथक् असरत् यथा गंगादीनां स्रोतः विभक्तं सत् निम्म देशाभिमुखं प्रसरति तथा विभिन्नः तत्तद्देवगृहं आत्मीयेन बलेन अगच्छत (*mahīva rītiḥ śavasā pṛthak asarat yathā gaṃgādīnāṃ srotaḥ vibhaktaṃ sat nimma deśābhimukhaṃ prasarati tathā vibhinnaḥ tattaddevagṛhaṃ ātmīyena balena agacchata*): as the current of the Gaṅgā flows towards low-lying regions and branches, so this herd of cows, in separate droves, reached the houses of the respective gods; thus the comparison with the Gaṅgā's current is stated.

The word शवः (*śavaḥ*) is read by Yāska among the names of strength (Ni. 3-9).

#### Ṛk 15

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> ब्रह्म॑णस्पते सु॒यम॑स्य वि॒श्वहा॑ रा॒यः स्या॑म र॒थ्यो॒३॒॑३वय॑स्वतः ।
> वी॒रेषु॑ वी॒रा उप॑ पृंगि न॒स्त्वं यदीशा॑नो॒ ब्रह्म॑णा॒ वेशि॑ मे॒॑
> हवं ॥ १५ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> ब्रह्मणः । पते । सुयमस्य । विश्वहा । रायः । स्याम । रथ्यः । वयस्वतः ।
> वीरेषु । वीरान् । उप । पृंगि । नः । त्वं । यत् । ईशानः । ब्रह्मणा । वेषि । मे
> हवं ॥ १५ ॥

> *brahmaṇaḥ | pate | suyamasya | viśvahā | rāyaḥ | syāma | rathyaḥ | vayasvataḥ |*
> *vīreṣu | vīrān | upa | pṛṃgi | naḥ | tvaṃ | yat | īśānaḥ | brahmaṇā | veṣi | me*
> *havaṃ ‖ 15 ‖*

### Printed p. 46

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे ब्रह्मणस्पते मंत्राधिपते सुयमस्य सुष्टु नियंतव्यस्य वयस्सतोऽन्न-
> युक्तस्य रायो धनस्य विश्वहा विश्लेषु सर्वॆष्टहःसु रथ्योऽधिपतयः स्याम ।
> भूयास्म । रथा एषां संतीति रथ्यः ॥ छंदसीवनिपाविति मत्त्वर्थीय ईकारः ॥ अपि
> च नोऽस्माकं वीरेषु वीर्याज्जातेष्वस्मदीयेषु पुत्रेषु वीरान् पुत्रान् त्वमुप
> पृज्ञि । संपृक्तान् कुरु । उत्पादय ॥ पृची संपर्के । रौधादिकं ॥ वयं पौत्रानपि
> लभेमहीति यावत् । ईशानः सर्वस्येश्वरस्त्वं ब्रह्मणा ॥ अन्ननामैतत् ॥ हविर्लक्ष-
> णेनान्नेन सह मे हवं मदीयं स्तुतिलक्षणमाह्वानं वेषि । कामयसे ॥ वी गति-
> प्रजननकांत्यसनखादनेषु ॥ अतो मयोक्तं कुर्वित्यर्थः ॥

> *he brahmaṇaspate maṃtrādhipate suyamasya suṣṭu niyaṃtavyasya vayassato 'nna-*
> *yuktasya rāyo dhanasya viśvahā viśleṣu sarvèṣṭahaḥsu rathyo 'dhipatayaḥ syāma |*
> *bhūyāsma | rathā eṣāṃ saṃtīti rathyaḥ ‖ chaṃdasīvanipāviti mattvarthīya īkāraḥ ‖ api*
> *ca no 'smākaṃ vīreṣu vīryājjāteṣvasmadīyeṣu putreṣu vīrān putrān tvamupa*
> *pṛjñi | saṃpṛktān kuru | utpādaya ‖ pṛcī saṃparke | raudhādikaṃ ‖ vayaṃ pautrānapi*
> *labhemahīti yāvat | īśānaḥ sarvasyeśvarastvaṃ brahmaṇā ‖ annanāmaitat ‖ havirlakṣa-*
> *ṇenānnena saha me havaṃ madīyaṃ stutilakṣaṇamāhvānaṃ veṣi | kāmayase ‖ vī gati-*
> *prajananakāṃtyasanakhādaneṣu ‖ ato mayoktaṃ kurvityarthaḥ ‖*

Gist: May we be lords of well-regulated, food-bearing wealth every day; since you accept my invocation with oblation, grant heroic sons among our sons.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

brahmaṇaspate—O Brahmaṇaspati. suyamasya—of that which is excellently regulated. vayasvataḥ—and endowed with food. rāyaḥ—of wealth. viśvahā—on all days. rathyaḥ—as lords. syāma—may we be (grant it). yat—because. īśānaḥ—you, the lord of all. brahmaṇā—together with food in the form of the oblation. me—my. havam—invocation. veṣi—you desire (therefore). naḥ—our. vīreṣu—among the heroic sons. vīrān—heroic sons. upa pṛṅgi—cause them to be born.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Brahmaṇaspati, grant that we may be, on all days, lords of wealth that is excellently [निर्मितवादुदू (*nirmitavādudū*) "constructed", as supplied] ordered and endowed with food. Because you, lord of all, desire my invocation made with the food of the oblation, cause heroic sons to be born among our heroic sons.

**Printed English translation**

May we, Brahmanaspathi, be daily in the possession of well-regulated and food-supplying wealth: add for us posterity to posterity, since you, who are the ruler (of all), accept my invocation with (offered sacrificial) food.

### Printed p. 47

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 3, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 47.*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this ṛk the word ब्रह्मणस्पते (*brahmaṇaspate*) is explained as मंत्राधिपते (*maṃtrādhipate*): Brahmaṇaspati is the lord (master) of all mantras; thus the address is explained.

The sacrificers: रायः विश्वहा रथ्यः स्याम (*rāyaḥ viśvahā rathyaḥ syāma*) — the wish is prayed for that they be lords, at all times, of riches that are certainly worth protecting and that come with food. For the word रथ्यः (*rathyaḥ*) the meaning is given as रथाः एषां संति (*rathāḥ eṣāṃ saṃti*), "those who possess chariots", lords.

नः वीरेषु वीर्रा उपपृच्छि (*naḥ vīreṣu vīrrā upapṛcchi*) (as supplied; the Ṛk reads *vīrān upa pṛṅgi*) — the word वीरेषु (*vīreṣu*) is explained as "those born of virility", i.e. sons; the prayer is that Brahmaṇaspati grant us, the sacrificers, grandsons as well.

ईशानः (*īśānaḥ*) — it is explained that Bṛhaspati is the lord of everything, and since this god desires ब्रह्मणा मे हवं वेषि (*brahmaṇā me havaṃ veṣi*), the invocation in the form of praise made by the sacrificer who comes with food in the form of the oblation, the wish is also expressed that he fulfil the sacrificers' desire.

Yāska has read the word ब्रह्म (*brahma*) among the names of wealth (Ni. 3-9) and also among the names of food. Here it is used in the sense of food.

#### Ṛk 16

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> ब्रह्म॑णस्पते॒ त्वम॒स्य यं॒ता सू॒क्तस्य॑ बोधि॒ तन॑यं च जिन्व ।
> विश्वं॒ तद्भ॒द्रं यदवं॑ति दे॒वा बृ॒हद्व॑देम वि॒दथे॑ सु॒वीराः॑ ॥ १६ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> ब्रह्मणः । पते । त्वं । अस्य । यंता । सुउक्तस्य । बोधि । तनयं । च । जिन्व ।
> विश्वं । तत् । भद्रं । यत् । अवंति । देवाः । बृहत् । वदेम । विदथे । सुवीराः ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे ब्रह्मणस्पते यंता सर्वस्य जगतो नियंता त्वमस्य सूक्तस्येदं सूक्तं॥
> क्रियाग्रहणं कर्तव्यमिति कर्मणः संप्रदानत्वाच्च तुर्थ्यर्थे षष्ठी॥ बोधि। बुध्यस्व ।

> *he brahmaṇaspate yaṃtā sarvasya jagato niyaṃtā tvamasya sūktasyedaṃ sūktaṃ‖*
> *kriyāgrahaṇaṃ kartavyamiti karmaṇaḥ saṃpradānatvācca turthyarthe ṣaṣṭhī‖ bodhi| budhyasva |*

### Printed p. 48

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 3, Sū. 24, p. 48.*

> बुद्धा तनयं पुत्रं चास्मभ्यं जिन्व । प्रेरय ॥ जिविः प्रीणनार्थः । अत्र प्रेरणे
> वर्तते धातूनामनेकार्थत्वात् ॥ यद्भद्रं कल्याणं देवाः सर्वॆSवंति रक्षंति
> विश्वं सर्वं तद्भद्रं च जिन्वेति शेषः । वयं च सुवीराः शोभनैर्विरैः पुत्रपौत्रा-
> दिभिरुपेताः संतो बृहत् प्रौढं स्तोत्रं विदथे । विदंति यष्टव्यतयास्मिन्देवान्
> जानंतीति विदथो यज्ञः । तस्मिन् वदेम । ब्रूयाम ॥ ३ ॥

> *buddhā tanayaṃ putraṃ cāsmabhyaṃ jinva | preraya ‖ jiviḥ prīṇanārthaḥ | atra preraṇe*
> *vartate dhātūnāmanekārthatvāt ‖ yadbhadraṃ kalyāṇaṃ devāḥ sarvèSvaṃti rakṣaṃti*
> *viśvaṃ sarvaṃ tadbhadraṃ ca jinveti śeṣaḥ | vayaṃ ca suvīrāḥ śobhanairviraiḥ putrapautrā-*
> *dibhirupetāḥ saṃto bṛhat prauḍhaṃ stotraṃ vidathe | vidaṃti yaṣṭavyatayāsmindevān*
> *jānaṃtīti vidatho yajñaḥ | tasmin vadema | brūyāma ‖ 3 ‖*

Gist: O Brahmaṇaspati, controller of the world, hear this hymn and send us a son; send us all the good that the gods protect; blessed with fine sons may we recite exalted praise at the sacrifice (*vidatha* = sacrifice).

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

brahmaṇaspate—O Brahmaṇaspati. yantā—controller of the whole world. tvaṃ—you. asya sūktasya—(recited by us) this hymn. bodhi—understand. yat—by which greatness. bhadraṃ—all good. devāḥ—the gods. avanti—protect. viśvaṃ—the whole. tat—that good. jinva—cause to come to us; (therefore) tanayaṃ ca—a son also (jinva—cause to be born to us). suvīrāḥ—we, accompanied by sons and others endowed with virility. bṛhat—exalted praise. vidathe—in this sacrifice. vadema—we shall recite.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Brahmaṇaspati, you, controller of the whole world, understand (hear) this hymn [पतितवाद (*patitavāda*) (*patitavāda*), "fallen", as supplied; presumably *paṭhita*, "recited" — [?]] by us. By the same greatness by which the gods protect all good, cause all good to come to us also. Therefore bring about the gain of a son. We, accompanied by sons and others endowed with virility, shall recite exalted praise at this sacrifice.

**Printed English translation**

Brahmanaspathi, you are the regulator of this (world), understand (the purport of) our hymn, and grant us posterity; for all is prosperous that the gods protect : (and therefore) may we, blessed with excellent descendants, glorify you at this sacrifice.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

Addressing Brahmaṇaspati, it is explained that this god is सर्वस्य जगतो नियंता (*sarvasya jagato niyaṃtā*), the controller of the whole world, and that अस्य सूक्तस्य बोधि (*asya sūktasya bodhi*) means he should listen to this hymn that we recite (the text prints पडिसुव (*paḍisuva*), "that we cause", for the expected पठिसुव (*paṭhisuva*) — as supplied).

तनयं च जिन्व—पुत्रं च प्रेरय । जिविः प्रीणनार्थोSत्र प्रेरणे वर्तते । (*tanayaṃ ca jinva—putraṃ ca preraya | jiviḥ prīṇanārthoStra preraṇe vartate |*) The root जिवि (*jivi*) is used in the sense "to gladden", but here it is used in the sense "to impel"

### Printed p. 49

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 4, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 49.*

(Special Topics continued.) — thus Sāyaṇa explains; and the prayer is that Brahmaṇaspati hear this hymn of ours and impel our sons also to recite this hymn; and सर्वॆ देवाः सर्वं अवंति तत् भद्रं च जिन्व (*sarvè devāḥ sarvaṃ avaṃti tat bhadraṃ ca jinva*): that all the gods impel the good that protects the whole world to come to us.

सुवीराः—शोभनैः वीरैः पुत्रपौत्रादिभिरुपेताः (*suvīrāḥ—śobhanaiḥ vīraiḥ putrapautrādibhirupetāḥ*) — the word सुवीर (*suvīra*) is explained as children and grandchildren endowed with all kinds of good qualities; it is described that, accompanied by them, we recite exalted praises at sacrifices.

विदथे—यष्टव्यतया अस्मिन् देवान् जानाति इति विदथो यज्ञः । (*vidathe—yaṣṭavyatayā asmin devān jānāti iti vidatho yajñaḥ |*) The meaning of the word *vidatha* is explained: the rite in which oblations are offered with the gods in view is called a sacrifice.

## Sūkta 25 (Maṇḍala 2)

*The twenty-fifth sūkta.*

**Sāyaṇa's introduction to the sūkta** (Sanskrit, transcribed; see the Anuvāda below for the sense):

> इंधान इति पंचर्चं तृतीयं सूक्तं गार्त्समदं जागतं ब्राह्मणस्पत्यं ।
> तथा चानुक्रांतं । इंधानः पंच जागतं त्विति । लैंगिको विनियोगः ॥

*Gist:* *indhānaḥ* is the third sūkta of Anuvāka 3, of five ṛks, by Gṛtsamada, in *jagatī* metre, with Brahmaṇaspati as deity; the application is by indication (*liṅga*).

**Anuvāda (Kannada).** The sūkta *indhāno agniṃ* is the third sūkta of the third anuvāka. It has five ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Gṛtsamada; the deity is Brahmaṇaspati; the metre is *jagatī*. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: इंधानः पंच जागतं तु (*iṃdhānaḥ paṃca jāgataṃ tu*). The application (*viniyoga*) is *laiṅgika*.

**Sūkta—25**

Maṇḍala 2 ‖ Anuvāka 3 ‖ Sūkta 25 ‖
Aṣṭaka 2 ‖ Adhyāya 7 ‖ Varga 4 ‖
Number of ṛks in the Sūkta: 5 ·
Ṛṣi: Gṛtsamada · Devatā: as supplied बृह्णस्पतिः (*bṛhṇaspatiḥ*) (the Anuvāda gives Brahmaṇaspati) · Chandas: *jagatī*.

#### Ṛk 1

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> इंधा॑नो अ॒ग्निं व॑नवद्वनुष्य॒तः कृ॒तब्र॑ह्मा शूशुवद्रा॒तह॑व्य॒ इत् ।
> जा॒तेन॑ जा॒तमति॒ स प्र स॑र्सृते॒ यंयं॒ युजं॑ कृणु॒ते ब्रह्म॑ण॒-
> स्पतिः॑ ॥ १ ॥

> *iṃdhāno agniṃ vanavadvanuṣyataḥ kṛtabrahmā śūśuvadrātahavya it |*
> *jātena jātamati sa pra sarsṛte yaṃyaṃ yujaṃ kṛṇute brahmaṇa-*
> *spatiḥ ‖ 1 ‖*

### Printed p. 50

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 25, p. 50.*

#### Ṛk 1 (continued)

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> इंधानः । अग्निं । वनवत् । वनुष्यतः । कृतब्रह्मा । शूशुवत् । रातहव्यः । इत् ।
> जातेन । जातं । अति । सः । प्र । सर्सृते । यंयं । युजं । कृणुते । ब्रह्मणः ।
> पतिः ॥ १ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> अग्निमिंधानो ब्रह्मणस्पतेर्यागार्थमग्निं प्रज्वलयन्यजमानो वनु-
> ष्यतो हिंसिष्यतो हिंसां कुर्वतो वा शत्रून्वनवत् । वनुयात् हिंस्यात् ॥
> वनुष्यतिर्हंतिकर्मति यास्कः । इदिति जार्थे ॥ कृतब्रह्मा ! ब्रह्म स्तोत्रं कृतं
> येन स तथोक्तः । रातहव्यः । रातं दत्तं हव्यं हविर्येन । एवंभूतः सन्
> शूशुवत् । वर्धते॥ टुओश्वि गतिवृद्ध्योः। अस्माण्ण्यंताल्लुजु रूपं॥ तथा यजमानो
> जातेन पुत्रेण जातं जनितमुत्पादितं स्वस्य पौत्रमिति प्रसस्पृते : अतीत्य भृशं
> प्रसरति । पौत्रमपि पश्यन् चिरकालं जीवेदित्यर्थः ॥ सर्तेर्यज् लुगंताद्वत्य-
> येनात्मनेपदं ॥ यंयं यजमानं ब्रह्मणस्पतिः स्तोत्रहविषोः स्वीकरणेन
> युजं सखायं कृणुते । कुरुते । स समान एव भवतीत्यर्थः ॥

*Gist:* the sacrificer who kindles the fire for Brahmaṇaspati destroys hostile enemies, prospers through hymn and oblation, and lives long enough to see his grandson; the grammatical remarks (roots *vanu*, *śuṣ/śū*, *sṛ*) are noted but not explained.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

(For the sacrifice addressed to Brahmaṇaspati) *agnim*—the fire; *indhānaḥ*—the sacrificer who makes it blaze; *vanuṣyataḥ*—the enemies who cause obstruction to the sacrifice; *vanavat*—he strikes and drives far away; *it*—and; *kṛtabrahmā*—composing hymns; *rātahavyaḥ*—offering oblations; *śūśuvat*—he gains increase; *yaṃyam*—whichever sacrificer; *brahmaṇaspatiḥ*—Brahmaṇaspati; *yujam*—as a friend; *kṛṇute*—makes (him his own); *saḥ*—that sacrificer; *jātena*—(beyond) the son; *jātam*—the grandson born; *ati pra sarsṛte*—passes beyond and lives long (he lives long enough to see grandsons and others also).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The sacrificer who makes the fire blaze for the sacrifice addressed to Brahmaṇaspati strikes down and drives far away the enemies who obstruct the sacrifice; and, composing hymns and offering oblations, he gains increase. The sacrificer whom Brahmaṇaspati makes his friend lives long, so that he sees all his sons and grandsons and rejoices.

### Printed p. 51

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 4, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 51.*

**Printed English translation**

Kindling the fire, may (the worshipper) overcome the malevolent (disturbers of the rite); may he, repeating prayers, and laden with oblations, prosper; he whom Brahmanaspathi makes his associate, lives to behold the son of his son.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

अग्निं इंधानः (*agniṃ iṃdhānaḥ*)—ब्रहणस्पतेर्यागार्थमग्निं प्रज्वलर्य यजमानः (*brahaṇaspateryāgārthamagniṃ prajvalarya yajamānaḥ*). Here the word *indhānaḥ* is given the sense "the sacrificer who kindles the fire"; the meaning is explained thus: in the sacrifice performed for Brahmaṇaspati, the sacrificer drives far away, by the greatness of the fire, all the enemies who harm him. Yāska reads वनुष्यतिर्हिंसा कर्मा (*vanuṣyatirhiṃsā karmā*) (Ni. 5-2) ["vanuṣyati means the act of harming"], and cites it, explaining वनुयाम (*vanuyāma*), वनुष्यतः (*vanuṣyataḥ*).

कृतब्रह्मा (*kṛtabrahmā*)—The word *brahma* is read by Yāska (Ni. 3-9) in the senses of food and wealth; Sāyaṇa here explains *brahma* as "hymn" with कृतं ब्रह्म स्तोत्रं येन (*kṛtaṃ brahma stotraṃ yena*).

जातेन जातं (*jātena jātaṃ*)—यजमानः पुत्रेण जनितं स्वस्य पौत्रं अतीत्य भृशं प्रसरति । पौत्रमपि पश्यन् चिरकालं जीवेत् (*yajamānaḥ putreṇa janitaṃ svasya pautraṃ atītya bhṛśaṃ prasarati | pautramapi paśyan cirakālaṃ jīvet*). Here *jātena* means "by the son", i.e. by the sacrificer's son; *jātam* means the grandson born of such a son. So the words *jātena jātam* mean the son of the sacrificer's son, i.e. the grandson. The prayer made here to Brahmaṇaspati is that he grant us sacrificers life to last until the time of such a grandson, so that we may go on living [the source's word जीनिसिरुवंतॆ (*jīnisiruvaṃtè*) is printed thus, as supplied].

ब्रह्मणस्पतिः यंयं युजं कृणुते सः (*brahmaṇaspatiḥ yaṃyaṃ yujaṃ kṛṇute saḥ*)—The explanation of the word *brahmaṇaspati* has been given earlier. Some hold that he is Bṛhaspati, others that he is a deity different from Bṛhaspati. It is explained that this god regards as his friend the sacrificer who has satisfied him with oblations and the like offered in his name, and makes him one of greatness equal to his own.

### Printed p. 52

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 25, p. 52.*

#### Ṛk 2

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> वी॒रेभि॑र्वि॒रान्व॑नद्वनुष्य॒तो गोभी॑ र॒यिं प॑प्रथ॒द्योध॑ति॒ त्मना॑ ।
> तो॒कं च॒ तस्य॒ तन॑यं च वर्धते॒ यंयं॒ युजं॑ कृणु॒ते ब्रह्म॑ण॒-॑
> स्पतिः ॥ २ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> वीरेभिः । वीरान् । वनवत् । वनुष्यतः । गोभिः । रयिं । पप्रथत् । बोधति । त्मना ।
> तोकं । च । तस्य । तनयं । च । वर्धते । यंऽयं । युजं । कृणुते । ब्रह्मणः ।
> पतिः ॥ २ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> स यजमानो वीरेभिर्वीरैरात्मीयैः पुत्रैर्वनुष्यतो हिंसतो वीरान्
> शत्रुपुत्रान् वनवत् । वनुयात् हिंस्यात् । अपि च गोभिः सहितं धनं पप्रथत् ।
> प्रथयति विस्तारयति ॥ प्रथयतेर्लुजि चज्येतद्रूपं ॥ तथा त्मना आत्मनैव बोधति ।
> सर्वं जानाति । अनन्याधीनज्ञानो भवतीत्यर्थः । किंच तस्य यजमानस्य तोकं
> पुत्रस्तनयं ॥ विभक्तिव्यत्ययः ॥ तनयस्तत्पुत्रश्च वर्धते । प्रवृद्दो भवति । यद्वा
> तोकं तनयं पुत्रं पौत्रं च ब्रह्मणस्पतिर्वर्धयति ॥ वृधिरंतर्भावितण्यर्थो
> द्रष्टव्यः । अन्यद्धतं ॥

*Gist:* that sacrificer, with his own sons, destroys the enemies' sons, increases wealth with cattle, knows everything by himself; his son and grandson prosper (or Brahmaṇaspati makes them prosper).

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

*yaṃyam*—whichever sacrificer; *brahmaṇaspatiḥ*—Brahmaṇaspati; *yujam*—as a friend; *kṛṇute*—makes (that sacrificer); *vīrebhiḥ*—together with his heroic sons; *vanuṣyataḥ*—the harming; *vīrān*—enemy sons; *vanavat*—he will strike down; (and) *gobhiḥ*—with cattle; *rayim*—wealth; *paprathat*—he extends and increases; *tmanā*—by his own power; *bodhati*—he knows everything; *tasya*—of that sacrificer; *tokam ca*—the son; *tanayam ca*—and the grandson; *vardhate*—gain increase.

### Printed p. 53

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 4, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 53.*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The sacrificer whom Brahmaṇaspati makes his friend, together with his heroic sons, strikes down the harming sons of his enemies; and he expands and increases his wealth together with cattle. By his own power he knows everything. The son and grandson of such a sacrificer attain increase.

**Printed English translation**

(Surrounded) by his descendants, may he surpass the malevolent descendants (of his adversaries), for hc [sic] is famed for great wealth of cattle, and understands (all things) of himself: his sons and grandsons prosper whom Brahmanaspathi makes his associate.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

वीरेभिः वीरान् वनवत् (*vīrebhiḥ vīrān vanavat*)—Yāska explains वीरो वीरयत्यमित्रान् वेतेर्वा स्याद्गतिकर्मणो वीरयतेर्वा (*vīro vīrayatyamitrān vetervā syādgatikarmaṇo vīrayatervā*) (Ni. 1-7, printed "ಸಿ." [sic]). From this the word *vīra* means one who destroys others by his own power (a hero). Here Sāyaṇa explains वीरेभिः (*vīrebhiḥ*) as आत्मीयैः पुत्रैः (*ātmīyaiḥ putraiḥ*), the sons (children) of the sacrificer, and वीरान् (*vīrān*) as the sons of enemies. The sense is: by the favour of Brahmaṇaspati may the sacrificer subdue the enemy heroes by the heroes on his own side.

गोभिः पप्रथत् (*gobhiḥ paprathat*)—गोभिस्सहितं धनं विस्तारयति (*gobhissahitaṃ dhanaṃ vistārayati*). For the word गौः (*gauḥ*) Yāska gives the meanings earth, animal, hide, phlegm, bowstring, the sun, the suṣumnā channel, rays (Ni. 2-5,6,7.8). Here the meaning "wealth in cows, consisting of cattle, horses, goats and other animals" (paśu) is given, and it is described that the sacrificer expands wealth of this kind by the favour of Brahmaṇaspati (the printed "ಬ್ರಹ್ಮಸ್ಪಣತಿ" is a slip of the supplied text).

त्मना बोधति (*tmanā bodhati*)—आत्मनैव सर्वं जानाति अनन्याधीनज्ञानो भवति (*ātmanaiva sarvaṃ jānāti ananyādhīnajñāno bhavati*). त्मना (*tmanā*) is explained as "by his own nature"; the sense that the sacrificer does so by the favour of Brahmaṇaspati is thereby made explicit.

तोकं तनयं वर्धते (*tokaṃ tanayaṃ vardhate*)—तोकं तनयस्तत्पुत्रः च प्रवृद्धो भवति (*tokaṃ tanayastatputraḥ ca pravṛddho bhavati*); or तोकं तनयं पुत्रं पौत्रं च वर्धयति ब्रह्मणस्पतिः (*tokaṃ tanayaṃ putraṃ pautraṃ ca vardhayati brahmaṇaspatiḥ*). Both senses are stated: Brahmaṇaspati makes the sacrificer's sons and grandsons increase, or by Brahmaṇaspati's favour they themselves increase.

### Printed p. 54

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 25, p. 54.*

#### Ṛk 3

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> सिंधु॒र्न क्षोदः॒ शिमी॑वा ऋघाय॒तो वृषे॑व॒ वध्रे॑र॒भि व॒ष्टो-॑
> जसा ।
> अ॒ग्नेरि॑व॒ प्रसि॑ति॒र्नाह॒ वर्त॑वे॒ यंयं॒ युजं॑ कृणु॒ते ब्रह्म॑ण॒स्पतिः॑॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> सिंधुः । न । क्षोदः । शिमीवान् । ऋघायतः । वृषाइव । वध्रीन् । अभि । वष्टि ।
> ओजसा ।
> अग्नेः इव । प्रसितिः । न । अह । वर्तवे । यंयं । युजं । कृणुते । ब्रह्मणः ।
> पतिः ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> सिंधुः स्यंदनशीला नदी क्षोदो न क्षुद्यमानं कूलं यथा भिनत्ति तथा
> शिमीवान् । शिमाति कर्मनाम । ब्रह्मणस्पतेः परिचरणात्मना कर्मणा युक्तो
> यजमान ऋघायतो हिंसतः शत्रून् ओजसा बलेनाभिवष्टि । हंतुमभितः
> कामयते ॥ वश कांतौ ॥ इदमपरं निदर्शनं । वृषेव वध्रीन् । वध्रयो
> निष्पिष्टमुष्का निर्वीर्या बलीवर्दाः । तान् यथा वृषा सेचनसमर्थः सवीर्यो
> वृषभोऽभिभवति तद्वच्छत्रूनभिभवतीत्यर्थः । अपि चासावग्नेरिव प्रसितिः ।
> प्रसीयते बध्यतेnनयेति प्रसितिर्ज्वाला ॥ षिञ् बंधने । करणे क्तिन् ॥ यथाग्ने-
> र्ज्वालाप्रतिहतप्रसरा एवमपि न वर्तवे । वारयितुं निवर्तयितुं ना नैव
> शक्यते ॥ अहशब्दोऽवधारणे । वृणोतेरंतर्भावितण्यर्थात् वर्ततेर्वा तुमर्थे
> तवेन्प्रत्ययः । अन्यद्धतं ॥

*Gist:* the sacrificer, engaged in service of Brahmaṇaspati, overcomes the harming enemies by strength as a river breaks its bank and a bull throws down weak old oxen; like fire's flame he cannot be checked.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

*sindhuḥ*—the flowing river; *kṣodaḥ na*—as it cuts and splits its bank; *vadhrīn*—the impotent old oxen; *vṛṣeva*—as a strong bull throws them down; *śimāvān* [sic, as supplied]—the sacrificer engaged in the rite that serves Brahmaṇaspati; *yaghāyataḥ* [sic, as supplied]—the enemies who harm him; *ojasā*—by his strength; *abhi vaṣṭi*—surrounding them,

### Printed p. 55

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 4, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 55.*

he wishes to destroy them; *yaṃyam*—whichever sacrificer; *brahmaṇaspatiḥ*—Brahmaṇaspati; *yujam*—as a friend; *kṛṇute*—makes (that sacrificer); *agneḥ iva prasitiḥ*—as fire spreads its flame and advances unobstructed; *na vartave aha*—(by anyone) he gains unstoppable increase.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

As a flowing river cuts and splits its bank, and as a strong bull throws down impotent old oxen, the sacrificer who is engaged in the service of Brahmaṇaspati wishes to surround with his strength and destroy the enemies who harm him. The sacrificer whom Brahmaṇaspati makes his friend gains an increase that no one can stop, as fire spreads its flame and advances unobstructed.

**Printed English translation**

As a river (washes away) its banks, so the devout worshipper, of Brahmanaspathi), [sic] overpowers his enemies by his strength, as a bull (overcomes) an ox : like the spreading flame of fire, it is impossible to stop him, whom Brahmanaspathi makes his associate.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

शिमीर्वा ऋघायतः ओजसा अभि वष्टि (*śimīrvā ṛghāyataḥ ojasā abhi vaṣṭi*)—The word शिमी (*śimī*) is read by Yāska among the names of action (Ni. 3-2), and it is also said शिमीति कर्मनाम, शमयतेर्वा शक्नोतेर्वा (*śimīti karmanāma, śamayatervā śaknotervā*) (Ni. 5-12). Sāyaṇa explains this passage as ब्रह्मणस्पतेः परिचरणात्मना कर्मणा युक्तो यजमानः हिंसतः शत्रुन् बलेन हंतुमभितः कामयते (*brahmaṇaspateḥ paricaraṇātmanā karmaṇā yukto yajamānaḥ hiṃsataḥ śatrun balena haṃtumabhitaḥ kāmayate*): the sacrificer who serves and worships Brahmaṇaspati desires to destroy with his strength those who harm him. Three similes are stated here. First, सिंधुः क्षोदः न (*siṃdhuḥ kṣodaḥ na*)—स्यंदनशीला नदी क्षुद्यमानं कूलं यथा भिनत्ति (*syaṃdanaśīlā nadī kṣudyamānaṃ kūlaṃ yathā bhinatti*): as a flowing river breaks and splits its bank. Second, वृषेव वध्रीन् (*vṛṣeva vadhrīn*)—वध्रयो निष्पिष्टमुष्का निर्वीर्या बलीवर्दाः र्तान् यथा सेचनसमर्थः सवीयः वृषभो अभिभवति (*vadhrayo niṣpiṣṭamuṣkā nirvīryā balīvardāḥ rtān yathā secanasamarthaḥ savīyaḥ vṛṣabho abhibhavati*) [the supplied text prints र्तान् (*rtān*) and सवीयः (*savīyaḥ*) thus]: as bulls of abundant strength gore and throw down feeble old oxen whose seed is crushed. Third, अग्नेरिव प्रसितिः (*agneriva prasitiḥ*)—यथा अग्निर्ज्वालाप्रतिहतप्रसरा वारयितुं निवर्तयितुं वा नैव शक्यते (*yathā agnirjvālāpratihataprasarā vārayituṃ nivartayituṃ vā naiva śakyate*): as the flame of a forest fire can neither be checked nor turned back; 

### Printed p. 56

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 25, p. 56 (printed "ಸಾಯಣಭಾವ್ಯಸಹಿತಾ" in the supplied text).*

thus it is described. By this it is described that the power of the sacrificer who has won Brahmaṇaspati's favour is most wonderful in overcoming his enemies.

#### Ṛk 4

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> तस्मा॑ अर्षंति दि॒व्या अ॑स॒श्चतः॒ स सत्व॑भिः प्रथ॒मो गोषु॑ गच्छति ।
> अनि॑भृष्टतविषिर्हं॒त्योज॑सा॒ यंयं॒ युजं॑ कृणु॒ते ब्रह्म॑ण॒स्पतिः॑ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> तस्मै । अर्षंति । दिव्याः । असश्चतः । सः । सत्त्वभिः । प्रथमः । गोषु । गच्छति ।
> अनिभृष्टतविषिः । हंति । ओजसा । यंयं । युजं । कृणुते । ब्रह्मणः पतिः ॥ ४ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> तस्यॆ यजमानाय दिव्या दिवि भवा वृष्टिलक्षणा आपोऽसश्चतोऽसज्यमाना
> अनिरुद्धाः सत्योऽर्षंति । गच्छंति प्राप्नुवंति ॥ आर्तेर्लॆटि रूपं ॥ तथा स
> यजमानः सत्वभिः । सत्वानः साधकाः परिचारकाः । तैः सहितः प्रथमोऽन्येभ्यो
> यजमानेभ्यः पूर्वभावी सन् गोषु गच्छति । गवादिधनं प्राप्नोतीत्यर्थः । किंच
> अनिभृष्टतविषिः । तविषीति बलनाम । अनिभृष्टा परैरबाधिता तविषी बलं यस्य
> तादृशः सन् ओजसा बलेन हंति शत्रून् ॥

*Gist:* for that sacrificer the rains of heaven pour unobstructed; with his attendants he is first to obtain cattle and wealth, and with unconquered strength he kills enemies.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

*yaṃyam*—whichever sacrificer; *brahmaṇaspatiḥ*—Brahmaṇaspati; *yujam*—as a friend; *kṛṇute*—makes; *tasmai*—for that sacrificer; *divyāḥ*—the rains born of the heavenly world; *asaścataḥ*—without hindrance; *arṣanti*—pour; *haḥ* [sic, as supplied; for *saḥ*?]—he; *satvabhiḥ*—with his attendants; *prathamaḥ*—before other sacrificers; *goṣu*—wealth in the form of cattle; *gacchati*—obtains; *anibhṛṣṭatavisiḥ*—possessing strength that cannot be defeated by others; *ojasā*—by his vigour; *hanti*—he destroys (the enemies).

### Printed p. 57

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6 [sic, as supplied], Varga 4, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 57.*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The sacrificer whom Brahmaṇaspati makes his friend: for him the rains born of the heavenly world pour down without hindrance. Together with his attendants he obtains wealth in the form of cattle before other sacrificers. Possessing a strength that others cannot defeat, he destroys his enemies by his valour.

**Printed English translation**

For him, the unobstructed rains of heaven descend : first among the devout, he acquires (wealth) in cattle: of irresistible vigour, he destroys his enemies by his strength, whom Brahmanaspati makes his associate.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

For the sacrificers who please Brahmaṇaspati with oblations and the like, दिव्याः असश्चतः अर्षंति (*divyāḥ asaścataḥ arṣaṃti*): the waters produced from the excellent rain stored in the atmosphere will [?] (the word as supplied is मॊरकुवुवु (*mòrakuvuvu*)) as they wish. And सत्वभिः प्रथमः:—परिचारकैः सहितः अन्येभ्यः यजमानेभ्यः पूर्वभावी गोषु गच्चति (*satvabhiḥ prathamaḥ:—paricārakaiḥ sahitaḥ anyebhyaḥ yajamānebhyaḥ pūrvabhāvī goṣu gaccati*) [sic, गच्चति (*gaccati*) as supplied]: the sacrificer, accompanied by attendants, obtains wealth such as cattle. Further, अनिभृष्टतविषिः ओजसा बलेन हंति परैरबादिता तविषी यस्य तादृशः बलेन शत्रुहंति (*anibhṛṣṭataviṣiḥ ojasā balena haṃti parairabāditā taviṣī yasya tādṛśaḥ balena śatruhaṃti*): it is explained that the sacrificer who has earned Brahmaṇaspati's favour, possessing abundant strength that others cannot assail ("ಮತು" as supplied) and that suffices to subdue enemies, becomes able to subdue his enemies by it.

The word दिव्य (*divya*) is interpreted by Yāska as "born in heaven" दिविज (*divija*) (Ni. 7-18). The word तविषी (*taviṣī*) is stated among the names of strength (Ni. 3-9).

The word ओजः (*ojaḥ*) is stated both among the names of strength (Ni. 3-9) and among the names of water (Ni. 2-24), and is explained ओज ओजतेर्वा उज्जतेर्वा (*oja ojatervā ujjatervā*) (Ni. 6-8).

#### Ṛk 5

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> तस्मा॒ इद्विश्वे॑ धुनयंत॒ सिंध॒वोऽच्छि॑द्रा॒ शर्म॑ दधिरे पु॒रूणि॑ ।
> दे॒वानां॑ सु॒म्मे सु॒भगः॒ स ए॑धते॒ यंयं॒ युजं॑ कृणु॒ते ब्रह्म॑ण॒॑
> स्पतिः ॥ ५ ॥

> *tasmā idviśve dhunayaṃta siṃdhavo 'cchidrā śarma dadhire purūṇi |*
> *devānāṃ summe subhagaḥ sa edhate yaṃyaṃ yujaṃ kṛṇute brahmaṇa*
> *spatiḥ ‖ 5 ‖*

### Printed p. 58

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 25, p. 58.*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> तस्मै । इत् । विश्वे । धुनयंत । सिंधवः । अच्छिद्रा । शर्म । दधिरे । पुरूणि ।
> देवानां । सुम्मे । सुभगः । सः । विधते । यंयं । युजं । कृणुते । ब्रह्मणः ।
> पतिः ॥ ५ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> तस्मा इत् तस्मा एव यजमानाय विश्वे सिंधवः सर्वाः स्यंदनशीला नद्यो
> धुनयंत । चलयंति । एतदभीष्टफलसिद्ध्यर्थं प्रवहंतीत्यर्थः । तथा अच्छिद्रा
> अच्छिद्राणि छिद्ररहितानि संततानि पुरूणि बहूनि शर्म शर्माणि सुखानि दधिरे ।
> तस्मा एव धार्यंते । यद्वा शर्मेति गृहनाम । छिद्ररहितानि बहूनि सदनानि
> धार्यंते । सुभगः शोभनधनो देवानां सुम्मे देवैः प्रत्ये सुखे वर्तमानः
> सन् विधते । वर्धते । शिष्यं स्पष्टं ॥

*Gist:* for that sacrificer all the flowing rivers stream; many unbroken comforts (or houses) are held for him; rich in wealth and in the gods' favour he prospers.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

*yaṃyam*—whichever sacrificer; *brahmaṇaspatiḥ*—Brahmaṇaspati; *yujam*—as a friend; *kṛṇute*—makes; *tasmā it*—for him alone; *viśve sindhavaḥ*—all the rivers; *dhunayanta*—flow; *acchidrā*—without break, everlasting; *purūṇi*—and abundant; *śarma*—comforts (or houses); *dadhire*—are held (for him); *saḥ*—that sacrificer; *subhagaḥ*—possessing excellent wealth; *devānām*—given by the gods; *summe*—while living in happiness; *vidhate*—enjoys increase.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The sacrificer whom Brahmaṇaspati makes his friend: for his sake all the rivers flow. Comforts (houses) that are unbroken, everlasting and abundant are held for him alone. That sacrificer, possessing excellent wealth and living in the happiness given by the gods, enjoys increase. (The supplied text has "ಅವನಿಗೋ, / ಸ್ಕರವೇ" at the line break, for अवनिगोस्करवे (*avanigoskarave*) "for his sake".)

**Printed English translation**

Verily, for him all rivers flow : him do uninterrupted and numerous pleasures await : blessed with the felicity of the Gods he ever prospers, whom Brahmanaspati makes his associate.

### Printed p. 59

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 5, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 59.*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

विश्वे सिंधवः तस्मा इत् धुनयंत (*viśve siṃdhavaḥ tasmā it dhunayaṃta*)—सर्वाः स्यंदनशीला नद्यः एतदभीष्टफलसिद्ध्यर्थं प्रवहंति (*sarvāḥ syaṃdanaśīlā nadyaḥ etadabhīṣṭaphalasiddhyarthaṃ pravahaṃti*): it is stated, as an extraordinary glory of Brahmaṇaspati, that all the rivers full of currents flow for the sacrificers who have pleased him, only so as to grant the desired objects.

अच्छिद्राणि पुरूणि शर्म दधिरे (*acchidrāṇi purūṇi śarma dadhire*)—अच्छिद्राणि (*acchidrāṇi*) means "without a break" (continuous, strung together without interruption). The word शर्म (*śarma*) is stated to mean both "house" and "comfort" (Ni. 3-13, as supplied, "ಸಿ."). It is described that the sacrificers obtain abundant comfort not mixed with sorrow and the like in the middle period, and छिद्ररहितानि बहूनि सदनानि (*chidrarahitāni bahūni sadanāni*), many firm houses.

Further, the sacrificer who has earned Brahmaṇaspati's favour—सुभगः देवानां सुम्मे विधते (*subhagaḥ devānāṃ summe vidhate*)—शोभनधनः देवैः प्रत्तॆ सुखे वर्तमानः वर्धते (*śobhanadhanaḥ devaiḥ prattè sukhe vartamānaḥ vardhate*) [sic, प्रत्तॆ (*prattè*) as supplied]: having excellent wealth and living in the excellent happiness granted by the gods, he grows; so it is described. The word सुम्म (*summa*) is read among the names of happiness (Ni. 3-13, as supplied, "ಸಿ.").

## Sūkta 26 (Maṇḍala 2)

*The twenty-sixth sūkta.*

**Sāyaṇa's introduction to the sūkta** (Sanskrit, transcribed; see the Anuvāda below for the sense):

> ऋजुरिति चतुर्यचं चतुर्थं सूक्तं गृत्समदस्यार्षं जागतं ब्राह्मणस्पत्यं ।
> तथा चानुक्रांतं । ऋजुश्चतुष्कमिति । गतो विनियोगः ॥

*Gist:* *ṛjuḥ* is the fourth sūkta of Anuvāka 3, of four ṛks, by Gṛtsamada, in *jagatī* metre, with Brahmaṇaspati as deity; the application was stated before.

**Anuvāda (Kannada).** The sūkta *ṛjur* is the fourth sūkta of the third anuvāka. It has four ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Gṛtsamada; the deity is Brahmaṇaspati; the metre is *jagatī*. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: ऋजुश्चतुष्कमिति (*ṛjuścatuṣkamiti*). The application (*viniyoga*) has been stated before, that is, it is as in the preceding sūkta.

**Sūkta—26**

Maṇḍala 2 ‖ Anuvāka 3 ‖ Sūkta 26 ‖
Aṣṭaka 2 ‖ Adhyāya 7 ‖ Varga 5 ‖
Number of ṛks in the Sūkta: 4 ·
Ṛṣi: Gṛtsamada · Devatā: Brahmaṇaspati · Chandas: *jagatī*.

### Printed p. 60

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 26, p. 60 (printed "ಸಾಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯಸಹಿತಾ" in the supplied text).*

#### Ṛk 1

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> ऋ॒जुरिच्छंसो॑ वनवद्वनुष्य॒तो दे॑व॒यन्नि ददे॑वयंतम॒भ्य॑सत् ।
> सु॒प्रा॒वीरिद्व॑नवत्पृ॒त्सु दु॒ष्टरं॒ यज्वेदय॑ज्यो॒र्वि भ॑जाति॒ भो-॑
> जनं ॥ १ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> ऋजुः । इत् । शंसः । वनवत् । वनुष्यतः । देवयन् । इत् । अदेवयंतं । अभि ।
> असत् ।
> सुप्रअवीः । इत् । वनवत् । पृत्सु । दुस्तरं । यज्वा । इत् । अयज्योः । वि ।
> भजाति । भोजनं ॥ १ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> ऋजुरित् आर्जवयुक्त एव । यद्वा मुंजतिः प्रसाधनकर्मा । स्तोत्राणां
> प्रसाधक एव तंसो ब्रह्मणस्पतेः स्तोता वनुष्यतो हिंसतः शत्रून् वनवत् ।
> वनुयात् हिंस्यात् ॥ वनोतेर्हंतिकर्मण एतद्रूपं ॥ तथा देवयन्नित् देवं
> दानादिगुणयुक्तं ब्रह्मणस्पतिमात्मन इच्छन्नेव आदेवयंतं तद्वीपरीतं पुरुष
> मभ्यसत् । अभिभवेत् ॥ अस्तेर्लेट्येतद्रूपं ॥ तथा सुप्रावीरित् ॥ अवतेस्यप्यर्था-
> दौणादिक ईकारप्रत्ययः ॥ सुष्टु प्रकर्षेण ब्रह्मणस्पतेस्तरयितैव पृतु
> पृतनासु संग्रामेषु दुस्तरं तुरीतुमशक्यं वनवत् । हिंस्यात् । तथा यज्येत्
> ब्रह्मणस्पतिं हविषेष्टवानेव आयज्योरयज्जनो भोजनं भोगसाधनु विभजाति ।
> विशेषेण भजेतेत्यर्थः ॥

*Gist:* the upright praiser of Brahmaṇaspati may slay hostile enemies; wishing for the god he overcomes the godless man; he overcomes foes hard to overcome in battles; the sacrificer enjoys the enjoyment of the non-sacrificer.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

*ṛjur it*—the very one of upright mind; *śaṃsaḥ*—the praiser (of Brahmaṇaspati); *vanuṣyataḥ*—the harming enemies; *vanavat*—may he destroy; *devayan nit*—wishing the favour of the god Brahmaṇaspati; *adevayantam*—the man without devotion to the gods; *abhyasat*—may he overcome; *suprāvīr it*—satisfying (Brahmaṇaspati) with worship; *pṛtsu*—in battles (the supplied text breaks off here, at संग्राम (*saṃgrāma*))

### Printed p. 61

*Running head: Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 5, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 61.*

*(Pratipadārtha of Ṛk 1 continued from p. 60.)* *gaḷalli*—in the (battles); *dustaram*—(printed दुस्तरं (*dustaraṃ*)) enemies who cannot be defeated by others; *vanavat*—may he destroy; *yajyet*—(as printed; the Saṃhitā has *yajvā it*) worshipping (Brahmaṇaspati) with oblation; *ayajyoḥ*—of the man without sacrifice; *bhojanam*—the wealth that is a means of enjoyment; *vi bhajāti*—may he obtain fully.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

May the one who praises Brahmaṇaspati with an upright mind destroy the harming enemies. Wishing the favour of Brahmaṇaspati, the divine one, may he overcome men who have no devotion to the gods. Satisfying Brahmaṇaspati with worship, may he destroy in battles the enemy who cannot be overcome by others. Worshipping Brahmaṇaspati with oblation, may he obtain fully the wealth, the means of enjoyment, of the man without sacrifice.

**Printed English translation**

May the sincere praiser (of Brahmanaspati) verily overcome (his) enemies; may the worshipper of the gods overcome him assuredly who does not reverence them: may the propitiator (of Brahmanaspati) overcome him who is difficult to be overcome in battles: may he who offers sacrifice appropriate the food of him who does not sacrifice

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this ṛk the word *it* is read in the words *ṛjur it*, *devayan nit*, *suprāvīr it* and *yajvet* [sic, as printed यज्वेत् (*yajvet*)]. For this word *it*, Yāska has cited, as "*cid it* is of many senses" (चिदित्येषोऽनेककर्मा (*cidityeṣo 'nekakarmā*), Ni. 1-4), the use of (*cit*) in many kinds of meanings. Here it is explained that the word is used in the sense of emphasis (*avadhāraṇa*) in all four places.

For the first word, *ṛjur it*, the explanation is आर्जुवयुक्त एव । यद्वा यंजति प्रसाधनकर्मा । स्तोत्राणां प्रसाधक एव (*ārjuvayukta eva | yadvā yaṃjati prasādhanakarmā | stotrāṇāṃ prasādhaka eva*) [the supplied text has ಯಂಜತಿ where Sāyaṇa's text of p. 60 has the root *muñj*; not changed]: the sacrificer who worships Brahmaṇaspati is one who is endowed with a straight path, or one who wins (him) over with praises; so the meaning is described.

देवयन्नित्-दानादिगुणयुक्तं ब्रह्मणस्पतिं आत्मन इच्छन्नेन (*devayannit-dānādiguṇayuktaṃ brahmaṇaspatiṃ ātmana icchannena*) [as supplied; apparently *icchanneva*]: the sacrificer is one who desires as his lord Brahmaṇaspati, who possesses the qualities of granting desired ends to those who worship him with praises and so on; so the meaning is explained.

### Printed p. 62

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 26, p. 62.*

सुप्रावीत्—सुष्टु प्रकर्षेण ब्रह्मणस्पतेस्तर्पयित्यैव (*suprāvīt—suṣṭu prakarṣeṇa brahmaṇaspatestarpayityaiva*) [as supplied]: the sacrificer's quality is described as one who satisfies Brahmaṇaspati exceedingly.

यजेत्—ब्रह्मणस्पतिं हविषा इष्टवानेव (*yajet—brahmaṇaspatiṃ haviṣā iṣṭavāneva*): the sacrificer is one who delights Brahmaṇaspati in sacrifices with oblations. The qualities of the sacrificer having been described with these four words, it is further explained that the sacrificer rejects अदेवयंतं (*adevayaṃtaṃ*), those who do not worship Brahmaṇaspati, and the harming enemies, who lack these qualities; and that अयज्जनः भोगसाधनं विशेषेण भजते (*ayajjanaḥ bhogasādhanaṃ viśeṣeṇa bhajate*) [as supplied], he obtains the means of comfort of one who does not perform sacrifice.

#### Ṛk 2

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> यज॑स्व वीर॒ प्र वि॑हि मनाय॒तो भ॒द्रं मनः॑ कृणुष्व वृत्र॒तूर्ये॑ ।
> ह॒विष्कृ॑णुष्व सु॒भगो॒ यथास॑सि॒ ब्रह्म॑ण॒स्पते॒रव॒ आ वृ॑णीमहे ॥ २ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> यजस्व । वीर । प्र । विहि । मनायतः । भद्रं । मनः । कृणुष्ट । वृत्रतूर्ये ।
> हः । कृणष्ट्व । सुभगः । यथा । अससि । ब्रह्मणः । पतेः । आवः । आ । वृणीमहे ॥ २

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> ऋषिरात्मीयं पुरुषं शास्ति । हे वीर विशेषेण स्तुतीनां प्रेरक सखे
> यजस्व । ब्रह्मणस्पतिं स्तुत्या पूजय । मनायतो मन एव शीघ्रं प्रवर्तमानान्
> हिंसकान् यद्वा अभिमन्यमानान् शत्रुन्प्रति प्रविहि । प्रकर्षेण युद्धार्थं गच्छ ॥
> वी गत्यादिषु । छांदसो ह्रस्वः ॥ वृत्रतूर्ये वृत्राणामावारकाणां शत्रुणां
> हिंसाहेतुभूते संग्रामे भद्रं कल्याणं धार्ष्ट्योपेतं मनः कृणुष्ट । कुरुष्ट ।
> ब्रह्मणस्पतेरनुग्रहात्तवैव जयो भविष्यति । तथा ब्रह्मणस्पतये हविश्वरपुरोडाशा-
> दिकं कृणुष्ट । कुरु । यथा त्वं सुभगः शोभनधनोऽससि । भवेः ॥ अस्तेर्लेट्य।
> डागमः ॥ ब्रह्मणस्पतेर्यागेन त्वं शोभनधनो भविष्यसीति यावत् । अपि च वयं
> ब्रह्मणस्पतेरवो रक्षणमाभिमुख्येन वृणीमहे । प्रार्थयामहे ॥

*Gist:* the seer instructs his own man: O hero, worship Brahmaṇaspati with praise, go forth against the swift hostile enemies, make a brave mind, prepare oblations so that you prosper; we also solicit his protection.

*Note on the text as supplied:* the Pada line has कृणुष्ट (*kṛṇuṣṭa*) and कृणष्ट्व (*kṛṇaṣṭva*) for the Saṃhitā's कृणुष्व (*kṛṇuṣva*), and हः (*haḥ*) where हविः (*haviḥ*) would be expected; the bhāṣya has कृणुष्ट (*kṛṇuṣṭa*), हविश्वर (*haviśvara*) and अससि (*asasi*). Left as supplied.

### Printed p. 63

*Running head: Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 3, Varga 5, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 63.*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

*vīra*—O friend, who in a special way urge on the praise; *yajasva*—offer worship (to Brahmaṇaspati); *manāyataḥ*—(to defeat) the enemies who advance swiftly; *pra vihi*—set out facing the battle; *vṛtratūrye*—in the battle that destroys Vṛtra and the others; *bhadram*—firmly established and bringing welfare; *manaḥ*—the mind; *kṛṇuṣṭva* (printed कृणुष्ट्व (*kṛṇuṣṭva*))—make; *yathā*—in what manner; *subhagaḥ*—possessing excellent wealth; *asasi*—you become (in that way); *haviḥ*—cooked offerings, purodāśa and the like; *kṛṇuṣṭva*—(for Brahmaṇaspati) prepare; (*vayaṃ*—we also); *brahmaṇaspateḥ*—of Brahmaṇaspati; *avaḥ*—the protection; *ā*—facing it; *vṛṇīmahe*—we solicit.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O sacrificer, who in a special way urge on the praise, offer worship to Brahmaṇaspati. Set out facing the battle to defeat the enemies who advance swiftly, wishing only harm to you in their minds. In the battle that destroys Vṛtra and the others, make a mind that is firmly established and brings welfare. In the manner by which you become possessed of excellent wealth, prepare for Brahmaṇaspati the cooked offerings, purodāśa and the like. We also, facing it, solicit the protection of Brahmaṇaspati. (The supplied text has "ಪ್ರಾರ್ಥಿಸುತ್ತೇನೆ", "I solicit", in the singular; and "ಮನ್ನುಗ್ಗಿ" for ಮುನ್ನುಗ್ಗಿ.)

**Printed English translation**

Offer worship, O man, (to Brahmanaspati): proceed resolutely against those who are meditating (hostility): keep your mind firm in contending against foes: offer oblations whereby you may be prosperous: we also solicit the protection of Brahmanaspathi.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

The manner in which the seer, the seer of the mantra, instructs his own men, those dear to him, is stated in this mantra.

*vīra yajasva*—here the word *vīra* is explained as स्तुतीनां प्रेरक सखे (*stutīnāṃ preraka sakhe*), "friend who urges on the praises" (the supplied Kannada has "ಪರಿಸುವ", apparently for *prerisuva*, "urging"); and the injunction is expressed that Brahmaṇaspati must be worshipped with praise.

मनायतः विहि—मन इव शीघ्रं प्रवर्तमानान्, हिंसका. अभिमन्यमानान् शत्रु प्रति युद्धार्थं गच्छ (*manāyataḥ vihi—mana iva śīghraṃ pravartamānān, hiṃsakā. abhimanyamānān śatru prati yuddhārthaṃ gaccha*) [as supplied]: with a swiftness abundant like the speed of the mind, rush forward... (the supplied text breaks off here, at ಮುನ್ನುಗ್ಗಿ).

### Printed p. 64

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 7, Sūkta 26, p. 64 (printed "ಸಾಯಣಭಾಷ್ಯಸಹಿತಾ" in the supplied text).*

(Special Topics of Ṛk 2 continued from p. 63.) ...forward, coming and doing violence, or against enemies who are full of arrogance: so it is said.

*vṛtratūrye bhadraṃ manaḥ kṛṇuṣṭa*—Sāyaṇa explains the word *vṛtra* in this sentence as used in the sense of enemies who envelop and do harm. Yāska has explained the various meanings of the word *vṛtra* (Ni. 2-16), and the word *vṛtratūrya* he has read among the names of battle (Ni. 3-9). Here it is stated, as the view of the seer of the mantra, that a mind endowed with courage should be employed against the enemies who practise violence, and that "by the favour of Brahmaṇaspati victory will be yours alone" (ब्रह्मणस्पतेरनुग्रहात्तवैव जयो भविष्यति (*brahmaṇaspateranugrahāttavaiva jayo bhaviṣyati*)).

यथा त्वं सुभगः आससि—ब्रह्मणस्पतेर्यागेन त्वं शोभनधनो भविष्यसि (*yathā tvaṃ subhagaḥ āsasi—brahmaṇaspateryāgena tvaṃ śobhanadhano bhaviṣyasi*): it is said that by performing sacrifice with Brahmaṇaspati as the object, the attainment of excellent wealth will come about. And वयं ब्रह्मणस्पतेः अवः आवृणीमहे (*vayaṃ brahmaṇaspateḥ avaḥ āvṛṇīmahe*), "we greatly desire protection from Brahmaṇaspati": this wish is also expressed.

> स इज्जने॑न॒ स वि॒शा स जन्म॑ना॒ स पु॒त्रैर्वाजं॑ भरते॒ धना॒ नृभिः॑ ।
> दे॒वानां॒ यः पि॒तर॑मा॒विवा॑सति श्र॒द्धाम॑ना ह॒विषा॒ ब्रह्म॑ण॒स्पतिं॑ ॥ ३ "

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> सः । इत् । जनेन । सः । विशा । सः । जन्मना । सः । पुत्रैः । वाजं । भरते । धना ।

> देवानां । यः । पितरं । आविवासति । श्रद्धामनाः । हविषा । ब्रह्मणः । पतिं ॥ ३ ॥

> *devānāṃ | yaḥ | pitaraṃ | āvivāsati | śraddhāmanāḥ | haviṣā | brahmaṇaḥ | patiṃ ‖ 3 ‖*

### Printed p. 65

*Running head: Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 7 [sic, as supplied], Varga 5, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 65. (The running head was merged into the Pada block in the supplied text; placed here by hand.)*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> यो यजमानः श्रद्धामनाः श्रद्धा मनसि यस्य शादृशः सन् देवानां पितरं
> पालयितारं ब्रह्मणस्पतिं हविषा चरुपुरोडाशादिनाविवासति । परिचरति ॥ विवासतिः
> परिचरणकर्मा ॥ स इत् स एव यजमानो जनेन जातेन सर्वेण लोकेन
> वाजमन्नं भरते बिभर्ति संभरति वा । स एव विशा प्रजया जन्मनात्मीयेन बंधु-
> जनेन वाजं भरते । स एव पुत्रैरात्मीयैरपत्यैर्वाजं भरते । तथा नृभिर्नतृभि-
> रन्यैः परिचारकैर्धना धनानि भरते । बिभर्ति संपादयति वा । ब्रह्मणस्पतिमिष्टवतो
> यजमानस्य सर्वतो जना अन्नधनयोः साधका भवंतीति यावत् ॥

*Gist:* the sacrificer who serves Brahmaṇaspati, father of the gods, with oblations obtains food and wealth through all people, his kin, sons and attendants.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

यः (*yaḥ*)—the sacrificer who; श्रद्धामनाः (*śraddhāmanāḥ*)—having a mind full of faith; देवानां (*devānāṃ*)—of the gods; पितरं (*pitaraṃ*)—the protector; ब्रह्मणस्पतिं (*brahmaṇaspatiṃ*)—Brahmaṇaspati; हविषा (*haviṣā*)—with cooked offerings, caru, purodāśa and the like; आ विवासति (*ā vivāsati*)—serves and worships; स इत् (*sa it*)—that sacrificer alone; (as his own kin) जन्मना जनेन (*janmanā janena*)—with all the people who have come into being; वाजं (*vājaṃ*)—food; भरते (*bharate*)—obtains; सः (*saḥ*)—he alone; विशा (*viśā*)—with his subjects (obtains food); सः (*saḥ*)—he alone; पुत्रैः (*putraiḥ*)—with his sons (obtains food); नृभिः (*nṛbhiḥ*)—with men who are his attendants; धना (*dhanā*)—(obtains) riches.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The sacrificer who, with a mind full of faith, attends and worships Brahmaṇaspati, the protector of the gods, with caru, purodāśa and the like, that sacrificer alone obtains food together with all those who have come into being as his kin, together with his subjects and with his sons. That sacrificer alone obtains riches together with the men who are his attendants. (The supplied text has "ಚರುಪುರೋಡಾರಾದಿಗಳಿಂದ" for ಚರುಪುರೋಡಾಶಾದಿಗಳಿಂದ, and "ನರದೊಡನೆ", apparently for ನರರೊಡನೆ; left as supplied.)

**Printed English translation**

He who with a believing mind worships Brahmanaspati, the father of the gods, with oblations. he verily receives food, together with his sons, his kindred. his descendants. his people, and (obtains) riches together with (all) men.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

ब्रह्मणस्पतिमिष्टवतो यजमानस्य जनाः सर्वतो अन्नधनयोः साधकाः भवंति (*brahmaṇaspatimiṣṭavato yajamānasya janāḥ sarvato annadhanayoḥ sādhakāḥ bhavaṃti*): all people, in many ways, give food and wealth and help the sacrificer who performs a sacrifice directed to the god Brahmaṇaspati. This meaning is explained in this Ṛk by four sentences

### Printed p. 66

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 26, p. 66.*

(continued) ...four sentences.

श्रद्धामनाः हविषा आ विवासति स इत् वाजं भरते (*śraddhāmanāḥ haviṣā ā vivāsati sa it vājaṃ bharate*): here the word विवासति (*vivāsati*) Yāska (Ni. 3-13-11-23, as printed) has read among the words meaning service. It is stated that the sacrificer who, full of devotion, has satisfied Brahmaṇaspati with oblations obtains food and nourishes all others.

In the same way, आत्मीयेन बंधुजनेन, आत्मीयैरपत्यैः नेतृभिरन्यैः परिचारकैः वाजं भरते संपादयति वा (*ātmīyena baṃdhujanena, ātmīyairapatyaiḥ netṛbhiranyaiḥ paricārakaiḥ vājaṃ bharate saṃpādayati vā*): it is explained that for a sacrificer of this kind his kinsfolk, his sons, and the servants who attend him also obtain food and wealth and are nourished by him. (The supplied text has "ಅವನು ಪುತ್ರರೂ" and "ವಿವರಿಸಿದೆ" preceded by "ಎಂದ"; left as supplied.)

#### Ṛk 4

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> यो अ॑स्मै ह॒वैर्घृ॒तव॑द्भि॒रवि॑ध॒त्प्र, तं प्रा॒चा न॑यति॒ ब्रह्म॑ण॒स्पतिः॑ ।
> उ॒रु॒ष्यती॒मंह॑सो॒ रक्ष॑शी रि॒षो३ं॒३॒॑होश्चि॑दस्मा उरु॒चक्रि॒रद्भु॑तः ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> यः । अस्मै । हव्यैः । धृतवत्भिः । अविधत् । प्र । तं । प्राचा । नयति ।
> ब्रह्मणः । पतिः ।
> उरुष्यति । ईं । आंहसः । रक्षति । रिषः । आंहोः । चित् । अस्मै । उरुचक्रिः ।
> अद्भुतः ॥ ४ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> यो यजमानोऽस्मै ब्रह्मणस्पतये ॥ क्रियाग्रहणं कर्तव्यमिति कर्मणः
> संप्रदानत्वाच्चतुर्थी ॥ इमं ब्रह्मणस्पतिं धृतवद्भिरुपस्तरणाभिघारणात्मकेनाज्ये-
> नोपेतैर्हवैर्हविर्भिरविधत् । परिचरति ॥ विध विधाने तौदादिकः । आयं परिचरण-

> *yo yajamāno 'smai brahmaṇaspataye ‖ kriyāgrahaṇaṃ kartavyamiti karmaṇaḥ*
> *saṃpradānatvāccaturthī ‖ imaṃ brahmaṇaspatiṃ dhṛtavadbhirupastaraṇābhighāraṇātmakenājye-*
> *nopetairhavairhavirbhiravidhat | paricarati ‖ vidha vidhāne taudādikaḥ | āyaṃ paricaraṇa-*

### Printed p. 67

*Running head: Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 5, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 67.*

> कर्मसु पठितः ॥ तं यजमानं ब्रह्मणस्पतिः प्राचा प्राचीनेन ऋजुना मार्गेण
> प्रणयति । प्रकृष्टफलं प्रापयति । अपि च ईमेनमंहसः पापादुरुष्यति । रक्षति ॥
> उरुष्यती रक्षाकर्मेति यास्कः ॥ तथा रिषो हिंसकादेनं रक्षति । अंहोश्चित् ।
> अवृत्तेर्दारिद्रात् । यद्वा अगत्य हंतुरपि रक्षति । अपि चास्मै यजमानायाद्भुतः ।
> महन्नामैतत् । आश्चर्यभूतो महान् ब्रह्मणस्पतिरुरुचक्रिरुरोविस्तीर्णस्योप-
> कारस्य कर्ता भवति ॥

*Gist:* Brahmaṇaspati leads the worshipper by a straight path to his reward, protects him from sin, the harmful foe and poverty, and as a great and wonderful one does him many favours.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

यः (*yaḥ*)—the sacrificer who; अस्मै (*asmai*)—with this Brahmaṇaspati as the object; धृतवद्भिः (*dhṛtavadbhiḥ*)—endowed with ghee; हव्यैः (*havyaiḥ*)—with oblations; अविधत् (*avidhat*)—worships; तं (*taṃ*)—that sacrificer; ब्रह्मणस्पतिः (*brahmaṇaspatiḥ*)—Brahmaṇaspati; प्राचा (*prācā*)—by a most straight path; प्र नयति (*pra nayati*)—leads towards the fruit; (and) ईं (*īṃ*)—this sacrificer; अंहसः (*aṃhasaḥ*)—from sin; उरुष्यति (*uruṣyati*)—protects; रिषः (*riṣaḥ*)—from the harmful enemy; रक्षति (*rakṣati*)—protects; अंहोश्चित् (*aṃhościt*)—from the sin in the form of poverty also (protects); अस्मै (*asmai*)—to this same sacrificer; अद्भुतः (*adbhutaḥ*)—the great-souled Brahmaṇaspati; उरुचक्रिः (*urucakriḥ*)—becomes also abundantly a benefactor.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The sacrificer who worships Brahmaṇaspati with oblations endowed with ghee, that sacrificer Brahmaṇaspati leads along a most straight path towards the fruit. And he protects this sacrificer from sin, from the harmful enemy and from poverty. To this same sacrificer the great-souled Brahmaṇaspati becomes abundantly a benefactor.

**Printed English translation**

Brahmanaspathi leads by a straight path (to his reward), the worshipper who propitiates him with oblations of butter : he guards him against sin; he protects him from foes and from distresss [sic] ; and wonderful (in might), confers upon him many (favours)-

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

तं प्राचा प्रनयति—ब्रह्मणस्पतिः ऋजुना मार्गेण प्रकृष्टं फलं प्रापयति (*taṃ prācā pranayati—brahmaṇaspatiḥ ṛjunā mārgeṇa prakṛṣṭaṃ phalaṃ prāpayati*): Brahmaṇaspati, pleased by the offerings of oblations and the like, accompanied by the substances beginning with ghee (printed धृतादि (*dhṛtādi*)) which have been prepared (the supplied text breaks off here, at the end of the page, after "ಬ್ರಹ್ಮಣಸ್ಪತಿಯು").

### Printed p. 68

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 27, p. 68.*

leads the sacrificer along the right path and makes him a sharer of excellent fruit. Here the word प्राचा (*prācā*) is explained as प्राचीनेन (*prācīnena*): it is made clear that the meaning is a straight path followed by the forefathers.

ईं अंहसः उरुष्यति—एनं पापात् रक्षति (*īṃ aṃhasaḥ uruṣyati—enaṃ pāpāt rakṣati*): for the particle ईं (*īṃ*) the meaning "this sacrificer" is given here. Yāska has explained पादपूरणास्ते अनर्थकाः कमीमिद्विति (*pādapūraṇāste anarthakāḥ kamīmidviti*) (Ni. 1-9): the particles कं, ईं, इत्, उ (*kaṃ, īṃ, it, u*) are employed only to fill out a sentence and convey no meaning of their own. Having commented उरुष्यती रक्षाकर्मा इति यास्कः (*uruṣyatī rakṣākarmā iti yāskaḥ*), it is described that Brahmaṇaspati protects the sacrificer from sins.

For the word अंहोश्चित् (*aṃhościt*), two meanings are given: अवृत्तेर्दारिद्र्यात् यद्वा अगत्य हंतुरपि (*avṛtterdāridryāt yadvā agatya haṃturapi*), that is, from poverty (want of livelihood), or from one who comes near and kills; it is stated that Brahmaṇaspati protects from these.

अस्मै अद्भुतः—आचार्यभूतः महान् (*asmai adbhutaḥ—ācāryabhūtaḥ mahān*): the word अद्भुत (*adbhuta*) is stated among the names of "great" (Ni. 3-13). It is described that Brahmaṇaspati, being a teacher and a revered one to the sacrificer, performs abundant service for him.

## Sūkta 27 (Maṇḍala 2)

*The twenty-seventh sūkta.*

**Sāyaṇa's introduction to the sūkta** (Sanskrit, transcribed; see the Anuvāda below for the sense):

> इमा गिर इति सप्तदशर्चं पंचमं सूक्तं गृत्समदपुत्रस्य कूर्मस्यार्षं
> गृत्समदस्यैव वा त्रैष्टुभमादित्यदेवताकं । तथा चानुक्रांतं । इमा गिरस्त्रूना
> कूर्मो गार्त्समदो हि वादित्यमिति ॥ सूक्तविनियोगो लैंगिकः । आदित्यदेवताके
> पशौ पुरोडाशस्यॆमा गिर इत्येषानुवाक्या । अग्नीषोमाविति खंडे सूत्रितं । इमा
> गिर आदित्येभ्यो धृतसूस्त आदित्यास उरवो गभीराः । आ-३-८ । इति ॥

*Gist:* *imā giraḥ* is the fifth sūkta of Anuvāka 3, of seventeen ṛks, by Kūrma son of Gṛtsamada (or Gṛtsamada himself), in *triṣṭubh* metre, with the Ādityas as deity; its application is by indication (*laiṅgika*), the first ṛk being the *anuvākyā* for the oblation cake in an animal sacrifice to the Ādityas.

**Anuvāda (Kannada).** The sūkta *imā giraḥ* is the fifth sūkta of the third anuvāka. It has seventeen ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Kūrma, son of Gṛtsamada, or it is said that Gṛtsamada himself is the seer. The Ādityas are the deity; the metre is *triṣṭubh*. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: इमा गिरस्त्रूना कूर्मो गार्त्समदो हि वादित्यमिति (*imā girastrūnā kūrmo gārtsamado hi vādityamiti*) [as supplied]. The application of the sūkta is *laiṅgika* (by indication). In an animal sacrifice related to the Ādityas, the first ṛk of this sūkta, *imā giraḥ*, is to be recited as the *anuvākyā* mantra while the oblation cake is offered; this is set out by the sūtra *imā gira ādityebhyo ghṛtasūsta ādityāsa uravo gabhīrāḥ* in the section *Agnīṣomau* of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra (Ā. 3-8).

### Printed p. 69

*Running head: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 6, p. 69.*

**Sūkta—27**

Maṇḍala 2 ‖ Anuvāka 3 ‖ Sūkta 27 ‖
Aṣṭaka 2 ‖ Adhyāya 7 ‖ Varga 6, 7, 8 ‖
Number of ṛks in the Sūkta: 17 ·
Ṛṣi: as supplied "ಕರ್ಮೋ ಗಾತ್ಸರ್ಮವೋ ಗೃತ್ಸಮದೋ ವಾ" (Kūrma Gārtsamada, or Gṛtsamada; the supplied reading is garbled, left as supplied) · Devatā: the Ādityas · Chandas: *triṣṭubh* (the supplied text has "ಭಂದುಃ" for the label).

#### Ṛk 1

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> इ॒मा गिर॑ आदि॒त्येभ्यो॑ घृ॒तस्नूः॑ स॒नाद्राज॑भ्यो जु॒ह्वा॑ जुहोमि ।
> शृ॒णोतु॑ मि॒त्रो अ॑र्य॒मा भगो॑ नस्तुविजा॒तो वरु॑णो॒ दक्षो॒॑
> अंशः ॥ १ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> इमाः । गिरः । आदित्येभ्यः । धृतस्नूः । सनात् । राजभ्यः । जुह्वा । जुहोमि ।
> शृणोतु । मित्रः । अर्यमा । भगः । नः । तुविजातः । वरुणः । दक्षः । अंशः ॥ १ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> आदित्येभ्योऽदितेः पुत्रेभ्यो राजभ्यो राजमानेभ्य ईश्वरेभ्यो वा
> मित्रादिभ्यो धृतस्नूर्घृतप्रस्राविणीर्घृतं क्षरंतीरिमा गिरः स्तुतिलक्षणा वाचः
> सनात्सर्वदा जुह्वा । वाज्ञामैतत् । वागिंद्रियेण जुहोमि । प्रयच्छामि । करो-
> मिति यावत् ! यद्वा जुहूस्थानीयेन धृतं क्षरंति वाग्रूपाणि हवींषि जुहोमि।
> आदित्यानुद्दिश्य त्यजामि । मंत्राणां पयोघृतादिक्षणहेतुत्वं च य ऋचोऽधीते
> पयसः कूल्या अस्येत्यादिना तैत्तिरीयके स्पष्टमाम्मातं । तै. आ. २. १०. ७. ।
> नोऽस्मदीयस्ता गिरो मित्रादयः प्रत्येकं शृणोतु । ते च तैत्तिरीयेऽष्टौ
> पुत्रासो आदितेरित्युपक्रम्य स्पष्टमनुक्रांताः । मित्रश्च वरुणश्च धाता चार्यमा

> *ādityebhyo 'diteḥ putrebhyo rājabhyo rājamānebhya īśvarebhyo vā*
> *mitrādibhyo dhṛtasnūrghṛtaprasrāviṇīrghṛtaṃ kṣaraṃtīrimā giraḥ stutilakṣaṇā vācaḥ*
> *sanātsarvadā juhvā | vājñāmaitat | vāgiṃdriyeṇa juhomi | prayacchāmi | karo-*
> *miti yāvat ! yadvā juhūsthānīyena dhṛtaṃ kṣaraṃti vāgrūpāṇi havīṃṣi juhomi|*
> *ādityānuddiśya tyajāmi | maṃtrāṇāṃ payoghṛtādikṣaṇahetutvaṃ ca ya ṛco 'dhīte*
> *payasaḥ kūlyā asyetyādinā taittirīyake spaṣṭamāmmātaṃ | tai. ā. 2. 10. 7. |*
> *no 'smadīyastā giro mitrādayaḥ pratyekaṃ śṛṇotu | te ca taittirīye 'ṣṭau*
> *putrāso āditerityupakramya spaṣṭamanukrāṃtāḥ | mitraśca varuṇaśca dhātā cāryamā*

### Printed p. 70

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 27, p. 70.*

> च अंशुश्च भगश्च इंद्रश्च विवस्वांश्चेत्येत इति । मित्रः प्रमीतेस्त्रायकः । यद्वा
> सर्वेषां स्निग्धः । अरीन् यच्छति नियच्छतीत्यर्यमा ! भगो भजनीयो देवः ।
> तुवीति बहुनाम । बहुषु देशेष्वनुग्रहार्थं प्रादुर्भूतः । वरुणविशेषणमेतत् ।
> पापस्य निवारणाद्वरुणः । दक्षः समर्थः । एतच्चांशस्य विशेषणं । एते प्रत्येकं
> शृण्वंतु ॥

*Gist:* To the Ādityas, sons of Aditi, I offer these ghee-flowing praises with the ladle of speech; may Mitra, Aryaman, Bhaga, Varuṇa, Dakṣa and Aṃśa each hear them.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

आदित्येभ्यः (*ādityebhyaḥ*)—to the sons of Aditi; राजभ्यः (*rājabhyaḥ*)—to the shining ones, the deities Mitra and the rest, addressing them; धृतस्नूः (*dhṛtasnūḥ*)—that cause ghee to flow; इमा गिरः (*imā giraḥ*)—these words in the form of praise; सनात् (*sanāt*)—always; जुह्वा (*juhvā*)—by means of the ladle (*juhū*) that is the organ of speech; जुहोमि (*juhomi*)—I offer [the supplied text has "ಆರ್ಪಿಸುತ್ತೇನೆ"]; नः (*naḥ*)—these praise-words of ours; मित्रः (*mitraḥ*)—Mitra; अर्यमा (*aryamā*)—Aryaman; भगः (*bhagaḥ*)—Bhaga; तुविजातः (*tuvijātaḥ*)—(for the good of the world) born in many places; वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*)—Varuṇa; दक्षः (*dakṣaḥ*)—Dakṣa; अंशः (*aṃśaḥ*)—Aṃśa; शृणोतु (*śṛṇotu*)—let hear.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

To the sons of Aditi, the shining Mitra and the other deities, I offer always these praise-words, which cause ghee to flow, by means of the ladle that is the organ of speech. May Mitra, Aryaman, Bhaga, Varuṇa who is born in many places for the good of the world, Dakṣa and Aṃśa hear these praise-words of ours.

**Printed English translation**

I present cotinually [sic], with the ladle (of speech), these oblation-droping [sic] hymns to the royal Adityas: May Mitra, Aryaman, Bhaga, the multipresent Varuna, the powerful Ansha, hear us.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

आदित्येभ्यः राजभ्यः धृतस्नूः गिरः जुह्वा जुहोमि—आदितिपुत्रेभ्यः राजमानेभ्य ईश्वरेभ्यो वा मित्रादिभ्यः धृतं क्षरंतीः स्तुतिलक्षणाः वाचः वागिंद्रियेण करोमि (*ādityebhyaḥ rājabhyaḥ dhṛtasnūḥ giraḥ juhvā juhomi—āditiputrebhyaḥ rājamānebhya īśvarebhyo vā mitrādibhyaḥ dhṛtaṃ kṣaraṃtīḥ stutilakṣaṇāḥ vācaḥ vāgiṃdriyeṇa karomi*): one kind of meaning has been described thus: to the sons of Goddess Aditi, who shine with brilliance or are lords of all, Mitra and the rest, I offer, with the organ of speech, praises that pour nectar (ghee), as one makes an oblation with the ladle, the instrument of sacrifice.

Secondly: यद्वा जुहूस्थानीयेन धृतं क्षरंति वाग्रूपाणि हवींषि आदित्यानुद्दिश्य त्यजामि (*yadvā juhūsthānīyena dhṛtaṃ kṣaraṃti vāgrūpāṇi havīṃṣi ādityānuddiśya tyajāmi*): addressing the Ādityas, with the [supplied text breaks off here, at the end of the page, in the words "ಜೂಹೂ ಎಂಬ ಹೋಮ"] ladle called *juhū*, the sacrificial instrument of oblation

### Printed p. 71

*Running head: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 6, p. 71.*

(Special Topics of Ṛk 1, continued) …the ladle, I offer oblations in the form of speech that pour ghee, through the organ of speech, which is like a vessel for making the oblation. This is how it is explained. The point that the mantras offer ghee and other substances abundantly is supported by the sentence of the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa, यदृचोऽधीते पयसः कुल्या अस्य (*yadṛco 'dhīte payasaḥ kulyā asya*) (Tai. Brā. 2-10).

जुह्वा (*juhvā*)—वागिंद्रियेण (*vāgiṃdriyeṇa*)—वाज्ञामैतत् (*vājñāmaitat*) [sic, as supplied]: such is also Sāyaṇa's explanation. Yāska has read the word जिह्वा (*jihvā*) among the names of speech (Si. 2-23).

नः शृणोतु (*naḥ śṛṇotu*)—for this sentence Sāyaṇa explains अस्मदीयाः ताः गिरः मित्रादयः प्रत्येकं शृणोतु (*asmadīyāḥ tāḥ giraḥ mitrādayaḥ pratyekaṃ śṛṇotu*): may Mitra and the other Ādityas, each separately, all hear these our words of praise that we, the praisers, offer. He then quotes the sentence of the Taittirīya Āraṇyaka, "अष्टौ पुत्रासो अदितेः (*aṣṭau putrāso aditeḥ*) … मित्रश्च वरुणश्च धाताचार्यमाच अंशुश्च भगश्च इंद्रश्च विवस्वांश्चेत्येते (*mitraśca varuṇaśca dhātācāryamāca aṃśuśca bhagaśca iṃdraśca vivasvāṃścetyete*)" (Tai. Ā. 1-13-3), which says that the goddess Aditi has eight sons: Mitra, Varuṇa, Dhātā, Aryamā, Aṃśu, Bhaga, Indra and Vivasvān.

मित्रः (*mitraḥ*)—प्रमितेस्त्रायकः यद्वा सर्वेषां स्निग्धः (*pramitestrāyakaḥ yadvā sarveṣāṃ snigdhaḥ*): Sāyaṇa explains the word Mitra as one who protects living beings from dangers so that they do not die, or as one who is friendly towards living beings. Yāska explains the word Mitra as मित्रः प्रमितेस्त्रायते सम्मिन्वाने द्रवतीति वा वेदयतेर्वा (*mitraḥ pramitestrāyate samminvāne dravatīti vā vedayatervā*) (Si. 10-2).

अरी गच्छति नियच्छतीति आर्यमा (*arī gacchati niyacchatīti āryamā*)—Sāyaṇa states the explanation of the word Aryamā as one who restrains enemies. Yāska explains: अर्यमादित्यो अरीन्नियच्छति (*aryamādityo arīnniyacchati*) (Si. 11-23).

भगः भजनीयो देवः (*bhagaḥ bhajanīyo devaḥ*): Sāyaṇa's explanation is "the god who is worthy to be served". Yāska comments on the word Bhaga as भगो भजतेः (*bhago bhajateḥ*) (Ni. 1-7); he also read it among the names of wealth (Ni. 3-9) and explained the meaning as भगस्य भागधेयस्य धर्मस्य (*bhagasya bhāgadheyasya dharmasya*) (Ni. 9-31).

वरुणः तुविजातः (*varuṇaḥ tuvijātaḥ*)—अनुग्रहार्थं बहुषु देशेषु प्रादुर्भूतः पापनिवारणात् वरुणः (*anugrahārthaṃ bahuṣu deśeṣu prādurbhūtaḥ pāpanivāraṇāt varuṇaḥ*): Sāyaṇa explains that, since he has become manifest in many regions in order to protect living beings out of compassion and to remove their sins, he is called Varuṇa. Yāska explains it as वृणोतिति सतः (*vṛṇotiti sataḥ*) [sic, as supplied] (Ni. 10-5).

दक्षः अंशः (*dakṣaḥ aṃśaḥ*)—समर्थः (*samarthaḥ*): the word Dakṣa is given the meaning "able, possessed of power". It is explained as an epithet of the Āditya famed by the name Aṃśa. Thus these Mitra, Aryamā

### Printed p. 72

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 26 [as printed], p. 72.*

Varuṇa and Aṃśa are invoked to hear separately the hymns recited by us, the priests, and to show favour.

#### Ṛk 2

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> इ॒मं स्तोमं॒ सक्र॑तवो मे अ॒द्य मि॒त्रो अ॑र्य॒मा वरु॑णो जुषन्त ।
> आ॒दि॒त्यासः॒ शुच॑यो॒ धार॑पूता॒ अवृ॑जिना अनव॒द्या अरि॑स्वाः ॥ २ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> इमं । स्तोमं । सक्रतवः । मे । अद्य । मित्रः । अर्यमा । वरुणः । जुषन्त ।
> आदित्यासः । शुचयः । धार्पूताः । अवृजिनाः । अनवद्याः । अरिस्वाः ॥ २ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> पूर्वोक्त एव पशाविमं स्तोममित्येषा वपाया याज्या । सूत्रितं च ।
> इमं स्तोमं सक्रतवो मे अद्य तिस्रो भूमीर्धारयन् त्रीरुत द्यून् ।
> आ. ३ ८. । इति ॥
> इमं मेऽस्मदीयं स्तोमं स्तोत्रं सक्रतवः समानकर्माणः समानप्रजा
> वा मित्रादयोऽद्येदानीं जुषंत । जुषंतां सेवंतां । कीदृशास्ते । आदित्या-
> सोऽदितेः पुत्राः शुचयो दीप्यमानाः । धारपूता उदकधाराभिरिव पूताः ।
> निर्मला इत्यर्थः । अवृजिनाः । वृजी वर्जने । अवर्जितारः । सर्वानुग्राहका
> इत्यर्थः। अनवद्याः। अवद्येन गर्ह्येण पापेन रहिताः । अरिष्टाः केनाप्यहिंसिताः ॥

*Gist:* This Ṛk is the yājyā for the vapā (omentum) offering in the animal sacrifice mentioned above; may Mitra and the other Ādityas, pure and blameless, enjoy my praise today.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

आदित्यासः—the sons of Aditi; शुचयः—the shining ones; धारपूता—pure like a stream of water; अवृजिना—those who forsake (none of their devotees) but favour all; अनवद्या—free from sin; अरिष्टाः [sic: the Saṃhitā has अरिस्वाः]—not harmed by anyone; सक्रतवः—possessed of equal (rites or) wisdom; मित्रः—Mitra; अर्यमा—Aryaman; वरुणः—Varuṇa

### Printed p. 73

*Running head: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 6, p. 73.*

also; मे—recited by me; इमं स्तोमं—this praise; अद्य—at this time of sacrifice; जुषंत—let them enjoy.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

May the deities Mitra, Aryaman and Varuṇa—sons of Aditi, shining, pure like a stream of water, not forsaking any devotee but favouring all, free from sin, harmed by none, and possessed of equal rites and wisdom—enjoy at this time of sacrifice this praise recited by me. (May they hear it and be pleased.)

**Printed English translation**

May those of equal exploits, Mitra, Aryaman, Varuna, be pleased to-day by this my praise; they who are Adityas, luminous, purified by showers, who abandon none (that worship them), who are irreproachable, unassailable.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In the animal sacrifice spoken of earlier, this Ṛk, इमं स्तोमं (*imaṃ stomaṃ*), is to be recited as the yājyā mantra while the vapā oblation is made; this is supported by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, in the sūtra इमं स्तोमं सक्रतवो मे अद्य तिस्रो भूमीर्धारयन् त्रीरुत द्यून् (*imaṃ stomaṃ sakratavo me adya tisro bhūmīrdhārayan trīruta dyūn*) (Ā. 3-8).

सक्रतवः (*sakratavaḥ*)—समानकर्माणः समानप्रज्ञा वा मित्रादयः (*samānakarmāṇaḥ samānaprajñā vā mitrādayaḥ*): those having work of one kind, and possessed of knowledge of one manner—Mitra and the others—are described as hearing the praises of us, the priests, and being pleased.

These Ādityas are described as दीप्यमानाः (*dīpyamānāḥ*), brilliant; उदकधाराभिरिव पूताः (*udakadhārābhiriva pūtāḥ*), holy like streams of water; अवर्जितारः (*avarjitāraḥ*), bestowing favour on all; गर्ह्येन पापेन रहिताः (*garhyena pāpena rahitāḥ*), free from censured sins; and केनाप्यहिंसिताः (*kenāpyahiṃsitāḥ*), such that no one else can harm them. Thus their greatness has been made clear.

(A garbled line of the supplied text, "-~௨௪௬௧-", was removed.)

#### Ṛk 3

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> त आ॑दि॒त्यास॑ उ॒रवो॑ गभी॒रा अद॑ब्धास्तो॒ दिप्स॑नो भूर्य॒क्षाः ।
> अ॒न्तः प॑श्यंति वृजि॒नोत सा॒धु सर्वं॒ राज॑भ्यः पर॒मा चि॒दन्ति॑ ॥ ३ ॥

> *ta ādityāsa uravo gabhīrā adabdhāsto dipsano bhūryakṣāḥ |*
> *antaḥ paśyaṃti vṛjinota sādhu sarvaṃ rājabhyaḥ paramā cidanti ‖ 3 ‖*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> ते । आदित्यासः । उरवः । गभीराः । अदब्धासः । दिप्सन्तः । भूरि अक्षाः ।
> अन्तरिति । पश्यंति । वृजिना । उत । साधु । सर्वं । राजभ्यः । परमा । चित् । अन्ति ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> तस्मिन्नेव पशौ त आदित्यास इति हविषोऽनुवाक्या । सूत्रं तु पूर्वमेवो-
> दाहृतं ॥
> त आदित्यासोऽदितेः पुत्रा उरवो महांतो गभीरा गांभिर्योपेता

> दंभे । अस्मादिच्छासनि सनीवंतर्धेतीडभावपक्षे दंभ इच्चेतीकारः । दंभेर्हल्-
> ग्रहणस्य जातिवाचकत्वासिद्धमिति सनः कित्वेऽनुनासिकलोपः ॥ तथा भूर्यक्षाः ।

> सक्ष्यक्षोरिति षच् समासांतः ॥ एवंभूता आदित्या अंतर्मध्यॆ प्राणिनां हृदि
> प्रेरकतया वर्तमानाः संतो वृजिना वृजिनानि पापानि उत अपि च साधु साधूनि
> पुण्यानि च प्राणिभिः कृतानि पश्यंति । जानंति । परमा चित् परमाणि दूरदेशावस्थिता-

> *tasminneva paśau ta ādityāsa iti haviṣo 'nuvākyā | sūtraṃ tu pūrvamevo-*
> *dāhṛtaṃ ‖*
> *ta ādityāso 'diteḥ putrā uravo mahāṃto gabhīrā gāṃbhiryopetā*
> *adabbāsaḥ śatrubhirahiṃsitāḥ saṃto dipsaṃtaḥ śatrundaṃbhituṃ hiṃsitumicchaṃtaḥ ‖ daṃbhu*
> *daṃbhe | asmādicchāsani sanīvaṃtardhetīḍabhāvapakṣe daṃbha iccetīkāraḥ | daṃbherhal-*
> *grahaṇasya jātivācakatvāsiddhamiti sanaḥ kitve 'nunāsikalopaḥ ‖ tathā bhūryakṣāḥ |*
> *bhūrīṇi bahūnya kṣīṇi cakṣūṃṣi yeṣāṃ te tathoktāḥ | bahutejaso nā ‖ bahuvrīhau*
> *sakṣyakṣoriti ṣac samāsāṃtaḥ ‖ evaṃbhūtā ādityā aṃtarmadhyè prāṇināṃ hṛdi*
> *prerakatayā vartamānāḥ saṃto vṛjinā vṛjināni pāpāni uta api ca sādhu sādhūni*
> *puṇyāni ca prāṇibhiḥ kṛtāni paśyaṃti | jānaṃti | paramā cit paramāṇi dūradeśāvasthitā-*
> *nyapi sarvaṃ sarvāṇi dṛśyamānāni rājabhyo rājñāmīśvarāṇāmādityānāmaṃtyaṃtike*
> *samīpe vartata iti śeṣaḥ ‖ kādilopo bahulamityaṃtikaśabdasya kādilopaḥ ‖*

Gist: the Ādityas, dwelling within beings as inspirers, see their sins and merits; even far-off things are near to these lords.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

आदित्यासः (*ādityāsaḥ*): sons of Aditi; उरवः (*uravaḥ*): of great form; गभीराः (*gabhīrāḥ*): endowed with profundity; अदब्बासः (*adabbāsaḥ*) [as supplied]: not harmed by enemies; दिप्पंतः (*dippaṃtaḥ*) [as supplied]: wishing the destruction of enemies; भूर्रक्षाः (*bhūrrakṣāḥ*) [as supplied]: having many eyes or abundant radiance; ते (*te*): those Ādityas; अंतः (*aṃtaḥ*): dwelling within the heart (of beings); वृजिना (*vṛjinā*): (men's) sins; उत (*uta*): and; साधु (*sādhu*): meritorious acts; पश्यंति (*paśyaṃti*): know; परमा चित् (*paramā cit*): even when extremely far off; सर्वं (*sarvaṃ*): all things; राजभ्यः (*rājabhyaḥ*): to the lords, the Ādityas; अंति (*aṃti*): are as if very near.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

Those Ādityas, sons of Aditi, of great form, profound, unharmed by enemies, wishing the destruction of enemies, possessed of abundant radiance, dwelling within the hearts of beings, know the sins of men and also their meritorious acts. Even all things that are extremely far off remain as if very near to those Ādityas, the lords.

### Printed p. 75

**Printed English translation**

Those Adityas, mighty, profound, unsubdued, subduing, many eyed behold the innermost (thoughts of men), whether wicked or virtuous, whether far from or nigh to those royal (deities).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In the same animal sacrifice spoken of earlier, this Ṛk "ta ādityāsa" is to be used as the anuvākyā mantra when the oblation is offered; this is indicated in the sūtra cited earlier.

भूर्यक्षाः (*bhūryakṣāḥ*): बहूनि चक्षूंषि येषां ते बहुतेजसो वा (*bahūni cakṣūṃṣi yeṣāṃ te bahutejaso vā*). The Ādityas are described as seeing everything in many ways, as having many eyes, or as having abundant radiance.

अंतः (*aṃtaḥ*)—मध्यॆ प्राणिनां हृदि प्रेरकतया वर्तमानाः (*madhyè prāṇināṃ hṛdi prerakatayā vartamānāḥ*): dwelling in the hearts of beings in accordance with the acts they perform, and inspiring them; thus the Ādityas' quality of being inner controllers (antaryāmin) is described.

साधु पश्यंति (*sādhu paśyaṃti*)—प्राणिभिः कृतानि च पुण्यानि जानंति (*prāṇibhiḥ kṛtāni ca puṇyāni jānaṃti*): it is explained that they know all the merit that accords with the acts performed by the several beings.

परमा चित् सर्वं राजभ्यः अंति (*paramā cit sarvaṃ rājabhyaḥ aṃti*)—दूरदेशस्थितान्यपि सर्वाणि दृश्यमानानि ईश्वराणां आदित्यानां समीपे वर्तते (*dūradeśasthitānyapi sarvāṇi dṛśyamānāni īśvarāṇāṃ ādityānāṃ samīpe vartate*): all things situated in a distant place appear to the Ādityas, the lords of all, as if near; thus the keenness of their sight and the excellence of their knowledge are described.

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> दी॒र्घाधि॑यो॒ रक्ष॑माणा असु॒र्य॑मृ॒ता॑न॒ाश्चर्य॑माना ऋ॒णानि॑ ॥

> *dhārayanta ādityāso jagatsātha devā viśvasya bhuvanasya gopāḥ |*
> *dīrghādhiyo rakṣamāṇā asuryamṛtānāścaryamānā ṛṇāni ‖*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> धारयन्तः । आदित्यासः । जगत् । स्थाः । देवाः । विश्वस्य । भुवनस्य । गोपाः ।
> दीर्घधियः । रक्षमाणाः । असुर्यं । ऋतवानः । चयमानाः । ऋणानि ॥ ४ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> साग्निचित्ये क्रत् दीक्षणीयायामादित्ययागस्य धारयंत इत्यादिके द्वे
> याज्यानुवाक्ये । अत एव भुवद्वद्भुवनपतिविशेषणविशिष्टादित्ययागेऽपि । तथा च
> दीक्षणीयायामिति खंडे सूत्रितं । धारयंत आदित्यासो जगत्स्था इति द्वे एते एव
> भुवद्वद्भो भुवनपतिभ्यो ना । आ. ४. २. । इति ॥
> जगज्जंगमं स्थाः स्थावरं च ॥ तिष्ठतेः कर्तर्यसुन् ॥ एतदुभयविधं
> भूतजातं धारयंतोऽवस्थापयंतो देवा दानादिगुणयुक्ता विश्वस्य भुवनस्य
> सर्वस्य लोकस्य गोपा गोपयितारो रक्षका दीर्घाधियो दीर्घाणि धियः
> कर्माणि ज्ञानानि ना येषां तादृशाः ॥ अन्येषामपि दृश्यत इति पूर्वपदस्य
> दीर्घः ॥ असुर्य० । असवः प्राणाः । तेन च तद्देतुभूता आपो लक्ष्यंते । अपां
> तद्देतुत्वं च छांदोग्ये स्पष्टमाम्नातं । आपोमयः प्राणः । छां. उ. ६-५-४ ।
> इति । असुमुदकं ददातीत्यसुरो मेघः । तत्र भवं मेघांतर्वर्तमानमुदकं
> रक्षमाणाः । तत्तत्काले वृष्टुत्पादनाय रक्षंतः ॥ असुरशब्दाद्भवे छंदसीति यत् ॥
> ऋतावानः सत्यवंतो यज्ञवंतो वा ॥ छंदसीवनिपाविति मत्वर्थीयो वनिप् ।
> पूर्ववद्धीर्घः ॥ ऋणानि स्तोतृभिरन्येभ्यः प्रदेयानि चयमाना अपगमयंतः ॥

> शेषः ॥

> *sāgnicitye krat dīkṣaṇīyāyāmādityayāgasya dhārayaṃta ityādike dve*
