> *jagajjaṃgamaṃ sthāḥ sthāvaraṃ ca ‖ tiṣṭhateḥ kartaryasun ‖ etadubhayavidhaṃ*
> *bhūtajātaṃ dhārayaṃto 'vasthāpayaṃto devā dānādiguṇayuktā viśvasya bhuvanasya*
> *sarvasya lokasya gopā gopayitāro rakṣakā dīrghādhiyo dīrghāṇi dhiyaḥ*
> *karmāṇi jñānāni nā yeṣāṃ tādṛśāḥ ‖ anyeṣāmapi dṛśyata iti pūrvapadasya*
> *dīrghaḥ ‖ asurya0 | asavaḥ prāṇāḥ | tena ca taddetubhūtā āpo lakṣyaṃte | apāṃ*
> *taddetutvaṃ ca chāṃdogye spaṣṭamāmnātaṃ | āpomayaḥ prāṇaḥ | chāṃ. u. 6-5-4 |*
> *iti | asumudakaṃ dadātītyasuro meghaḥ | tatra bhavaṃ meghāṃtarvartamānamudakaṃ*
> *rakṣamāṇāḥ | tattatkāle vṛṣṭutpādanāya rakṣaṃtaḥ ‖ asuraśabdādbhave chaṃdasīti yat ‖*
> *ṛtāvānaḥ satyavaṃto yajñavaṃto vā ‖ chaṃdasīvanipāviti matvarthīyo vanip |*
> *pūrvavaddhīrghaḥ ‖ ṛṇāni stotṛbhiranyebhyaḥ pradeyāni cayamānā apagamayaṃtaḥ ‖*
> *caya gatau | bhauvādiko 'nudāttet ‖ evaṃguṇaviśiṣṭānugrahā vā asmadadhīnā bhavaṃtviti*
> *śeṣaḥ ‖*

Gist: in the Agnicayana dīkṣaṇīyā, "dhārayanta" etc. are two yājyā-anuvākyā verses of the Āditya offering; the Ādityas sustain the moving and the fixed, guard the world, and keep the waters of the cloud.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

जगत् (*jagat*): moving beings; स्थाः (*sthāḥ*): fixed things; धारयंतः (*dhārayaṃtaḥ*): establishing and upholding; देवाः (*devāḥ*): possessed of the qualities of generosity etc.; विश्वस्य भुवनस्य (*viśvasya bhuvanasya*): of the whole world; गोपाः (*gopāḥ*): protectors; दीर्घधियः (*dīrghadhiyaḥ*): of far-reaching understanding; असुरं (*asuraṃ*) [as supplied]: the water residing in the clouds; रक्षमाणाः (*rakṣamāṇāḥ*): collecting and guarding it in due season; ऋतावानः (*ṛtāvānaḥ*): truthful; ऋणानि (*ṛṇāni*): the debts (which the praisers owe to others); चयमानाः (*cayamānāḥ*): discharging; आदित्यासः (*ādityāsaḥ*): the Ādityas (may favour us).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The Ādityas, who establish and uphold the moving beings and the fixed things, who are possessed of generosity and other qualities, protectors of the whole world, of far-reaching understanding, guarding the water residing in the clouds [text of the page ends here; the sentence continues in the next window]

### Printed p. 77

(Bhāvārtha of Ṛk 4, continued) …who collect it and guard it at the proper time, truthful, and discharging the debts that the praisers owe to others — may such Ādityas favour us.

**Printed English translation**

The divine Adityas are the upholders (of all things). moveable or immoveble [sic]; the protectors of the universe: the provident in acts; the collectors of rain; the possessors of veracity; the acquitters of our debts.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In the dīkṣaṇīyā rite when the Agnicayana is performed, in the Āditya offering, the Ṛk "dhārayanta ādityāsaḥ" धारयंत आदित्यासः (*dhārayaṃta ādityāsaḥ*) and the Ṛk following it are to be used as the yājyā and puro'nuvākyā mantras; and these Ṛks are also applied to the Āditya deities qualified as "bhuvadvat" and "bhuvanapati". This is concluded from the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, in the section beginning "tathā ca dīkṣaṇīyāyām", by the sūtra "dhārayanta ādityāso jagatsthā iti dve ete eva bhuvadvadbhyo bhuvanapatibhyo vā" धारयंत आदित्यासो जगत्सा इति द्वे एते एव भुवद्वद्भ्यो भुवनपतिभ्यो वा (*dhārayaṃta ādityāso jagatsā iti dve ete eva bhuvadvadbhyo bhuvanapatibhyo vā*) (Āś. 4-2 as printed).

जगत् स्थाःधारयंत— स्थावरं जंगमं च उभयविधं भूतजातं अवस्थापयंतः (*jagat sthāḥdhārayaṃta— sthāvaraṃ jaṃgamaṃ ca ubhayavidhaṃ bhūtajātaṃ avasthāpayaṃtaḥ*): it is described that the Ādityas have placed the two kinds of beings — the fixed, in the form of trees, creepers and so on, and the moving, in the form of animals, beasts, birds, men and the rest — each in accordance with its fitness; and the Ādityas are explained as देवाः—दानादिगुणयुक्ताः (*devāḥ—dānādiguṇayuktāḥ*), those endowed with qualities such as generosity.

दीर्घाधियः—धीर्घाणि धियः कर्माणि ज्ञानानि ना येषां (*dīrghādhiyaḥ—dhīrghāṇi dhiyaḥ karmāṇi jñānāni nā yeṣāṃ*): the Ādityas are described as those who perform abundant acts and are possessed of powerful knowledge.

असुरं रक्षमाणाः— असवः प्राणाः । तेन च तद्धेतुभूताः आपः लक्ष्यंते । असुमुदकं ददातीत्यसुरो मेघः । तत्र भवं मेघांतर्वतिनमुदकं तत्तत्काले वृष्ट्यत्पादनाय रक्षंतः (*asuraṃ rakṣamāṇāḥ— asavaḥ prāṇāḥ | tena ca taddhetubhūtāḥ āpaḥ lakṣyaṃte | asumudakaṃ dadātītyasuro meghaḥ | tatra bhavaṃ meghāṃtarvatinamudakaṃ tattatkāle vṛṣṭyatpādanāya rakṣaṃtaḥ*): here the meaning of the word *asu* is given as breath (life); from this the waters, which are the cause of breath, are meant. Because clouds give water to the earth, they are denoted by the word *asura*. It is described that the Ādityas guard, in the proper seasons, the water in the clouds so as to give it to the earth in the form of rain. And, अपां तद्देतुत्वं च आपोमयः प्राणः (*apāṃ taddetutvaṃ ca āpomayaḥ prāṇaḥ*) (Chāṃ. U. 6-5-4): explaining that the waters are the cause of breath, a sentence of the Chāndogya Upaniṣad is also cited.

ऋतावानः—सत्यवंतो यज्ञवंतो वा (*ṛtāvānaḥ—satyavaṃto yajñavaṃto vā*): the Ādityas are those who act by truth alone, or who perform sacrifice; ऋणानि चायमानाः—स्तोतृभिः अन्येभ्यः (*ṛṇāni cāyamānāḥ—stotṛbhiḥ anyebhyaḥ*) [the text of the page ends here; the sentence continues on p. 78]

### Printed p. 78

(Special Topics of Ṛk 4, continued) प्रदेयानि अपगमयंतः (*pradeyāni apagamayaṃtaḥ*) — it is described that the priests who praise them will have the debts they owe to others discharged.

#### Ṛk 5

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> वि॒द्यामा॑दित्या॒ अव॑सो वो अ॒स्य यद॑र्यमन्भ॒य आ चि॑न्मयो॒भु ।
> यु॒ष्माकं॑ मित्रावरुणा॒ प्रणी॑तौ॒ परि॒ श्वभ्रे॑व दुरि॒तानि॑ वृज्यां ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> विद्यां । आदित्याः । अवसः । वः । अस्य । यत् । अर्यमन् । भये । आ । चित् ।
> मयःभु ।
> युष्माकं । मित्रावरुणा । प्रनीत् । परि । श्वभ्रा इव । दुःइतानि । वृज्यां ॥ ५ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे आदित्याः अदितेः पुत्रा वो युष्माकमस्यावसः ॥ कर्मणि षष्ठी ॥
> इदं रक्षणं विद्यां । लभेय । भये राक्षसादिभ्य अगते सति यद्युष्मदीयं रक्षणं ।
> चिच्छब्दश्चार्थे । मयोभु मयसः सुखस्य भावयित्रु च तदितं लभेयेत्यन्वयः ।
> हे अर्यमन् हे मित्रावरुणा युष्माकं प्रणीतावस्मद्विषये प्रकृष्टे नयने सति
> श्वभ्रेव श्वभ्राणीव स्थातुमयोग्यानि गर्तवंति स्थलानीव दुरितानि पापानि परिवृज्यां ।
> परित्यजेयं ॥ वृजी वर्जने । बहुलं छंदसीति विकरणस्य लुक् ॥ ६ ॥

> *he ādityāḥ aditeḥ putrā vo yuṣmākamasyāvasaḥ ‖ karmaṇi ṣaṣṭhī ‖*
> *idaṃ rakṣaṇaṃ vidyāṃ | labheya | bhaye rākṣasādibhya agate sati yadyuṣmadīyaṃ rakṣaṇaṃ |*
> *cicchabdaścārthe | mayobhu mayasaḥ sukhasya bhāvayitru ca taditaṃ labheyetyanvayaḥ |*
> *he aryaman he mitrāvaruṇā yuṣmākaṃ praṇītāvasmadviṣaye prakṛṣṭe nayane sati*
> *śvabhreva śvabhrāṇīva sthātumayogyāni gartavaṃti sthalānīva duritāni pāpāni parivṛjyāṃ |*
> *parityajeyaṃ ‖ vṛjī varjane | bahulaṃ chaṃdasīti vikaraṇasya luk ‖ 6 ‖*

Gist: the Ādityas, sons of Aditi, are asked for the protection that brings happiness in time of fear from Rākṣasas etc.; under Aryaman's and Mitra-Varuṇa's guidance may I avoid sins as one avoids pits.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Ādityas: O Ādityas. Which protection of yours, when fear has come (from Rākṣasas etc.), removes that fear and produces happiness, that protection of yours — may I obtain it. Aryaman: O Aryaman. Mitra-Varuṇa: O Mitra and Varuṇa. When your excellent guidance exists, may I cross over sins [दुरिशानि (*duriśāni*) [?], as supplied] as one crosses a dangerous pit.

### Printed p. 79

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Ādityas, may we obtain that protection of yours which, even when fear has come from Rākṣasas and others, removes that fear and brings happiness. O Aryaman [अरम (*arama*) [?], as supplied] and Mitra-Varuṇa, under your excellent guidance may I cross over sins as one crosses a dangerous pit.

**Printed English translation**

May I be conscious, Adityas, of this your protection, the cause of happiness (and security) in danger; Aryaman. Mitra, and Varuna, May I, through your guidance, escape the sins which are like pitfalls (in my path).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this Ṛk the sacrificer prays for two kinds of protection performed by the Ādityas. First, वः अस्य अवसः विद्यां लभेय (*vaḥ asya avasaḥ vidyāṃ labheya*): may the protection given by the Ādityas be knowledge-giving. And भये मयोभु—भये राक्षसादिभ्य आगते सति सुखस्य च भावयितृ (*bhaye mayobhu—bhaye rākṣasādibhya āgate sati sukhasya ca bhāvayitṛ*): when fear caused by Rākṣasas and the like has come upon the sacrificer, may the protection given by the Ādityas produce happiness. This is the second wish described.

Addressing Aryaman and Mitra-Varuṇa: युष्माकं प्रणीतौ श्वभ्रे दुरिशानि [?] परिवृज्यां—युष्माकं प्रकृष्णनयने सति स्थातुमयोग्यानि गर्तवंति स्कूलानीव पापानि परित्यजेयं (*yuṣmākaṃ praṇītau śvabhre duriśāni [?] parivṛjyāṃ—yuṣmākaṃ prakṛṣṇanayane sati sthātumayogyāni gartavaṃti skūlānīva pāpāni parityajeyaṃ*): the wish is expressed that, while these two protect the sacrificer, he may reach the state of getting rid of the abundant sins which bring misfortune, as one falls into big pits and has no chance to stand firm. A comparison of sins with deep pits is stated, and it is explained that protection from them is of the nature of release.

The word मयोभु (*mayobhu*) is used in the sense "producer of happiness" (सुखस्य भावयितृ (*sukhasya bhāvayitṛ*)), since Yāska has stated मयः (*mayaḥ*) among the words for happiness (Ni. 3-13).

### Printed p. 80

#### Ṛk 6

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> सु॒गो हि वो॑ अर्यमन्मित्र॒ पन्था॑ अनृक्ष॒रो व॑रुण सा॒धुरस्ति॑ ।
> तेना॑दित्या॒ अधि॑ वोचता नो॒ यच्छ॑ता नो दुष्परि॒हन्नु॒ शर्म॑ ॥६॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> सुगः । हि । वः । अर्यमन् । मित्र । पन्थाः । अन्वृक्षरः । वरुण । साधुः । अस्ति ।
> तेन । आदित्याः । अधि । वोचत । नः । यच्छत । नः । दुःपरिहन्नु । शर्म ॥ ६ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे आर्यवान् मित्र वरुण वो युष्माकॆं सुगः सुखेन गंतव्योऽनृक्षरः ।
> ऋक्षरः कंटक ऋच्छतेरिति । तद्रहितः साधुः साधकः शोभनो वा । एवंगुणविशिष्टः
> पंथा मार्गोऽस्ति । विद्यते खलु । आतस्तेन पथास्मान् शोभनं नयतेति शेषः ।
> हे आदित्या नोऽस्माकमधिवोचत । अधिवचनं पक्षपातेन वचनं कुरुत । अपि च
> नोऽस्मभ्यं दुष्परिहंतु परिहंतुं विनाशयितुमशक्यं शर्म सुखं यच्छत । दत्त ॥
> दाण् दाने ! शपि प्राघ्रेत्यादिना यच्छादेशः ॥

> *he āryavān mitra varuṇa vo yuṣmākèṃ sugaḥ sukhena gaṃtavyo 'nṛkṣaraḥ |*
> *ṛkṣaraḥ kaṃṭaka ṛcchateriti | tadrahitaḥ sādhuḥ sādhakaḥ śobhano vā | evaṃguṇaviśiṣṭaḥ*
> *paṃthā mārgo 'sti | vidyate khalu | ātastena pathāsmān śobhanaṃ nayateti śeṣaḥ |*
> *he ādityā no 'smākamadhivocata | adhivacanaṃ pakṣapātena vacanaṃ kuruta | api ca*
> *no 'smabhyaṃ duṣparihaṃtu parihaṃtuṃ vināśayitumaśakyaṃ śarma sukhaṃ yacchata | datta ‖*
> *dāṇ dāne ! śapi prāghretyādinā yacchādeśaḥ ‖*

Gist: the path of Aryaman, Mitra and Varuṇa is easy and free of thorns; the Ādityas are asked to lead us by it, to speak kindly to us and to grant undisturbed happiness.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Aryaman: O Aryaman. Mitra: O god Mitra. Varuṇa: O Varuṇa. Your path: easy to travel (सुगः), free of thorns (अनृक्षरः), and auspicious (साधुः). Pantha: as the path. Asti hi: it is well known to exist. Tena: by that same path (lead us). Ādityāḥ: O Ādityas. Naḥ: with us. Adhi vocata: speak lovingly. Naḥ: to us. Duṣparihantu: that which cannot be destroyed. Śamaṃ [?]: happiness. Yacchata: give.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Aryaman, Mitra and Varuṇa, it is well known that your path is easy to travel, free of thorns and auspicious. Lead us by that same path. O Ādityas, speak lovingly with us. Give us happiness that enemies cannot destroy.

### Printed p. 81

**Printed English translation**

Aryaman, Mitra, and Varuna, easy is the path (you show us), and free from thorns, and pleasant: therefore, Adityas, (lead us) by (it): speak to us favouably [sic], and grant us happiness difficult to be disturbed.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

Addressing Aryaman, Mitra and Varuṇa, the wish is described that the sacrificer may enter upon a straight, thornless, pleasant path. अनृक्षरः साधुः पंथाः तेन (*anṛkṣaraḥ sādhuḥ paṃthāḥ tena*) — कंटकरहितः साधकः शोभनः मार्गः तेन शोभनं नयत (*kaṃṭakarahitaḥ sādhakaḥ śobhanaḥ mārgaḥ tena śobhanaṃ nayata*). The word अनृक्षरः is explained as ऋक्षरः कंटकः ऋच्छतेः (*ṛkṣaraḥ kaṃṭakaḥ ṛcchateḥ*) (Ni. 9-38); for this word the meaning is given as a path free from both thorns and fear.

आदित्याः नः अधिवोचत—अस्माकं पक्षपातेन वचनं कुरुत (*ādityāḥ naḥ adhivocata—asmākaṃ pakṣapātena vacanaṃ kuruta*): addressing the Ādityas, the wish is expressed that they speak to the sacrificers with confidence (affection). And दुष्परिहंतु शर्म यच्छत (*duṣparihaṃtu śarma yacchata*): the sacrificer's prayer is explained thus, that the Ādityas should bestow happiness that is always without break and does not meet destruction.

#### Ṛk 7

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> पिप॑र्तु नो॒ अदि॑ती॒ राज॑पु॒त्राति॒ द्वेषां॑स्यर्य॒मा सु॒गेभिः॑ ।
> बृ॒हन्मि॒त्रस्य॒ वरु॑णस्य॒ शर्मोप॑ स्याम पुरु॒वीरा॒ अरि॑ष्टाः ॥ ७ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> पिपर्तु । नः । अदितिः । राजपुत्रा । अति । द्वेषांसि । आर्यमा । सुगेभिः ।
> बृहत् । मित्रस्य । वरुणस्य । शर्म । उप । स्याम । पुरुवीराः । अरिष्टाः ॥ ७ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> राजपुत्रा राजानो राजमाना मित्रादयः पुत्रा यस्यास्तादृश्यदितिरदीना
> अखंडनीया वा देवी नोऽस्मान् द्वेषांसि द्वेष्टणि रक्षांस्यति पिपर्तु । अति

> *rājaputrā rājāno rājamānā mitrādayaḥ putrā yasyāstādṛśyaditiradīnā*
> *akhaṃḍanīyā vā devī no 'smān dveṣāṃsi dveṣṭaṇi rakṣāṃsyati pipartu | ati*

### Printed p. 82

> पारयतु । अतिक्रम्य तद्रहितं देशं प्रापयत्वित्यर्थः । आर्यमा च सुगेभिः सुष्टु
> गंतवैर्मार्गैरस्मान्सारयतु । अपि च वयिं पुरुवीरा बहुभिर्वीरैः पुत्रादिभिरु .
> पेता अरिष्टाः केनाप्यहिंसिताश्च संतो मित्रस्य वरुणस्य च बृहत्परिवृढं शर्म
> सुखमुपस्याम । उपगता भवेम ॥

> *pārayatu | atikramya tadrahitaṃ deśaṃ prāpayatvityarthaḥ | āryamā ca sugebhiḥ suṣṭu*
> *gaṃtavairmārgairasmānsārayatu | api ca vayiṃ puruvīrā bahubhirvīraiḥ putrādibhiru .*
> *petā ariṣṭāḥ kenāpyahiṃsitāśca saṃto mitrasya varuṇasya ca bṛhatparivṛḍhaṃ śarma*
> *sukhamupasyāma | upagatā bhavema ‖*

Gist: Aditi, whose sons are the shining Mitra and others, is asked to carry us across enemies' hatred; Aryaman to lead us by good paths; and we to attain the great happiness of Mitra and Varuṇa.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Rājaputrā: she whose sons are the shining Mitra and others. Aditi: the indivisible divine mother. Naḥ: us. Dveṣāṃsi: from the hatred of enemies. Ati pipartu: carry across (to a place free of hatred). Aryamā: Aryaman also. Sugebhiḥ: by excellent, easily travelled paths (lead us). (Vayam: we also.) Puruvīrāḥ: accompanied by many heroic sons. Ariṣṭāḥ: unharmed by anyone. Mitrasya: belonging to Mitra. Varuṇasya: belonging to Varuṇa. Bṛhat: very great. Śarma: happiness. Upa syāma: may we attain.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

May the divine mother, whose sons are the shining Mitra and other gods and who is indivisible, carry us across the hatred of enemies to a place free of such hatred. May Aryaman [अरमन (*aramana*) [?], as supplied] also lead us by excellent, easily travelled paths. May we too, accompanied by many heroic sons and unharmed by anyone, attain the great happiness belonging to Mitra and Varuṇa.

**Printed English translation**

May Aditi, the mother of royal sons, place us beyond the malice (of our enemies): may Aryaman lead us by easy paths, and may we, blessed with many descendants, and safe from harm, attain the great happiness of Mitra and Varuna.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

राजपुत्रा अदितिः—राजमानाः मित्रादयः पुत्राः यस्याः अखंडनीया या देवी (*rājaputrā aditiḥ—rājamānāḥ mitrādayaḥ putrāḥ yasyāḥ akhaṃḍanīyā yā devī*). The abundant power of Aditi is described: the goddess Aditi has sons who shine with radiance. The word Aditi is stated by Yāska among the names of the earth (Ni. 2-5), of speech (Ni. 2-23), of cows (Ni. 3-9), and of heaven-and-earth (Ni. 3-22). And

### Printed p. 83

Aditi is explained as अदितिः देवमाता अदीना (*aditiḥ devamātā adīnā*) ("Aditi, the divine mother, the undiminished") (Ni. 4-23); and, citing the Ṛk आदितिर्दौरदितिरंतरिक्षं (*āditirdauraditiraṃtarikṣaṃ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-89-10), it is described that Aditi's power is unbroken. The prayer is stated that this goddess may carry us, the sacrificers, beyond the demons (द्वेषांसि अति पिपर्तु (*dveṣāṃsi ati pipartu*)) and bring us to a country free of them, that is, make us dwell in regions where there is no affliction from rākṣasas.

By आर्यमा सुगेभिः (*āryamā sugebhiḥ*) the sacrificers pray for travel on a pleasant road, and for protection, from Aryaman. And by मित्रस्य वरुणस्य च बृहत् शर्म (*mitrasya varuṇasya ca bṛhat śarma*) it is wished that abundant happiness may come from Mitra and Varuṇa.

#### Ṛk 8

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> ति॒स्रो भूमी॑र्धारय॒न् त्रीरु॒त द्यून्त्रिणि॑ व्र॒ता वि॒दथे॑ अ॒न्तरे॑षां ।
> ऋ॒तेना॑दित्या॒ महि॑ वो महि॒त्वं तद॑र्यमन्वरुण मित्र॒ चारु॑ ॥ ८ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> तिस्रः । भूमिाः । धारयन् । त्रीन् । उत । द्यून् । त्रीणि । व्रता । विदथे । अंतः । एषां ।
> यतेन । आदित्याः । महि । वः । महित्वं । तत् । अर्यमन् । वरुण । मित्र । चारु ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> पूर्वोक्ते पशौ तिस्रो भूमीरिति पुरोडाशस्य याज्या । सूत्रं पूर्वमे-
> वोदाहृतं ॥
> तिस्रो भूमिाः । अत्र भूमिाशब्दो लोकत्रये वर्तते । यो द्वितीयस्यां
> तृतीयस्यां पृथिव्यामित्यत्र यथा पृथिवीशब्दः । भूम्यंतरिक्षस्वर्गांस्त्रीन् लोकान्
> आदित्या धारयन् । वृष्टिप्रदानादिना धारयंति । उत आपि च द्यून् दीप्तान् तत
> उपरितनान् महरादिकानपि त्रीन् लोकान् धारयंति । यद्वा दीप्तान् अग्निं
> वायुं सूर्यं च धारयंति । अपि चैषामादित्यानां विदथे यज्ञे०तर्मध्यॆ
> श्रीणि व्रता सवनत्रयनिष्पाद्यानि श्रीणि कर्माणि संति । यद्वा एषां लोकानामंत-
> र्मध्यॆ विदथे यज्ञे निमित्तभूते सति एषामादित्यानां त्रीणि कर्माणि रसादानधा-

> *pūrvokte paśau tisro bhūmīriti puroḍāśasya yājyā | sūtraṃ pūrvame-*
> *vodāhṛtaṃ ‖*
> *tisro bhūmiāḥ | atra bhūmiāśabdo lokatraye vartate | yo dvitīyasyāṃ*
> *tṛtīyasyāṃ pṛthivyāmityatra yathā pṛthivīśabdaḥ | bhūmyaṃtarikṣasvargāṃstrīn lokān*
> *ādityā dhārayan | vṛṣṭipradānādinā dhārayaṃti | uta āpi ca dyūn dīptān tata*
> *uparitanān maharādikānapi trīn lokān dhārayaṃti | yadvā dīptān agniṃ*
> *vāyuṃ sūryaṃ ca dhārayaṃti | api caiṣāmādityānāṃ vidathe yajñe0tarmadhyè*
> *śrīṇi vratā savanatrayaniṣpādyāni śrīṇi karmāṇi saṃti | yadvā eṣāṃ lokānāmaṃta-*
> *rmadhyè vidathe yajñe nimittabhūte sati eṣāmādityānāṃ trīṇi karmāṇi rasādānadhā-*

### Printed p. 84

> रणविसर्जनलक्षणानि संति । उत्तरोर्धर्चः प्रत्यक्षकृतः । हे आदित्या ऋतेन सत्येन
> यॆज्ञेन वा महि महत् प्रभूतं वो युष्माकं महित्वं महत्त्वं यन्महाभाग्यं
> हे अर्यमन् वरुण मित्र तन्महाभाग्यं चारु । शोभनं सर्वोत्कृष्टमित्यर्थः ॥

> *raṇavisarjanalakṣaṇāni saṃti | uttarordharcaḥ pratyakṣakṛtaḥ | he ādityā ṛtena satyena*
> *yèjñena vā mahi mahat prabhūtaṃ vo yuṣmākaṃ mahitvaṃ mahattvaṃ yanmahābhāgyaṃ*
> *he aryaman varuṇa mitra tanmahābhāgyaṃ cāru | śobhanaṃ sarvotkṛṣṭamityarthaḥ ‖*

Gist: the three worlds are the earth, atmosphere and heaven (or, alternatively, the three higher worlds, or Agni, Vāyu and Sūrya); the Ādityas sustain them, have three rites in the sacrifice, and their great might through truth or sacrifice is most excellent.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

तिस्रः भूमिाः (*tisraḥ bhūmiāḥ*): the three worlds beginning with the earth. धारयन् (*dhārayan*): (the Ādityas) have upheld. उत (*uta*): and. द्यून् (*dyūn*): the shining. त्रीन् (*trīn*): they have upheld also the three worlds beginning with Mahar. एषां (*eṣāṃ*): of these Ādityas. विदथे (*vidathe*): in the sacrifice, or, in the middle of the three worlds, occasioned by sacrifice. अंतः (*aṃtaḥ*): in the middle. त्रीणि व्रता (*trīṇi vratā*): three kinds of rites (accomplished through the three savanas) are well known (or: three kinds of rites, namely drinking up the water, holding it, and releasing it in the form of rain, are well known). आदित्याः (*ādityāḥ*): O Ādityas! वः (*vaḥ*): your. महित्वं (*mahitvaṃ*): greatness. ऋतेन (*ṛtena*): through truth or through sacrifice. महि (*mahi*): has grown in great form. अर्यमन् (*aryaman*): O Aryaman. वरुण (*varuṇa*): Varuṇa. मित्र (*mitra*): O Mitra. तत् (*tat*): that greatness of yours. चारु (*cāru*): is beautiful and the most excellent of all.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The Ādityas have upheld the three worlds beginning with the earth, and the three upper worlds beginning with Mahar. In the middle of the three worlds that belong to these Ādityas, three rites occasioned by sacrifice, that is, drinking up the water, holding it, and releasing it as rain, are well known. O Ādityas, your greatness [माहात्म (*māhātma*), as supplied] has grown great through truth. O Aryaman [अय्यमने (*ayyamane*), as supplied], Varuṇa, that greatness of yours is beautiful and the most excellent of all.

**Printed English translation**

They uphold the three worlds, the three heavens; and in their sacrifices three ceremonies (are comprised): by truth, Adityas, has your great might (been produced), such as is most excellent; Aryaman, Mitra, and Varuna.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In the matter stated before, it is explained in the Stotra already cited that this Ṛk तिस्रो भूमिाः (*tisro bhūmiāḥ*) is to be used as the yājyā mantra when the purodāśa oblation is offered. तिस्रो भूमिाः धारयन् (*tisro bhūmiāḥ dhārayan*): the meaning is explained thus: as in the mantra यो द्वितीयस्यां तृतीयस्यां पृथिव्यां (*yo dvitīyasyāṃ tṛtīyasyāṃ pṛthivyāṃ*) the word pṛthivī is said to mean the three worlds, so here too the word bhūmi is used in the sense of the three worlds.

### Printed p. 85

From this, भूम्यंतरिक्षस्वर्गस्थान् त्रीन् लोकान् आदित्याः वृष्टिप्रदानादिना धारयंति (*bhūmyaṃtarikṣasvargasthān trīn lokān ādityāḥ vṛṣṭipradānādinā dhārayaṃti*): the Ādityas uphold, that is, protect, the three worlds earth, atmosphere and heaven, and those who live in the three worlds, by granting rain, water and the like; this is how the meaning is described. And for the words द्यून् त्री९ (*dyūn trī9*) [as supplied] the meaning given with तत उपरितनान् महारादिर्का अपि (*tata uparitanān mahārādirkā api*) is: the three worlds beginning with Mahar, which lie above these three worlds; and one sense is described, that the Ādityas have upheld these. Then, with यद्वा दीर्प्ता अग्निं वायुं सूर्यं च धारयंति (*yadvā dīrptā agniṃ vāyuṃ sūryaṃ ca dhārayaṃti*), it is described that the Ādityas uphold also the shining Agni, Vāyu and Sūrya.

एषां विदथे अंतः व्रता त्रीणि संति (*eṣāṃ vidathe aṃtaḥ vratā trīṇi saṃti*), आदित्यानां एषां यज्ञ मध्यॆ सवनत्रय निष्पाद्यानि कर्माणि संति (*ādityānāṃ eṣāṃ yajña madhyè savanatraya niṣpādyāni karmāṇi saṃti*): the first meaning is explained thus: in the middle of the sacrifices performed in honour of these Ādityas, the duties (to be performed) in the rites of the morning, midday and evening savanas are also carried out. Secondly, यद्वा एषां लोकानां अंतर्मध्यॆ यज्ञे निमित्तभूते सति एषामादित्यानां रसादानधारण-विसर्जनलक्षणानि त्रीणि कर्माणि संति (*yadvā eṣāṃ lokānāṃ aṃtarmadhyè yajñe nimittabhūte sati eṣāmādityānāṃ rasādānadhāraṇa-visarjanalakṣaṇāni trīṇi karmāṇi saṃti*): the meaning is explained that, in the middle of the three worlds heaven, earth and atmosphere, at the time when sacrifice is performed, these Ādityas carry out three kinds of rites, receiving the waters, holding the waters received, and pouring them on the earth in the form of rain, and thereby protect sacrifices and other rites.

Addressing the Ādityas as हॆ अदित्याः आर्यमन्, मित्र, वरुण (*hè adityāḥ āryaman, mitra, varuṇa*) [as supplied], ऋतेन यज्ञेन सत्येन पा युष्माकं प्रभूतं महाभाग्यं चारु (*ṛtena yajñena satyena pā yuṣmākaṃ prabhūtaṃ mahābhāgyaṃ cāru*): it is described that their abundant glory, full of greatness, surpasses everything because it protects the sacrifice and truth. The meanings of the words Āryamā, Mitra and Varuṇa have been explained before. For the word ṛta the meanings truth and sacrifice are given by Yāska: सत्यं वा यज्ञं वा (*satyaṃ vā yajñaṃ vā*) (Ni. 4-19).

### Printed p. 86

#### Ṛk 9

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> श्री रो॑च॒ना दि॒व्या धा॑रयन्त हिर॒ण्ययाः॒ शुच॑यो॒ धार॑पूताः ।
> अस्व॑प्नजो अनिमि॒षा अद॑ब्धा उरु॒शंसा॑ ऋ॒जवे॒ मर्त्या॑य ॥ ९ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> श्री । रोचना । दिव्या । धारयन्त । हिरण्ययाः । शुचयः । धार्पूताः ।
> अस्वप्नजः । अनिमिषाः । अदब्धाः । उरुशंसाः । यजवे । मर्त्याय ॥ ९ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> श्री रोचना त्रीणि रोचनानि रोचमानाग्न्यादीनि तेजांसि दिव्या दिव्यानि
> दिविभवानि धारयंत । आदित्या अवास्थापयन् । किमर्थं । ऋजवे ऋजीकाय
> स्तोत्राणां मर्त्याय मनुष्याय भोगसिद्ध्यर्थमिति यावत् । कीदृशा आदित्याः ।
> हिरण्ययाः । हिरण्मया हिरण्यालंकृतसर्वांगाः । यद्वा हितरमणीयवेषाः ।
> शुचयो दीप्यमानाः । धारपूता उदकधारया क्षालिता इव निर्मलाः । अस्वप्न-
> जोऽसंजातस्वप्नाः ॥ सुपां सुलुगिति जसः सुः ॥ अपर आह । अस्वप्नजोऽस्वपंतो
> विगतनिद्राः ॥ स्वपितृषोर्नजिज्ञति नजिज् प्रत्यय इति तदेतत्पदकारस्यानभिमतं ॥
> अनिमिषा निमेषरहिताः । अदब्धा असुरादिभिरहिंसिताः । उरुशंसा उरुभिर्बहुभिः
> शंसनीयाः स्तोतव्याः । एवंगुणविशिष्टा आधारयंतेत्यन्वयः ॥

> *śrī rocanā trīṇi rocanāni rocamānāgnyādīni tejāṃsi divyā divyāni*
> *divibhavāni dhārayaṃta | ādityā avāsthāpayan | kimarthaṃ | ṛjave ṛjīkāya*
> *stotrāṇāṃ martyāya manuṣyāya bhogasiddhyarthamiti yāvat | kīdṛśā ādityāḥ |*
> *hiraṇyayāḥ | hiraṇmayā hiraṇyālaṃkṛtasarvāṃgāḥ | yadvā hitaramaṇīyaveṣāḥ |*
> *śucayo dīpyamānāḥ | dhārapūtā udakadhārayā kṣālitā iva nirmalāḥ | asvapna-*
> *jo 'saṃjātasvapnāḥ ‖ supāṃ sulugiti jasaḥ suḥ ‖ apara āha | asvapnajo 'svapaṃto*
> *vigatanidrāḥ ‖ svapitṛṣornajijñati najij pratyaya iti tadetatpadakārasyānabhimataṃ ‖*
> *animiṣā nimeṣarahitāḥ | adabdhā asurādibhirahiṃsitāḥ | uruśaṃsā urubhirbahubhiḥ*
> *śaṃsanīyāḥ stotavyāḥ | evaṃguṇaviśiṣṭā ādhārayaṃtetyanvayaḥ ‖*

Gist: the Ādityas, golden-adorned, shining, sleepless and unharmed, uphold the three heavenly lights for the upright mortal.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

हिरण्ययाः (*hiraṇyayāḥ*) (hiraṇyayāḥ): those of golden form, or those of a beneficial and charming appearance; शुचयः (*śucayaḥ*): the shining; धारपूताः (*dhārapūtāḥ*): those pure as if washed by a stream of water; अस्वप्नजः (*asvapnajaḥ*): those without sleep; अनिमिषाः (*animiṣāḥ*): those who strike (close) the eyelid [sic, as supplied: the Kannada says "who do close the eyelid"; the Bhāvārtha has "who do not close"]; अदब्धाः (*adabdhāḥ*): unharmed by Asuras and others; उरुशंसाः (*uruśaṃsāḥ*): praised by many — the Ādityas. ऋजवे (*ṛjave*): of the upright-minded worshipper; मर्त्याय (*martyāya*): for the good of man; दिव्या (*divyā*): born in the world of the gods; त्री रोचना (*trī rocanā*): the three luminous orbs beginning with Agni; धारयंत (*dhārayaṃta*): have upheld.

### Printed p. 87

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The Ādityas, of golden form, shining, pure as if washed by streams of water, sleepless, not closing the eyelids, unharmed by Asuras and others, praised by many, have upheld the three luminous orbs beginning with Agni, born in the world of the gods, for the good of the upright-minded worshipper, man.

**Printed English translation**

The Adityas, decorated with golden ornaments, brilliant, purified by showers, who never slumber, nor close their eyelids, who are unassailable, and praised by many, uphold the three bright heavenly regions for the sake of the upright man.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

श्री रोचना दिव्या धारयंत (*śrī rocanā divyā dhārayaṃta*): Sāyaṇa explains it as आदित्याः दिवि भवानि त्रीणि रोचमानानि अग्न्यादीनि तेजांसि अवस्थापर्य (*ādityāḥ divi bhavāni trīṇi rocamānāni agnyādīni tejāṃsi avasthāparya*) [as supplied]: it is described that the Ādityas have set firmly in place the three kinds of lights born in the sky, Agni, Sūrya and the rest, so as to bring happiness to those who praise them.

On the word हिरण्ययाः (*hiraṇyayāḥ*) he gives हिरण्यालंकृतसर्वांगाः (*hiraṇyālaṃkṛtasarvāṃgāḥ*) and हितरमणीयवेषाः (*hitaramaṇīyaveṣāḥ*): it is explained that the Ādityas have limbs adorned with golden ornaments, or have a dress and decoration that is beneficial and charming.

धारपूताः (*dhārapūtāḥ*): the Ādityas are pure, as if well washed by streams of water; अस्वप्नजः (*asvapnajaḥ*): without dreams or without sleep; उरुशंसाः (*uruśaṃsāḥ*): worthy of praise by many people. Thus the Ādityas' greatness, adornment, form and nature are described.

#### Ṛk 10

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> त्वं विश्वे॑षां वरुणासि॒ राजा॒ ये च॑ दे॒वा अ॑सुर॒ ये च॒ मर्ताः॑ ।
> श॒तं नो॑ रास्व श॒रदो॑ वि॒चक्षे॒ऽश्यामायूं॑षि॒ सुधि॑तानि॒ पूर्वा॑ ॥ १० ॥

> *tvaṃ viśveṣāṃ varuṇāsi rājā ye ca devā asura ye ca martāḥ |*
> *śataṃ no rāsva śarado vicakṣe 'śyāmāyūṃṣi sudhitāni pūrvā ‖ 10 ‖*

### Printed p. 88

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> त्वं । विश्वेषां । वरुण । असि । राजा । ये । च । देवाः । असुर । ये । च । मर्ताः
> शतं । नः । रास्व । शरदः । विचक्षे । अश्याम । आयूंषि । सुधितानि । पूर्वा ॥ १० ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे असुर शत्रूणां क्षेपक वरुण त्वं विश्वेषां सर्वॆषां राजासि । केषां ।
> ये च देवा देवजनाः ये च मर्ता मरणधर्माणो मनुष्याः । तेषामित्यर्थः।
> अतस्त्वं नोऽस्मभ्यं शतं शरदः शतसंख्याकान् संवत्सरान् विचक्षे विशेषेण दर्श-
> नाय रास्व ! प्रयच्छ ॥ रा दाने । व्यत्ययेनात्मनेपदं ॥ वयं च पूर्वा पूर्वै-
> र्देवैः सुधितानि सुष्टु हितानि शतसंवत्सरलक्षणान्यायुंष्यश्याम । व्याप्नुयाम ॥
> सुधितवसुधितनेमधितधिष्टधिषीय चेति निपात्यते । गतिरनंतर इति गतेः प्रकृतिस्वरत्वं ।
> पूर्वा । सुपार सुलुगिति तृतीयाया डादेशः ॥

> *he asura śatrūṇāṃ kṣepaka varuṇa tvaṃ viśveṣāṃ sarvèṣāṃ rājāsi | keṣāṃ |*
> *ye ca devā devajanāḥ ye ca martā maraṇadharmāṇo manuṣyāḥ | teṣāmityarthaḥ|*
> *atastvaṃ no 'smabhyaṃ śataṃ śaradaḥ śatasaṃkhyākān saṃvatsarān vicakṣe viśeṣeṇa darśa-*
> *nāya rāsva ! prayaccha ‖ rā dāne | vyatyayenātmanepadaṃ ‖ vayaṃ ca pūrvā pūrvai-*
> *rdevaiḥ sudhitāni suṣṭu hitāni śatasaṃvatsaralakṣaṇānyāyuṃṣyaśyāma | vyāpnuyāma ‖*
> *sudhitavasudhitanemadhitadhiṣṭadhiṣīya ceti nipātyate | gatiranaṃtara iti gateḥ prakṛtisvaratvaṃ |*
> *pūrvā | supāra sulugiti tṛtīyāyā ḍādeśaḥ ‖*

Gist: Varuṇa, lord of gods and mortals, is asked to grant a hundred years of sight, so that we may enjoy the lives enjoyed by the ancients; the tail gives the grammar of रास्व (*rāsva*), सुधितानि (*sudhitāni*) and पूर्वा (*pūrvā*).

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

असुर (*asura*): O destroyer of enemies; वरुण (*varuṇa*): O Varuṇa; त्वं (*tvaṃ*): you; ये च देवाः (*ye ca devāḥ*): whatever gods there are; ये च मर्ताः (*ye ca martāḥ*): whatever men there are; विश्वेषां (*viśveṣāṃ*): of all of these; राजा असि (*rājā asi*): you are the lord; नः (*naḥ*): to us; शतं शरदः (*śataṃ śaradaḥ*): a hundred years; विचक्षे (*vicakṣe*): for seeing; रास्व (*rāsva*): give; (वयं च (*vayaṃ ca*): we also); पूर्वा (*pūrvā*): by the ancient sages; सुधितानि (*sudhitāni*): fully enjoyed; आयूंषि (*āyūṃṣi*): lifetimes; अश्याम (*aśyāma*): may we enjoy.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Varuṇa, destroyer of enemies, you are the lord of all the gods and all men. Let us see the full hundred years; as the former sages fully enjoyed a life of a hundred years, so may we also enjoy a full lifetime.

**Printed English translation**

Varuna, destroyer of foes, you are the sovereign over all, whether they be gods or mortals: grant to us to behold a hundred years, and that we may enjoy lives such as were enjoyed by ancient (sages).

### Printed p. 89

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

(Special Topics of Ṛk 10.) In this Ṛk Varuṇa is praised. The word असुर (*asura*) is explained as "destroyer of enemies"; it is described that Varuṇa is a destroyer of enemies and is the king of men and of gods; and the wish is expressed that this god may grant us, the sacrificers, to see a hundred years, that is, to live a hundred years. Further, पूर्वा सुधिता नि आयूंषि अश्याम: that we may live a hundred years, as was determined by the ancient gods; so the sacrificers pray for this god's favour, that such a lifespan may be granted to us.

#### Ṛk 11

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> न द॑क्षि॒णा वि चि॑किते॒ न स॒व्या न प्रा॒चीन॑मादित्या॒ नोत प॒श्चा ।
> पा॒क्या॑ चिद्वसवो धी॒र्या॑ चिद्यु॒स्मानी॑तो॒ अभ॑यं॒ ज्योति॑रश्यां ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> न । दक्षिणा । वि । चिकिते । न । सव्या । न । प्राचीनं । आदित्याः । न । उत । पश्चा ।
> पाक्या । चित् । वसवः । धीर्या । चित् । युष्मानीतः । अभयं । ज्योतिः । अश्यां ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> अदित्यदेवताके पशौ न दक्षिणेत्येषा हविषो याज्या । तिस्रो भूमीर्धार-
> यन्त्रींरुत द्यून् न दक्षिणा वि चिकिते न सन्या । आ. ३. ८. । इति ॥
> हे आदित्याः दक्षिणा दक्षिणो न भागोविचिकिते । मया न विज्ञायते । सव्या
> सव्योवामभागोऽपि न ज्ञायते ॥ उभयत्र सुपां सुलुगिति सोराजादेशः । तथा
> प्राचीनं पुरस्तादपि न ज्ञायते ॥ विभाषांचेरदिक्ष्मियामिति स्वार्थे खः ॥ उत अपि
> च पश्चा पश्चात् पृष्ठभागेऽपि न ज्ञायते । नैशेन तमसावृतत्वात् आंतरादज्ञा
> नाद्वा ॥ पश्च पश्चा च छंदसि । पा. ५. ३. ३३. । इति निपात्यते ॥ किं बहुना । पाक्या
> चित् । पाक्यः पक्तव्योऽपरिपक्वज्ञानोऽपि ॥ डुपचष् पाके । यहलोर्ण्यत् ।
> तित्स्वरितं ॥ धीर्या चित् धीरे नेतव्यत्वेन भवो धीर्यः कातरः ॥ भवे छंदसीति

> *adityadevatāke paśau na dakṣiṇetyeṣā haviṣo yājyā | tisro bhūmīrdhāra-*
> *yantrīṃruta dyūn na dakṣiṇā vi cikite na sanyā | ā. 3. 8. | iti ‖*
> *he ādityāḥ dakṣiṇā dakṣiṇo na bhāgovicikite | mayā na vijñāyate | savyā*
> *savyovāmabhāgo 'pi na jñāyate ‖ ubhayatra supāṃ sulugiti sorājādeśaḥ | tathā*
> *prācīnaṃ purastādapi na jñāyate ‖ vibhāṣāṃceradikṣmiyāmiti svārthe khaḥ ‖ uta api*
> *ca paścā paścāt pṛṣṭhabhāge 'pi na jñāyate | naiśena tamasāvṛtatvāt āṃtarādajñā*
> *nādvā ‖ paśca paścā ca chaṃdasi | pā. 5. 3. 33. | iti nipātyate ‖ kiṃ bahunā | pākyā*
> *cit | pākyaḥ paktavyo 'paripakvajñāno 'pi ‖ ḍupacaṣ pāke | yahalorṇyat |*
> *titsvaritaṃ ‖ dhīryā cit dhīre netavyatvena bhavo dhīryaḥ kātaraḥ ‖ bhave chaṃdasīti*

### Printed p. 90

> यत् ॥ कातरोऽपि ॥ उभयत्र सुपां सुलुगित्याकारः ॥ हे वसवो वासयितार
> आदित्या युष्मानीतो युष्माभिर्नितः शोभनेन मार्गेण प्रापितः सन् अभयं
> भयरहितं ज्योतिः सौरं तेज आंतरं वा ज्ञानात्मकमश्यां । प्राप्नुयां ॥

> *yat ‖ kātaro 'pi ‖ ubhayatra supāṃ sulugityākāraḥ ‖ he vasavo vāsayitāra*
> *ādityā yuṣmānīto yuṣmābhirnitaḥ śobhanena mārgeṇa prāpitaḥ san abhayaṃ*
> *bhayarahitaṃ jyotiḥ sauraṃ teja āṃtaraṃ vā jñānātmakamaśyāṃ | prāpnuyāṃ ‖*

Gist: Addressed to the Ādityas: neither right, left, front nor behind is known to the speaker; may he, guided by them, reach fearless light; the tail gives the grammar of पाक्या (*pākyā*), धीर्या (*dhīryā*), पश्चा (*paścā*).

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

आदित्याः (*ādityāḥ*): O Ādityas; दक्षिणा (*dakṣiṇā*): what lies on my right; न वि चिकिते (*na vi cikite*): is not known to me! सव्या (*savyā*): what lies on the left; न (*na*): is not known! प्राचीनं (*prācīnaṃ*): what lies in front; न (*na*): is not known. उत (*uta*): and; पश्चा (*paścā*): what lies at the back; न (*na*): is not known. पाक्या चित् (*pākyā cit*): though without mature knowledge; धीर्या चित् (*dhīryā cit*): though timid; वसवः (*vasavaḥ*): O Ādityas; युष्मानीतः (*yuṣmānītaḥ*): walking on the path laid down by you; अभयं (*abhayaṃ*): fearless; ज्योतिः (*jyotiḥ*): light; अश्यां (*aśyāṃ*): may I obtain.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Ādityas, because of my ignorance and the darkness of night, I do not know what lies on my right, nor on my left, nor in front, nor behind. I am one without mature knowledge, and timid. Yet, walking on the path laid down by you, may I obtain the fearless light.

**Printed English translation**

Neither is the right hand known to us, Adityas, nor is the left : neither is that which is in front. nor that which is behind. (discerned by me): Givers of dwellings, may I, who am immature (in knowledge), and timid (in spirit), obtain, when guided by you, the light that is free from fear.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In the sacrifice of an animal belonging to the Ādityas, this Ṛk, न दक्षिणा (*na dakṣiṇā*), is to be used as the yājyā mantra when the oblation is offered, as is set out by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra passage तिस्रो भूमीर्धारयन्श्रीरुत द्यून् न दक्षिणा वि चिकिते न सव्या (*tisro bhūmīrdhārayanśrīruta dyūn na dakṣiṇā vi cikite na savyā*). In this Ṛk a prayer made to the Ādityas is stated: that the ignorance, like pitch darkness, may be destroyed.

### Printed p. 91

(Special Topics of Ṛk 11, continued.) Addressing the Ādityas, with the words दक्षिणा (*dakṣiṇā*), सव्या (*savyā*), प्राचीनं पश्चा (*prācīnaṃ paścā*) it is described that the darkness of night, or the ignorance grown up in the mind, envelops me who knows nothing and makes the right and left, and the arrangement of the east and west directions, imperceptible; and with पाक्या चित् (*pākyā cit*), धीर्या चित् (*dhīryā cit*), युष्मानीतः अभयं ज्योतिरश्यां (*yuṣmānītaḥ abhayaṃ jyotiraśyāṃ*) a prayer is stated: that the sacrificer, though of small knowledge (slow-witted) or dull, being favoured by these Ādityas and having fear from no one, may obtain the light that destroys the darkness connected with the sun, or the knowledge that removes ignorance.

पाक्या (*pākyā*): पक्तव्यः अपरिपक्वज्ञानः (*paktavyaḥ aparipakvajñānaḥ*); Sāyaṇa explains it as one without complete knowledge. Yāska reads the word पाकः (*pākaḥ*) among the words for the praiseworthy (Ni. 3-13) and explains (Ni. 3-12): पाकः पक्तव्यो भवति विपक्वप्रज्ञ आदित्यः (*pākaḥ paktavyo bhavati vipakvaprajña ādityaḥ*). The explanation of the word पाक (*pāka*) was given earlier, in the Vāmana Sūkta.

धीर्या चित् (*dhīryā cit*): धीरे नेतव्यत्वेन भवः धीर्यः कातरः (*dhīre netavyatvena bhavaḥ dhīryaḥ kātaraḥ*): Sāyaṇa explains the meaning as one who is intelligent (is taught) and acts as instructed, that is, one who has not even a little knowledge. For the word चित् (*cit*) the sense of "and" (samuccaya) is stated.

#### Ṛk 12

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> यो राज॑भ्य ऋत॒निभ्यो॑ द॒दाश॒ यं व॒र्धय॑न्ति पु॒ष्टय॑श्च॒ नित्याः॑ ।
> स रे॒वान्या॑ति प्रथ॒मो रथे॑न वसु॒दावा॑ वि॒दथे॑षु प्रश॒स्तः ॥ १२ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> यः । राजभ्यः । ऋतनिभ्यः । ददाश । यं । वर्धयन्ति । पुष्टयः । च । नित्याः ।
> ७ । रेवान् । याति । प्रथमः । रथेन । वस्तुदावा । विदथेषु । प्रशस्तः ॥ १२ ॥

> *yaḥ | rājabhyaḥ | ṛtanibhyaḥ | dadāśa | yaṃ | vardhayanti | puṣṭayaḥ | ca | nityāḥ |*
> *7 | revān | yāti | prathamaḥ | rathena | vastudāvā | vidatheṣu | praśastaḥ ‖ 12 ‖*

### Printed p. 92

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> यो यजमानो राजभ्यो रुजमानेभ्य ऋतनिभ्य ऋतस्य सत्यस्य वा नेतृभ्य
> आदित्येभ्यो ददाश । चरुपुरोडाशादिकं ददाति। यं यजमानं पुष्टयः पोषका
> नित्याः शाश्वता आदित्याश्च वर्धयंति । स यजमानो देवान्थनवान् प्रथमः प्रथितः
> प्रख्यातः वसुदावा वसूनां धनानां दाता सन् प्रशस्तः सर्वैः प्रशस्यमानो विद-
> धेषु वेद्येषु लब्धव्येषु गृहेषु रथेन वाहनेन याति । गच्छति ॥

*Gist: the sacrificer who offers cooked oblations to the Ādityas is made to prosper by them, and goes to the sacrifice in a chariot, wealthy, famed and praised.*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

yaḥ: the sacrificer who; rājabhyaḥ: to the shining ones; ṛtanibhyaḥ: to the Ādityas, who are leaders of the sacrifice; dadāśa: offers cooked offerings, cakes and the like (and); yaṃ: whom (which sacrificer); puṣṭayaḥ: the nourishing; nityāḥ: the eternal Ādityas; vardhayanti: cause to prosper; saḥ: that sacrificer; revān: as one wealthy; prathamaḥ: as one renowned; vasudāvā: as a giver of wealth; praśastaḥ: as one praised by all; vidatheṣu: in the sacrificial houses (to the sacrificial houses); rathena: seated in a chariot; yāti: goes.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The sacrificer who offers cooked offerings, cakes and the like to the shining Ādityas, the leaders of the sacrifice, and whom the nourishing and eternal Ādityas cause to prosper, that sacrificer, wealthy, renowned, a giver of wealth and praised by all, goes seated in a chariot to the sacrificial houses.

**Printed English translation**

He who presents offerings to the royal and true (Adityas); he whom their constant favours exalt ; he proceeds, wealthy. renowned, munificent and honoured, to sacrifices, in his chariot.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

ऋतनिभ्यः ददाश (ऋतनिभ्यः ददाश (*ṛtanibhyaḥ dadāśa*)): he offers cooked offerings, cakes and the like to the Ādityas, the leaders of truth or of the sacrifice. Because the Ādityas stand in front and protect truth and the sacrifice, they are leaders; knowing this, the sacrificer offers them cooked offerings and cakes, the form of the oblation, and

### Printed p. 93

(continued) so pleases them. These Ādityas, पुष्टयः नित्याः (*puṣṭayaḥ nityāḥ*), nourish the whole world and are imperishable, and so they favour the sacrificer who offers them oblations, that he may prosper. The sacrificer who has won their favour, by the wealth obtained through that favour, is the chief of all, famous among all, and a giver of wealth to others; thus the greatness of the Ādityas' favour, as obtained by sacrificers, is described. And it is explained that the sacrificer, praised by others, seated in a chariot, goes towards the sacrificial house (प्र शस्तः विदथेषु रथेन याति (*pra śastaḥ vidatheṣu rathena yāti*)).

The word वसु (*vasu*) is given by Yāska among the names of rays (Ni. 2-13) and among the names of wealth (Ni. 3-9, as printed). In यस्त्वा यंतं वसुना प्रातरित्वः (*yastvā yaṃtaṃ vasunā prātaritvaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-125--2) it is used in the sense of food (वसुना अन्नेन (*vasunā annena*)). Sāyaṇa says it is used here in the sense of wealth, and explains the word वसुदावा (*vasudāvā*) as one who gives wealth.

#### Ṛk 13

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> शुचि॑र॒सः सू॒यव॑सा॒ अद॑ब्ध॒ उप॑ क्षेति वृ॒द्धव॑याः सु॒वीरः॑ ।
> निकि॒ष्टं घ्न॒न्त्यन्ति॑तो॒ न दू॒राद्य आ॑दि॒त्यानां॒ भव॑ति॒ प्रणी॑तौ ॥ १३ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> शुचिः । अपः । सुयवसाः । अदब्धः । उप । क्षेति । वृद्धवयाः । सुवीरः ।
> नकिः । तं । घ्न्ति । अन्तितः । न । दूरात् । यः । आदित्यानां । भवति । प्रनीशौ ॥ १३ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> स यजमानः शुचिर्दिप्यमानः शुद्धो वा अदब्धः सपत्नैरबाधितो वृद्ध-
> वयाः प्रभूतान्नः सुवीरः शोभनपुत्रश्च सन् सुयवसाः शोभनसस्यान्यपो वृष्टि-
> लक्षणान्युदकान्युपक्षेति । उपनिवसति । सर्वदा सुसस्यानामपां समीपे निवसती-
> त्यर्थः । तथा नकिर्न च तं घ्नंति हिंसंत्यंतितो न अंतिकात्समीपदेशाच्च दूराद्वि-
> प्रकृष्टाच्च देशादागता बाधकाः ॥ अंतिकस्य तसि कादिलोप इति लोपः ॥ यो
> यजमान आदित्यानामदितेः पुत्राणां मित्रादीनां प्रणीतौ प्रकृष्टे नयने प्रापणे
> भवति वर्तते स इति पूर्वत्र संबंधः ॥

*Gist: the sacrificer is pure, unharmed, rich in food and sons, dwells near fertile waters, and no enemy near or far harms him who is under the Ādityas' guidance.*

### Printed p. 94

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

yaḥ: the sacrificer who; ādityānāṃ: of the Ādityas; praṇītau: in the excellent guidance; bhavati: is (that sacrificer); śuciḥ: as one pure; adabdhaḥ: not harmed by others; vṛddhavayāḥ: possessing abundant food; suvīraḥ: with excellent sons; being so, sūyavasāḥ: fit for growing good plants; apaḥ: of the waters; upa: near; kṣeti: dwells; taṃ: that sacrificer; nakiḥ: no one; ghnanti: harms; antitaḥ: whether near; dūrāt: or far; na: none can harm.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The sacrificer who lives under the excellent guidance of the Ādityas is pure, unharmed by others, possessed of abundant food and excellent sons, and dwells near waters fit for growing good plants. No one harms that sacrificer, whether near or far.

**Printed English translation**

Pure unmolested, possessing (abundant) food and virtuous descendants, he dwells amidst fertilizing waters: no one, whether nigh or afar, harms him who is (safe) in the good guidance of the Adityas.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

शुचिः (शुचिः (*śuciḥ*)): shining or pure. The word शुचि (*śuci*) is explained by Yāska as शुक् शोचतेः, शुचि शोचतेः, ज्वलतिकर्मणः (*śuk śocateḥ, śuci śocateḥ, jvalatikarmaṇaḥ*) (Ni. 6-1). Sāyaṇa gives the meanings "shining with radiance" and "perfectly pure". So two meanings are stated: the sacrificer who worships the Ādityas is bright with lustre and also perfectly pure. Further, this sacrificer is not subject to the harm of enemies, possesses abundant food, has virtuous sons, and सुसस्यानां अपां समीपे सर्वदा निवसति (*susasyānāṃ apāṃ samīpe sarvadā nivasati*): he lives always in regions full of plants and rich in water, without difficulty in food and drink; thus the power of the Ādityas' favour that can reach the sacrificer is stated.

नकिः (नकिः (*nakiḥ*)): this word is stated by Yāska in the list of all words (Ni. 3-14) and is used here in the sense of "nearby" (as the Kannada puts it). The greatness of the Ādityas is described: enemies cannot harm the sacrificer who has gained their favour, whether from near or from far.

### Printed p. 95

#### Ṛk 14

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अदि॑ते॒ मित्र॒ वरु॑णो॒त मृ॑ळ॒ यद्वो॑ व॒यं च॑क्र॒मा कच्चि॒दागः॑ ।
> उ॒र्व॑श्या॒मभ॑यं॒ ज्योति॑रिन्द्र॒ मा नो॑ दी॒र्घा अ॒भि न॑शं॒तमि॑स्राः ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> आदते [?] । मित्र । वरुण । उत । मृळ । यत् । वः । चक्रम । कत् । चित् । आगः ।
> उरु । अश्यां । अभयं । ज्योतिः । इन्त्र । मा । नः । दीर्घाः । अभि । नशन् । शमिस्ताः [?] ॥ १४ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> उत आपि च हे आदिते मित्र वरुण ॥ आमंत्रितं पूर्वमविद्यमानवदिति
> पूर्वपूर्वस्थ्यामंत्रितस्याद्यमानवद्भावात् सर्वेषां षाष्ठिकमामंत्रितस्येत्या-
> द्युदात्तत्वं ॥ यूयं प्रत्येकं मृळ । आस्माकमुपरि दयां कुरु । रक्षेत्यर्थः ।
> यत् यद्यपि वयं वो युष्माकं कच्चित् किंचिदागोSपराधं चक्रम । अकुर्म ।
> तथापि मृलेत्यर्थः । हे इंद्र परमैश्वर्योपेतादित्य उरु विस्तीणमभयं भय-
> रहितं ज्योतिस्तद्वीयं प्रकाशं ज्ञानात्मकं वाश्यां । अहं प्राप्नुयां । दीर्घा
> विस्त्रतास्तमिस्रास्तमसा युक्ता निशाः ॥ ज्योत्सातमिस्तातृंगिणा । पा. ५. २. ११४ । इति
> निपात्यते ॥ ता नोऽस्यान् माभिनशन् । माभिमुख्यन व्याप्पुवंतु ॥

*Gist: the Ādityas are addressed: forgive whatever offence we have committed; may I obtain fearless wide light, and may long nights of darkness not overtake us.*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

uta: and; adite: O Aditi; mitra: O Mitra; varuṇa: O Varuṇa; yat: if; vayaṃ: we; vaḥ: in regard to you; kaccit: any; āgaḥ: offence; cakrama: have committed; mṛḷa: show kindness to us and protect us; indra: O Āditya, endowed with excellent wealth; uru: wide; abhayaṃ: and fearless; jyotiḥ: light; aśyāṃ: may I obtain; dīrghāḥ: very long; tamisrāḥ: nights full of darkness; naḥ: us; mā abhi naśan: may they not encircle and pervade.

### Printed p. 96

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Aditi, Mitra, Varuṇa, even if we have committed some offence against you, show kindness to us and protect us. O Āditya, endowed with excellent wealth, may I obtain wide and fearless light. May the very long nights, full of darkness, not encircle and pervade us.

**Printed English translation**

Aditi, Mitra, Varuna, have pity upon us, even although we may have committed some offence against you: may I obtain, Indra, that great light which is free from peril; let not the protracted glooms (of night) envelop us.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

The meanings of the words Aditi, Mitra and Varuṇa have been explained earlier. Here these deities are addressed by these words, and protection is prayed for: that each of them should protect the sacrificer and the priests with kindness. Further, the prayer is described as asking them to pardon the offences committed by the sacrificer and the priests and to protect them.

Yāska has explained the meaning of the word Indra (Ni. 10-8); this explanation was given at length in the earlier volumes. Here the Ādityas are addressed by the word Indra; the meaning is explained as "those having unsurpassed wealth", and it is prayed that these Ādityas may grant the sacrificer their own abundant radiance or knowledge. It is also hoped that the Ādityas will so act that the nights, covered with pitch darkness and extending for a very long time, may pass happily for the sacrificer.

#### Ṛk 15

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> उ॒भे अ॑स्मै पीपयतः समी॒ची दि॒वो वृ॒ष्टिं सु॒भगो॒नाम॒ पुष्य॑न् ।
> उ॒भा क्षया॑वा॒जय॑न्याति पृ॒त्सूभावर्धौ॑ भवतः सा॒धू अ॑स्मै ॥ १५ ॥

> *ubhe asmai pīpayataḥ samīcī divo vṛṣṭiṃ subhagonāma puṣyan |*
> *ubhā kṣayāvājayanyāti pṛtsūbhāvardhau bhavataḥ sādhū asmai ‖ 15 ‖*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> नाम । पुष्यन् ।
> उभा । क्षयौ । आजयन् । याति । पृत्सु । उभौ अर्थौ [?] । भवतः । साधू इति ।
> अस्मै ॥ १५ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> य आदित्यानां भवति प्रणीताविति व्यवहितोऽप्यत्रेदंशेन परामृश्यते ।
> अस्मै यजमानाय सविाची संगच्छमाने उभे द्यावापृथिव्यौ पीपयतः । प्राय-
> यतः । कामान्वर्धयतः ॥ प्यायि वृद्दौ । अस्माण्ण्यंताच्छांदसो लुज् । व्यत्ययेन
> तसस्तामादेशाभावो धातोः पीभावश्च ॥ स च सुभगः शोभनधनो दिवो द्युलो-
> कादागतां वृष्टिं पुष्यन् वर्धयन् भवति । नामेति प्रसिद्धिर्द्योत्यते । किंच पृत्सु
> पृतनासु संग्रामेष्वजयन् शत्रूनाभिमुख्येनाभिभवन् उभा क्षयावुभौ निवासौ ॥
> क्षियंति निवसंत्यस्मिन्निति क्षयो निवासस्थानं । पुंसि संज्ञायां घः प्रायेणेत्यधिकरणे
> घः । क्षयो निवास इत्याद्युदात्तत्वं ॥ तौ परैरनाक्रांतं स्वकीयं निवासस्थानं
> आत्मनापहृतं परकीयं निवासस्थानं च याति । गच्छति । तथास्मै यजमानायोभा-
> वर्धौ जगतो भागौ चराचरात्मकौ मर्त्यामर्त्याकौ वा साधू साधकौ भवतः ॥

> *ya ādityānāṃ bhavati praṇītāviti vyavahito 'pyatredaṃśena parāmṛśyate |*
> *asmai yajamānāya saviācī saṃgacchamāne ubhe dyāvāpṛthivyau pīpayataḥ | prāya-*
> *yataḥ | kāmānvardhayataḥ ‖ pyāyi vṛddau | asmāṇṇyaṃtācchāṃdaso luj | vyatyayena*
> *tasastāmādeśābhāvo dhātoḥ pībhāvaśca ‖ sa ca subhagaḥ śobhanadhano divo dyulo-*
> *kādāgatāṃ vṛṣṭiṃ puṣyan vardhayan bhavati | nāmeti prasiddhirdyotyate | kiṃca pṛtsu*
> *pṛtanāsu saṃgrāmeṣvajayan śatrūnābhimukhyenābhibhavan ubhā kṣayāvubhau nivāsau ‖*
> *kṣiyaṃti nivasaṃtyasminniti kṣayo nivāsasthānaṃ | puṃsi saṃjñāyāṃ ghaḥ prāyeṇetyadhikaraṇe*
> *ghaḥ | kṣayo nivāsa ityādyudāttatvaṃ ‖ tau parairanākrāṃtaṃ svakīyaṃ nivāsasthānaṃ*
> *ātmanāpahṛtaṃ parakīyaṃ nivāsasthānaṃ ca yāti | gacchati | tathāsmai yajamānāyobhā-*
> *vardhau jagato bhāgau carācarātmakau martyāmartyākau vā sādhū sādhakau bhavataḥ ‖*

The printed text carries two footnotes, "1 ಜಯಗಣೇಶಾಯ ನಮಃ" (an invocation, "Victory to Gaṇeśa, salutation") and "1 ಪ್ರವರ್ತಕ" ("instigator"), whose points of attachment are not shown in the supplied text.

*Gist: the sacrificer under the Ādityas' guidance is nourished by heaven and earth together, thrives with rain, and wins both his own and his enemies' dwellings; both halves of creation favour him.*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

asmai: for this sacrificer (who is under the guidance of the Ādityas); ubhe: both heaven and earth; samīcī: coming together; pīpayataḥ: fulfil his desires; (he, being) subhagaḥ: possessed of excellent wealth; divaḥ: from the heavenly world; vṛṣṭiṃ: the rain (by the rain); puṣyan nāma: surely obtains growth; pṛtsu [?]: in battles; ājayan: defeating enemies; ubhā kṣayau: both kinds of dwelling-places, his own and those of his enemies; yāti: enters; asmai: for this sacrificer; ubhāvardhau: both kinds of creative works, moving and unmoving; sādhū: as favourable; bhavataḥ: become. (The Kannada text prints "dyāvāpṛthivī" as व्यावापृथिविगळिब्बरू (*vyāvāpṛthivigaḷibbarū*) [?] and "pṛtsu" as हृत्सु (*hṛtsu*) [?], apparently misreadings.)

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

For the sacrificer who is under the guidance of the Ādityas, heaven and earth, coming together, fulfil his desires. Possessed of excellent wealth, he obtains growth through the rain that has come from the heavenly world.

### Printed p. 98

In battles, defeating his enemies, he enters both the dwelling-places that are his own and those of his enemies. To this sacrificer both kinds of creative works, moving and unmoving, become favourable.

**Printed English translation**

Both (heaven and earth) combined, cherish him (whom the Adityas protect); verily prosperous, he thrives, with the rain of heaven ; victorious in battles, (he defends) both his own dwelling (and assaults) that of his foe; to him both portions (of creation) are propitious.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

It is explained that for the sacrificer who has obtained the favour of the Ādityas and stands in the path they show, heaven and earth, ever together, bring about growth, favourably, so that his desires are fulfilled.

पृत्सु आजयन् उभौ क्षयौ याति (*pṛtsu ājayan ubhau kṣayau yāti*) — ⟦ಸಂಗ್ರಾಮೇಷು ಶತ್ರೂನಾಭಿಮುಖ್ಯೇನಾಭಿಭವನ್,
ಪರೈರನಾಕ್ರಾಂತಂ ಸ್ವಕೀಯಂ ನಿವಾಸಸ್ಥಾನಂ ಆತ್ಮನಾಪಹೃತಂ ಪರಕೀಯಂ ನಿವಾಸಸ್ಥಾನಂ ಚ ಗಚ್ಛತಿ ।⟧ The word *pṛt* is listed by Yāska (Ni. 3-9) among the names of battle. By the favour of the Ādityas the sacrificer overcomes his enemies in wars, and obtains his own dwelling (house) not assailed by others and also the houses of others which he has seized. That is, keeping his own dwelling in comfort and seizing the dwellings of his enemies as well, he lives happily by his own strength.

क्षयः क्षियंति निवसंत्यस्मिन्निति । (*kṣayaḥ kṣiyaṃti nivasaṃtyasminniti |*) The meaning of the word *kṣaya* is explained as places so arranged as to be convenient for dwelling and inhabited by people, that is, houses.

उभावर्धौ साधू—जगतः चराचरात्मकौ भागौ, मर्त्यामर्त्यात्मकौ वा । (*ubhāvardhau sādhū—jagataḥ carācarātmakau bhāgau, martyāmartyātmakau vā |*) The world is described as having two meanings: either it contains two kinds, the stationary (that does not move), in the form of trees, creepers and the like, and the moving, with beings able to move; or it contains two kinds of beings, those subject to death and those not subject to death, i.e. gods and mortals. All these are explained as becoming favourable and effective for the sacrificer.

#### Ṛk 16

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> या वो॑ मा॒या अ॑भि॒द्रुहे॑ यजत्राः॒ पाशा॑ आदित्या रि॒पवे॒ विचृ॑त्ताः ।
> अ॒श्वीव॒ ता अति॑ येषं॒ रथे॒नारि॑ष्टा उ॒रावा [?] शर्म॑न्ताम ॥ १६ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> याः । वः । मायाः । अभिद्रुहे । यजत्राः । पाशाः । आदित्याः । रिपवे । विचृत्ताः ।
> अश्वीऽइव [?] । तान् । अति । येषं । रथेन । अरिष्टाः । उरौ । आ । शर्मन् । स्याम ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे यजत्रा यष्टव्या आदित्या वो युष्माकं या माया अभिद्रुहेऽभि-
> द्रोहं कुर्वते राक्षसादये निर्मिताः ये च पाशा रिपवे शत्रवे विचृत्ताः ॥ चृती
> हिंसासंग्रंथनयोः । प्रसारिता इत्यर्थः । तान्पाशान् मायाश्च रथेनातियेषं ।
> अतीत्य प्रयतेयं । अतितरेयमित्यर्थः । तत्र दृष्टांतः । अश्वीव । यथा शोभ-
> नाश्वः कश्चित्पुरुषो दुस्तरान्मार्गान् शीघ्रमतिक्रामति तद्वत् ॥ येषृ प्रयत्ने
> भौवादिकोऽनुदात्तेत् । छांदसो लज् । व्यत्ययेन परस्मैपदं ॥ तथा मयम-
> रिष्टाः शत्रुभिरहिंसिताः संत उर् विस्तीर्णे शर्मन् ॥ शर्मेति गृहनाम ॥ त्वया
> दत्ते सुखे गृहे ना आ समंतात् स्याम । वर्तमाना भवेम ॥

*Gist: the Ādityas' illusions and snares, spread against fiends and foes, are to be crossed by the sacrificers as a horseman quickly crosses a hard road; may they dwell unharmed in a wide house.*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

yajatrāḥ: worthy of sacrifice; ādityāḥ: O Ādityas; abhidruhe: for the binding of fiends and others who work harm; vaḥ: your; yāḥ māyāḥ: whatever powers of illusion there are; ripave: for the binding of enemies; pāśāḥ: whatever snares; vicchatāḥ [?]: are spread out (such); tān: those snares and illusions; aśvīva: as a man possessed of a horse crosses a hard road; rathena: by chariot; ati yeṣam: help us to cross; ariṣṭāḥ: we, unharmed by enemies; urau: in a wide; śarman: house; ā syāma: may we be happy.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Ādityas, worthy of sacrifice, with whatever powers of illusion you have for binding the fiends and others who work harm, and whatever snares are spread for binding enemies, using those snares and illusions as well, help us also to cross by chariot, as a man with a horse crosses a hard road. Unharmed by enemies, may we be happy in a wide house.

### Printed p. 100

**Printed English translation**

Adorable Adityas, may I pass (safe) in your car from the illusions which (you devise) for the malignant, the snares which are spread for your foes, (in like manner) as a horseman (passes over a road); and thus may we abide secure in infinite felicity.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

*yajatrāḥ ādityāḥ*: the Ādityas are addressed as worthy of being worshipped by the sacrificers with sacrifice. Since they have spread illusions and snares for the restraint of the fiends who work harm in every way, and for the destruction of enemies bent on injury, the sacrificers pray that, like अश्वीव रथेन (*aśvīva rathena*) — a man with a good (swift) horse who quickly crosses roads hard to cross (the Sanskrit explanation is quoted in the bhāṣya, beginning शोभनाश्वः कश्चित् पुरुषः (*śobhanāśvaḥ kaścit puruṣaḥ*)) — they may cross, seated in the chariot by the favour of the Ādityas, using the illusions spread for the binding and restraint of enemies and fiends. This is explained by way of an illustration.

*ariṣṭāḥ urau śarman ā syāma*: the word *śarma* is listed among the names of house (Ni. 3-13; as printed in the supplied text, "ಸಿ. ೩-೧೩"). The sacrificers' wish is described: may we, unharmed by the injury of enemies, be happy in the large house granted by the Ādityas.

> मा रा॒यो रा॑जन्स्य॒यमा॒दव॑ [?] स्थां बृ॒हद्व॑देम वि॒दथे॑ सु॒वीराः॑ ॥

> *māhaṃ maghono varuṇa priyasya bhūridānna ā vidaṃ śūnamāpeḥ |*
> *mā rāyo rājansyayamādava [?] sthāṃ bṛhadvadema vidathe suvīrāḥ ‖*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे वरुण मघोनो धनवतः प्रियस्यानुकूलस्य भूरिदाव्यो भूरेर्बहुलस्य
> दातुराढ्यस्याहं मा अविदं । मावेदयानि । आवेदनं विज्ञापनं मा करवाणि । किं
> तदनावेद्यं । आपेर्ज्ञातेः शूनं शून्यं दारिद्रं ॥ वर्णलोपश्चांदसः ॥ यद्वा
> शूनं गतं प्राप्तं दारिद्रं ॥ टुओश्वि गतिवृद्ध्योः । श्विदितो निष्ठायामितीट् प्रति-
> षेधः । यजादित्वात्संप्रसारणं । ओदितश्चति नकारः । वृषादित्वादाद्युदात्तत्वं ॥
> एतदुक्तं भवति अस्मदीयाः पुत्रादयः क्षुधिता इति प्रभुप्रभुसमीपेभिधाय
> याचमानो मा भूवमिति । हेराजन् सुयमात् । सुष्टु नियम्यतेनेन पुरुषो
> गृह इति सुयमः ॥ ईषद्दुःसुष्टिति खलु ॥ ताद्बशाद्रायो धनान्मावस्थां ।
> अवयुत्य स्थितो मा भूवं । वयं सुवीराः शोभनपुत्रा विदथे यज्ञॆ गृहे
> वर्तमानाः संतो बृहत् फ्रौढं स्तोत्रं वदेम । उच्चारयाम ॥

*Gist: may I not, before a wealthy generous patron, plead my kinsmen's poverty and beg; may we, rich in sons, recite a great hymn at the sacrifice.*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

varuṇa: O Varuṇa; maghonaḥ: of the wealthy one; priyasya: of the favourable one; bhūridānvaḥ: of the one who gives abundantly, before such a rich man; ahaṃ: I; āpeḥ: of my near kinsman; śūnaṃ: the poverty; mā ā vidaṃ: may I not have to declare; rājan: O lord Varuṇa; suyamāt: from the wealth gathered under restraint; rāyaḥ: by wealth; mā ava sthāṃ: may I not become empty; (vayaṃ: we); suvīrāḥ: together with excellent sons; vidathe: in the sacrificial house; bṛhat: a mature hymn; vadema: we recite.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Varuṇa, before a wealthy, favourable, abundantly giving rich man, may I not say that my sons and other near kinsmen suffer in poverty and so ask him for wealth. May I not be left empty of the wealth gathered under restraint. Together with excellent sons we recite a mature hymn in the sacrificial house.

### Printed p. 102

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 3 [as printed; should be 2], Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 27, p. 102.*

**Printed English translation**

May I never (have to) represent, Varuna, the destitution of a (once) opulent, dear, and munificent kinsman : may I never, royal Varuna, be devoid of well-regulated riches : (and) may we, blessed with excellent descendants, worthily glorify you at this sacrifice.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

By the word वरुण (*varuṇa*) the Āditya is addressed here. It was explained earlier that Varuṇa too is one of the twelve Ādityas.

मघोनः—धनवतः (*maghonaḥ—dhanavataḥ*): the word मघ (*magha*) is read by Yāska (Ni. 3-9) among the names of wealth; the meaning "possessed of wealth" is given.

प्रियस्य भूरिदान्वः आपेः शुनं मा आ विदं (*priyasya bhūridānvaḥ āpeḥ śunaṃ mā ā vidaṃ*) (explained as): in the presence of a very generous, wealthy man, may I, the sacrificer, not become a petitioner by declaring poverty, saying that our sons and others are hungry. The prayer is explained thus: may we, the sacrificers, not have to go to a rich man who is beneficent and gives abundantly and ask for wealth, saying that our sons and the rest live in poverty.

शूनं—शून्यं, यद्वा शूनं गतं प्राप्तं दारिद्रं (*śūnaṃ—śūnyaṃ, yadvā śūnaṃ gataṃ prāptaṃ dāridraṃ*): the meaning of the word शून (*śūna*) is explained as the absence of any kind of thing, or the poverty (want) that leads to a state of emptiness.

राजन् सुयमात् रायः मा अवस्थाम् (*rājan suyamāt rāyaḥ mā avasthām*)—सुष्टु नियम्यते अनेन पुरुषो गृहे इति सुयमः (*suṣṭu niyamyate anena puruṣo gṛhe iti suyamaḥ*): Varuṇa is addressed by the word *rājan*, and it is prayed that by his favour the wealth gathered by us, the sacrificers, may never be destroyed.

The sacrificers say: बृहद्वदेम विदथे सुवीराः (*bṛhadvadema vidathe suvīrāḥ*): at sacrifices, together with our excellent sons, we recite hymns; therefore bestow favour on us. This is the prayer.

### Printed p. 103

*Running head: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 9, p. 103.*

## Sūkta 28 (Maṇḍala 2)

*The twenty-eighth sūkta.*

**Sāyaṇa's introduction to the sūkta** (Sanskrit, transcribed; see the Anuvāda below for the sense):

> इदमित्येकादशर्चं षष्ठं सूक्तं । आत्रानुक्रम्यते' इदमेकादश वारुणमिति।
> पूर्वसूक्ते कूर्मो गार्त्समदो हि वेत्युक्तत्वादस्कोत्तरस्य च कूर्मो
> गृत्समदो वा ऋषिः । त्रिष्टुप् छंदः । वरुणो देवता । विनियोगो लैंगिकः ।
> अत्र केचिदाहुः । इदमेकादशर्चं तु वारुणं भयपापनुत् । ऋणदारिद्र्यदुःस्वप्न-
> नाशनं चेति शुश्रुम । ऋग्विधा. १२५-१ । इति ॥

*Gist:* *idam* is the sixth sūkta of Anuvāka 3, of eleven ṛks, by Kūrma Gārtsamada or Gṛtsamada, in *triṣṭubh* metre, to Varuṇa; its application is by indication; the Ṛgvidhāna is quoted as saying it removes fear, sin, debt, poverty and bad dreams.

**Anuvāda (Kannada).** The sūkta *idaṃ kaverādityasya* is the sixth sūkta of the third anuvāka; it has eleven ṛks. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said इदमेकादश वारुणं (*idamekādaśa vāruṇaṃ*) ("these eleven are to Varuṇa"). Since the previous sūkta states that the seer is कूर्मो गार्त्समदो हि वा (*kūrmo gārtsamado hi vā*), the seer of this sūkta and of the next is Kūrma, son of Gṛtsamada, or Gṛtsamada himself; the metre is *triṣṭubh*; the deity is Varuṇa; the application is *laiṅgika*. Concerning this sūkta, some say, from the verse इदमेकादशर्चं तु वारुणं भयपापनुत् । ऋणदारिद्र्यदुःस्वप्ननाशनं चेति शुश्रुम (*idamekādaśarcaṃ tu vāruṇaṃ bhayapāpanut | ṛṇadāridryaduḥsvapnanāśanaṃ ceti śuśruma*) (Ṛgvidhāna 2-21-1 as printed in the Anuvāda; the bhāṣya quotation prints the reference as 125-1), that for those who recite this eleven-ṛk hymn to Varuṇa, fear, sin, poverty and bad dreams are removed.

**Sūkta—28**

Maṇḍala 2 ‖ Anuvāka 3 ‖ Sūkta 5 [as printed; the sūkta is the sixth of the anuvāka] ‖
Aṣṭaka 2 ‖ Adhyāya 7 ‖ Varga 9, 10 ‖
Number of ṛks in the Sūkta: 11 ·
Ṛṣi: Kūrma Gārtsamada, or Gṛtsamada · Devatā: Varuṇa · Chandas: *triṣṭubh*.

#### Ṛk 1

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> इ॒दं क॒वेरा॑दि॒त्यस्य॑ स्व॒राजो॒ विश्वा॑नि॒ सान्त्य॒भ्य॑स्तु [?] म॒ह्ना ।
> अति॒यो म॒न्द्रो [?] य॒जथा॑य दे॒वः सु॑की॒र्तिं भि॑क्षे॒ वरु॑णस्य॒ भूरेः॑॥

> *idaṃ kaverādityasya svarājo viśvāni sāntyabhyastu [?] mahnā |*
> *atiyo mandro [?] yajathāya devaḥ sukīrtiṃ bhikṣe varuṇasya bhūreḥ‖*

*(The supplied text prints no Ṛk number after this verse. Possible slips flagged in the Kannada text: सान्त्यभ्यस्तु (*sāntyabhyastu*) (flagged in the Kannada text) where the Pada gives सन्ति (*santi*) and अभि (*abhi*) separately, and मन्द्रो (*mandro*) (flagged in the Kannada text) where the Pada gives मन्दः (*mandaḥ*).)*

### Printed p. 104

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 28, p. 104.*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> इदं । कवेः । आदित्यस्य । स्वराजः । विश्वानि । सन्ति । अभि । अस्तु । मह्ना ।
> अति । यः । मन्दः । यजथाय । देवः । सुकीर्तिं । भिक्षे । वरुणस्य । भूरेः॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> कवेः क्रांतदर्शिन आदित्यास्यादितेः पुत्रस्य स्वराजः स्वयमेव राजमानस्येश्वरस्य
> वा वरुणस्येदं हविः स्तोत्रं वा यजमानः करोतीति शेषः । स च विश्वानि सर्वाणि
> संति द्वितीयभावनिकारभांजि [?] भूतजातानि मह्ना महिम्ना वरुणप्रासादाल्लभेना-
> भ्यस्तु । अभिभवतु । यो देवो द्योतमानो वरुणो यजथाय यष्टे
> पुरुषाय अति अतिशयेन मंद्रः स्तुत्यो हर्षयिता वा भवति तस्य भूरेः भर्तुर्वरु-
> णस्य सुकीर्तिं शोभनां स्तुतिं भिक्षे । प्रार्थये । यद्वा वरुणस्यॆति तृतीयार्थे
> षष्ठी । तेन वरुणेन दातव्यां शोभनां कीर्तिमहं याचे ॥

> *kaveḥ krāṃtadarśina ādityāsyāditeḥ putrasya svarājaḥ svayameva rājamānasyeśvarasya*
> *vā varuṇasyedaṃ haviḥ stotraṃ vā yajamānaḥ karotīti śeṣaḥ | sa ca viśvāni sarvāṇi*
> *saṃti dvitīyabhāvanikārabhāṃji [?] bhūtajātāni mahnā mahimnā varuṇaprāsādāllabhenā-*
> *bhyastu | abhibhavatu | yo devo dyotamāno varuṇo yajathāya yaṣṭe*
> *puruṣāya ati atiśayena maṃdraḥ stutyo harṣayitā vā bhavati tasya bhūreḥ bharturvaru-*
> *ṇasya sukīrtiṃ śobhanāṃ stutiṃ bhikṣe | prārthaye | yadvā varuṇasyèti tṛtīyārthe*
> *ṣaṣṭhī | tena varuṇena dātavyāṃ śobhanāṃ kīrtimahaṃ yāce ‖*

*Gist:* This praise or oblation is offered to Varuṇa, son of Aditi, self-luminous and a lord; by his grace may the sacrificer overcome all beings; I ask of this delightful god the good fame he gives. (The word द्वितीयभावनिकारभांजि (*dvitīyabhāvanikārabhāṃji*) (flagged in the Kannada text) is unclear in the supplied text.)

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

kaveḥ: of the far-seeing one; ādityasya: of the son of Aditi; svarājaḥ: of the self-luminous one; varuṇasya: to Varuṇa; idaṃ: this oblation or hymn (is offered); (sa ca: the sacrificer who offers this oblation); viśvāni: all; santi: beings; mahnā: by (the greatness gained through Varuṇa's grace) majesty; abhyastu: may he bring under his control; devaḥ: shining; yaḥ: which Varuṇa; yajathāya: to the man who performs the sacrifice; ati: exceedingly; mandraḥ: is delighting; bhūreḥ: of such a Varuṇa, the supporter; sukīrtiṃ: the excellent praise; bhikṣe: I beg [or: bhūreḥ: from such a Varuṇa, the supporter; sukīrtiṃ: the auspicious fame; bhikṣe: I ask].

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The sacrificer offers this oblation to Varuṇa, who is far-seeing, the son of Aditi, and self-luminous. Through his majesty gained by Varuṇa's favour may this sacrificer bring all beings under his control. From such a Varuṇa, the supporter, who is exceedingly delighting to the man who performs the sacrifice, I beg auspicious fame.

### Printed p. 105

*Running head: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 9, p. 105.*

**Printed English translation**

(The worshipper repeats) this praise of the sage, the self-radiant Aditya : may he preside over all beings by his power : I beg for fame of the sovereign Varuna, a diety [sic] who, when much pleased, (is propitious) to his adorer.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

कवेः (*kaveḥ*)—क्रांतदर्शिनः (*krāṃtadarśinaḥ*): Yāska has read the word *kavi* among the names of the intelligent (Ni. 12-13) and explained it: "the intelligent man is one who sees beyond (*krānta-darśana*), or from the root *kavate*" (Ni. 12-13). Varuṇa, the son of the goddess Aditi, is described as one who has seen the far shore of wisdom. स्वराजः (*svarājaḥ*)—स्वयमेव राजमानस्य ईश्वरस्य वा (*svayameva rājamānasya īśvarasya vā*): shining of himself, that is, brilliant without the help of other lights, and lord over all. The word इदं (*idaṃ*) is given the meaning oblation or hymn, and it is explained that the sacrificer offers the oblation or hymn to Varuṇa, who is self-luminous and sovereign over all.

विश्वानि संति (*viśvāni saṃti*)—सर्वाणि द्वितीयभाव विकारभांजि [?] भूतजातानि (*sarvāṇi dvitīyabhāva vikārabhāṃji [?] bhūtajātāni*) (the words द्वितीयभाव विकारभांजि (*dvitīyabhāva vikārabhāṃji*) are unclear in the supplied text): all created beings. It is explained that through the greatness arising from Varuṇa's favour the sacrificer may bring under his control all beings, moving and unmoving.

अतिमंद्रः (*atimaṃdraḥ*)—अतिशयेन स्तुत्यः हर्षयिता वा (*atiśayena stutyaḥ harṣayitā vā*): exceedingly praiseworthy, or a gladdener. The word *mandra* is read by Yāska among the names of speech (as printed: सि २-२३ (*si 2-23*), the reference letter being as supplied; the word before "names" is printed वाज्ञामगळल्लि (*vājñāmagaḷalli*)) and explained as "*mandrā* is delighting, or gladdening to the world" (as printed: सि. ११-३० (*si. 11-30*)). It is said that Varuṇa is praiseworthy to the sacrificer and the producer of joy.

सुकीर्तिं भिक्षे (*sukīrtiṃ bhikṣe*)—शोभनां स्तुतिं यद्वा वरुणेन दातव्यां शोभनां कीर्तिं (*śobhanāṃ stutiṃ yadvā varuṇena dātavyāṃ śobhanāṃ kīrtiṃ*): for the word *bhūri*, "of Varuṇa, the lord (*bhartuḥ*)": the sacrificer asks for the praise (the devout mind that performs it), or the excellent fame granted by the favour of Varuṇa.

#### Ṛk 2

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> तव॑ व्र॒ते सु॒भगा॑सः स्याम स्वा॒ध्यो॑ वरुण तुष्टु॒वांसः॑ ।
> उ॒पाय॑न उ॒षसां॒ गोम॑तीनाम॒ग्नयो॒न जर॑माणा॒ अनु॒द्यून्॥

> *tava vrate subhagāsaḥ syāma svādhyo varuṇa tuṣṭuvāṃsaḥ |*
> *upāyana uṣasāṃ gomatīnāmagnayona jaramāṇā anudyūn‖*

### Printed p. 106

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 28, p. 106.*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> तव । व्रते । सुभगासः । स्याम । सुआध्यः । वरुण । तुस्तुवांसः ।
> उपअयने । उषसां । गोमतीनां । अग्नयः । न । जरमाणाः । अनु । द्यून् ॥ २ ॥

> *tava | vrate | subhagāsaḥ | syāma | suādhyaḥ | varuṇa | tustuvāṃsaḥ |*
> *upaayane | uṣasāṃ | gomatīnāṃ | agnayaḥ | na | jaramāṇāḥ | anu | dyūn ‖ 2 ‖*

(Differences between the Saṃhitā and Pada as supplied: तुष्टुवांसः (*tuṣṭuvāṃsaḥ*) in the Saṃhitā but तुस्तुवांसः (*tustuvāṃsaḥ*) in the Pada; उपायन (*upāyana*) in the Saṃhitā against उपअयने (*upaayane*) in the Pada. Both are as supplied; no correction made.)

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे वरुण स्वाध्यः शोभनाध्यानास्तुष्टुवांसः स्तुतवंतो वयं तव व्रते
> त्वदीये परिचरणाख्यॆ कर्मणि वर्तमानाः सुभगासः स्याम । शोभनधना भवेम ।
> अपि च गोमतीनां रश्मिभिर्युक्तानामुषसामुपायन उपागमने सत्यग्नयो
> न यथाग्नयोऽग्निहोत्रादिकर्मार्थं समिध्यंते एवमनु द्यून् अनुदिनं
> जरमाणास्त्वां स्तुवंतो वयं दीप्यमाना भवेम ॥

*Gist:* O Varuṇa, may we, devout and praising you, engaged in your service, be rich; and as fires are kindled at dawn for the fire-offering, may we, praising you daily, shine.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Varuṇa: O Varuṇa; svādhyaḥ: we who are endowed with excellent meditation; tuṣṭuvāṃsaḥ: who praise (you); tava vrate: engaged in the work of worshipping you; subhagāsaḥ: possessed of excellent wealth; syāma: grant that we may become; gomatīnām: accompanied by rays; uṣasām: of the dawns; upāyane: at the time of rising; agnayo na: as the fires kindled for the fire-offering shine; anu dyūn: every day; jaramāṇāḥ: we who praise you; may we be shining.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Varuṇa, we who are endowed with excellent meditation and who praise you, engaged in the work of worshipping you, grant that we may become possessed of excellent wealth. As the fire kindled for the fire-offering shines at the rising of the dawns accompanied by rays, may we who praise you daily go on shining.

**Printed English translation**

May we, Varuna, deeply meditating on you earnestly praising you and (engaged) in your worship, be prosperous ; glorifying you daily, like the fires (that are kindled in your honour) at the coming of the luminous dawns.

### Printed p. 107

*Running head: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 9, p. 107.*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

व्रते (*vrate*)—त्वदीये परिचरणाख्यॆ कर्मणि वर्तमानाः (*tvadīye paricaraṇākhyè karmaṇi vartamānāḥ*): the word *vrata* is read by Yāska among the names of work (Ni. 3-2) and explained "*vrata* is a name of work, because it is chosen" (Ni. 2-13). Here Sāyaṇa gives the meaning: work consisting of service, relating to Varuṇa.

सुभगासः (*subhagāsaḥ*)—शोभनधनाः (*śobhanadhanāḥ*): here the prefix *su* is applied to the word *bhaga*. The word is used as an epithet of the sacrificers; the meaning wealth is given, and the sacrificers' prayer is stated, that they may be endowed with excellent wealth. Yāska explains भगो भजतेः (*bhago bhajateḥ*) (Ni. 1-7), reads it among the names of wealth (Ni. 3-9) with the meaning wealth, explains it as "dharma" in भगस्य भागधेयस्य (*bhagasya bhāgadheyasya*) (Ni. 9-21), and in प्रातर्जितं भगमुग्रं हुवेम (*prātarjitaṃ bhagamugraṃ huvema*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-41-2) as one of the gods, a son of the goddess Aditi.

गोमतीनां उषसां उपायने अग्नयो न (*gomatīnāṃ uṣasāṃ upāyane agnayo na*): Yāska gives various meanings of the word *go* (Ni. 2-5). Here it is used in the sense of ray (*raśmi*). After giving this sense, the passage रश्मिभिर्युक्तानां उषसां उपागमे यथा अग्नयो अग्नि होत्रादिकर्मार्थं समिध्यंते एवं त्वां स्तुवंतो वयं दीप्यमाना भवेम (*raśmibhiryuktānāṃ uṣasāṃ upāgame yathā agnayo agni hotrādikarmārthaṃ samidhyaṃte evaṃ tvāṃ stuvaṃto vayaṃ dīpyamānā bhavema*) is given. The sacrificers' prayer is expressed thus: as at the rising of the dawns the fire is kindled for the performance of the fire-offering and other rites, so may we sacrificers who praise Varuṇa shine with splendour every day.

#### Ṛk 3

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> तव॑ स्याम पुरु॒वीर॑स्य॒ शर्म॑न्नुरु॒शंस॑स्य वरुण प्रणेतः ।
> यू॒यं नः॑ पुत्रा अदितेरदब्धा अ॒भि क्ष॑मध्वं॒ युज्या॑य देवाः ॥

> *tava syāma puruvīrasya śarmannuruśaṃsasya varuṇa praṇetaḥ |*
> *yūyaṃ naḥ putrā aditeradabdhā abhi kṣamadhvaṃ yujyāya devāḥ ‖*

(The supplied text prints no Ṛk number after this verse.)

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> तव । स्याम । पुरुवीरस्य । शर्मन् । उरुशंसस्य । वरुण । प्रनेतरिति प्रनेतः ।
> यूयं । नः । पुत्राः । आदितेः । आदब्धाः । अभि । क्षमध्वं । युज्याय । देवाः ॥

> *tava | syāma | puruvīrasya | śarman | uruśaṃsasya | varuṇa | pranetariti pranetaḥ |*
> *yūyaṃ | naḥ | putrāḥ | āditeḥ | ādabdhāḥ | abhi | kṣamadhvaṃ | yujyāya | devāḥ ‖*

(The Pada: प्रनेतरिति प्रनेतः (*pranetariti pranetaḥ*) against Saṃhitā प्रणेतः (*praṇetaḥ*), and आदितेः (*āditeḥ*), आदब्धाः (*ādabdhāḥ*) against अदिते (*adite*), अदब्धा (*adabdhā*) in the Saṃhitā, are as supplied; the Pada here shows no Ṛk number. The Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya of this Ṛk follows on the next page.)

### Printed p. 108

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 28, p. 108.*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे प्रणेतः सर्वस्य जगतः प्रकर्षेण नेतर्वरुण पुरुवीरस्य पुरुभिर्बहुभिः-
> र्वीर्रैरुपेतस्य यद्वा पुरूणां बहूनां शत्रोणां विशेषेण प्रेरकस्योरु-
> शंसस्योरुभिर्बहुभिः स्तूयमानस्य तव शर्मन् शर्मणि सुखे गृहे ना स्याम ।
> अदितेः पुत्रा मित्रादयः ॥ परमपि छंदसीति परस्य षष्ठ्यंतस्य पूर्वामंत्रितांगव
> द्भावेनानुप्रवेशात् पदद्वयसमुदायस्याष्टमिकं सर्वानुदात्तत्वं ॥ अदब्बाः
> शत्रुभिरहिंसिताः । एवंभूता हे देवा यूयं नोऽस्मान् कृतापराधान्
> युज्याय सख्यायाभिक्षमध्वं । अस्माभिः कृतमपराधं सहध्वं ॥

*Gist:* Varuṇa, leader of all the world, may we dwell in your protection; sons of Aditi, unharmed by foes, forgive our offences and keep us in friendship.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Pranetaḥ (leader)—guide of the whole world; Varuṇa—O Varuṇa; Puruvīrasya—of you who are endowed with many kinds of valour; Uruśaṃsasya—praised by many people; Tava—your; Śarman—in the pleasant house; Syāma—may we be; Aditeḥ putrāḥ—O sons of Aditi, Mitra and the other gods; Adabdhāḥ—unharmed by enemies; Devāḥ—divine in nature; Yūyaṃ—you; Naḥ—us (even though we have committed offences); Yujyāya—for friendship; Abhi kṣamadhvaṃ—forgive.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Varuṇa, guide of the whole world, may we dwell in your pleasant house, you who are endowed with many kinds of valour and are praised by many people. O sons of Aditi, Mitra and the other gods, unharmed by enemies and divine in nature, forgive our offences and preserve our friendship with you.

**Printed English translation**

Varuna, chief guide (of men), may we abide in the felicity of you who are endowed with great prowess, and are glorified by many: divine sons of Aditi, unharmed (by foes), have compassion upon us through your benevolence.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

प्रणीतः (*praṇītaḥ*) (as supplied; the Ṛk has praṇetaḥ)—सर्वस्य जगतः प्रकर्षेण नेतः (*sarvasya jagataḥ prakarṣeṇa netaḥ*): Varuṇa leads the whole world abundantly, that is, he regulates it greatly; this is the meaning explained.

### Printed p. 109

*Running head: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 9, p. 109.*

पुरुवीरस्य (*puruvīrasya*)—बहुभिर्वीरैरुपेतस्य यद्वा बहूनां शत्रूणां विशेषेण प्रेरकस्य (*bahubhirvīrairupetasya yadvā bahūnāṃ śatrūṇāṃ viśeṣeṇa prerakasya*). The word *vīra* is explained by Yāska as वीरो वीरयत्यमित्रान् । वेतेर्वा स्याद्दतिकर्मणः । वीरयतेर्वा (*vīro vīrayatyamitrān | vetervā syāddatikarmaṇaḥ | vīrayatervā*) (Ni. 1-7) [text as supplied]. Sāyaṇa gives two meanings, "endowed with strength" or "enemies", and so describes Varuṇa either as endowed with many heroes or as one who drives out enemies.

शर्मन् (*śarman*)—गृहे सुखे वा (*gṛhe sukhe vā*): the word *śarma* is read by Yāska (Ni. 3-13) among the names both of "house" and of "happiness"; therefore Sāyaṇa gives both meanings.

In the first half of this Ṛk Varuṇa is praised, in the second half the Ādityas, Mitra and the others, sons of Aditi. The sacrificers' dwelling in the happiness or house favoured by Varuṇa is prayed for, and the prayer is expressed that Mitra and the other Ādityas should forgive the sacrificers' offences.

> *pra sīmādityo asṛjadvidhartā ṛtaṃ siṃdhavo varuṇasya yaṃti |*

> न । श्राम्यंति । न । वि । मुचंति । एते । वयः । न । पप्तुः । रघुया । परिज्मन् ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> विधर्ता सेतुरिव जलस्य विधारयितादित्योऽदितेः पुत्रो वरुण्ययतं विधारण-

> वरुणस्यर्तेन सिंधवः स्यंदनशीला नद्यो यंति ! गच्छंति प्रवहंति । एते सिंधवो
