> *prakarṣeṇa sṛṣṭavān ‖ aṇo 'pragṛhyasyānunāsika ityākāraḥ sānunāsikaḥ ‖ tena*
> *varuṇasyartena siṃdhavaḥ syaṃdanaśīlā nadyo yaṃti ! gacchaṃti pravahaṃti | ete siṃdhavo*

### Printed p. 110

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 28, p. 110.*

> न श्राम्यंति । श्रांतिं न प्राप्नुवंति । न विमुचंति । उपरता न भवंति । अनलसाः
> सर्वदैकरूप्येण प्रवहंतीत्यर्थः । एतदेवोच्यते । वयो न । वयः दक्षिण इव
> रघुया रघवः शीघ्रगामिनः परिज्मन् परिज्मनि भूम्यां पप्तुः । पतंति गच्छंति ॥
> पत्लृ गतौ । लिट्युसि तनिपत्योश्चंदसीत्युपधालोपॆः । स्थानिवद्भावाद्विर्वचनादि ।
> रघुया इत्यत्र सुपां सुलुगिति जसो याजादेशः ॥

*Gist:* Aditi's son Varuṇa, upholder like a dam, has sent forth the water (or truth); by his law the rivers flow untiringly and swiftly like birds. The grammatical tail explains the nasalised ā, the elision of the penultimate letter in the perfect *paptuḥ* (Pāṇini rules not numbered in the supplied text) and the substitution of *yāc* for *jas* in *raghuyā*.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Vidhartā—the holder of the water; Ādityaḥ—Varuṇa, son of Aditi; Ṛtaṃ—truth or water; Sīṃ—everywhere; Pra asṛjat—created; Varuṇasya—by Varuṇa's ordinance; Sindhavaḥ—the (flowing) rivers; Yanti—flow; Ete—these rivers; Na śrāmyanti—do not become weary; Na vi muñcanti—do not cease flowing; Vayo na—like birds; Raghuyā—swift; Parijman—on the earth; Paptuḥ—fall and glide.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

Varuṇa, the holder of the water and son of Aditi, created water everywhere. By the power of Varuṇa's command the rivers flow. These rivers neither become weary nor stop their flow. Swift like birds, they fall on the earth and flow on.

**Printed English translation**

The Aditya, the upholder, has created all this water : the rivers flow by the power of Varuna: they never weary, they never stop; they have descended with swiftness, like birds upon the circumambient (earth).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

ऋतं (*ṛtaṃ*)—विधारणहेतुभूतं सत्यं यद्वा नदीनामुपादानभूतमुदकं (*vidhāraṇahetubhūtaṃ satyaṃ yadvā nadīnāmupādānabhūtamudakaṃ*). The word *ṛta* is read by Yāska among the names of water, truth and sacrifice (Ni. 2-24, 4-19). Sāyaṇa gives two meanings: truth, the chief cause of upholding everything, or water, which is pre-eminent in the rivers.

विधर्ता (*vidhartā*)—सेतुरिव जलस्य विधारयिता (*seturiva jalasya vidhārayitā*): as a dam holds back water, so Varuṇa holds the rain-waters; the explanation compares him to a dam,

### Printed p. 111

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 9, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 111.*

सिंधवः—स्यंदनशीलाः (*siṃdhavaḥ—syaṃdanaśīlāḥ*): "the flowing ones". The Nirukta authors have read this word among the names of rivers (Ni. 2-24), and they have also explained it, "Sindhu, from flowing" (Ni. 5-27). The comment says that Varuṇa created the waters, held them up for the benefit of the worlds, and made them flow over the earth in the form of rivers. These rivers never stop their flow; as swift-going birds, flying in the sky, also move about on the earth, so these rivers too, so that their waters are never in one place, flow in a single unvaried course (अनलसाः ऐकरूप्येण प्रवहंति (*analasāḥ aikarūpyeṇa pravahaṃti*)), giving no room for even the least sloth. The commentary explains that Varuṇa created the waters for the good of the world, held them, and made them flow ceaselessly over the earth. The Nirukta authors, too, have cited this same idea from the Ṛk beginning as follows (Ma. Saṃ. 8-69-12):

सुदेवो असि वरुण यस्य ते सप्त सिंधवः । अनुक्षरंति काकुदं सूम्यं सुषिरामिव ॥ (*sudevo asi varuṇa yasya te sapta siṃdhavaḥ | anukṣaraṃti kākudaṃ sūmyaṃ suṣirāmiva ‖*)

(The passage ends with "thus they have cited it from the Ṛk"; a software-generated line about a decorative symbol was removed from kn.md here. The reference is given as (Ma. Saṃ. 8-69-12) in kn.md.)

#### Ṛk 5

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> वि मच्छ्र॑थाय रश॒नामि॒वाग॑ ऋ॒ध्याम॑ ते वरुण॒ खामृ॒तस्य॑ ।
> मा तंतु॑श्छेदि॒ वय॑तो॒ धियं॑ मे॒ मा मात्रा॑ शार्य॒पसः॑ पु॒र॒
> ऋतोः ॥ ५ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> वि । मत् । श्रथय । रशनांइव । आगः । यध्याम । ते । वरुण । खां । ऋतस्य ।
> मा । तंतुः । छेदि । वयतः । धियं । मे । मा । मात्रा । शारि । अपसः । पुरा ।
> ऋतोः ॥ ५ ॥

> *vi | mat | śrathaya | raśanāṃiva | āgaḥ | yadhyāma | te | varuṇa | khāṃ | ṛtasya |*
> *mā | taṃtuḥ | chedi | vayataḥ | dhiyaṃ | me | mā | mātrā | śāri | apasaḥ | purā |*
> *ṛtoḥ ‖ 5 ‖*

(Readings as supplied: Saṃhitā ऋध्याम (*ṛdhyāma*) against Pada यध्याम (*yadhyāma*), Saṃhitā मच्छ्रथाय (*macchrathāya*) and खामृतस्य (*khāmṛtasya*) against Pada मत् । श्रथय (*mat | śrathaya*) and खां । ऋतस्य (*khāṃ | ṛtasya*), Saṃhitā शार्यपसः (*śāryapasaḥ*) against Pada शारि । अपसः (*śāri | apasaḥ*); the bhāṣya below reads ऋध्याम. Not altered.)

### Printed p. 112

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 28, p. 112.*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे वरुण मत् मत्सकाशात् रशनामिव रज्जुमिवागो विहिताननुष्ठानजनितं
> पापं विश्रथय ! शिथिलीकुरु ! विमोचयेत्यर्थः । ते त्वत्संबंधिन ऋतस्योदकस्य
> पूर्णां खां नदीं ॥ खा इति नदीनाम ॥ तामृध्याम । लभेमहि । अपि च धियं
> कर्म वयतः संतन्वतो मे मम तंतुः कर्मसंततिर्मा छेदि। छिन्नो मा भूत् ।
> नैरंतर्येणेत्यर्थः । कथा ऋतोः समाप्तिकालात्पुरा पूर्वमपसः कर्मणो
> मात्रा शरीरं प्राज्योदीच्यांगलक्षणं मा शारि ! शीर्णं विकलं मा भूत् ॥
> कृ हिंसायां । कर्मणि लुज् ॥ पुर ऋतोरित्यत्र ऋत्यक इति प्रकृतिभावः ॥

> *he varuṇa mat matsakāśāt raśanāmiva rajjumivāgo vihitānanuṣṭhānajanitaṃ*
> *pāpaṃ viśrathaya ! śithilīkuru ! vimocayetyarthaḥ | te tvatsaṃbaṃdhina ṛtasyodakasya*
> *pūrṇāṃ khāṃ nadīṃ ‖ khā iti nadīnāma ‖ tāmṛdhyāma | labhemahi | api ca dhiyaṃ*
> *karma vayataḥ saṃtanvato me mama taṃtuḥ karmasaṃtatirmā chedi| chinno mā bhūt |*
> *nairaṃtaryeṇetyarthaḥ | kathā ṛtoḥ samāptikālātpurā pūrvamapasaḥ karmaṇo*
> *mātrā śarīraṃ prājyodīcyāṃgalakṣaṇaṃ mā śāri ! śīrṇaṃ vikalaṃ mā bhūt ‖*
> *kṛ hiṃsāyāṃ | karmaṇi luj ‖ pura ṛtorityatra ṛtyaka iti prakṛtibhāvaḥ ‖*

*Gist:* "O Varuṇa, loosen from me the sin born of omitted rites, like a rope; may we obtain the river full of water; let the thread of my rite not be cut, nor the body of the rite broken before its completion." The grammatical tail explains the aorist *śāri* (from कृ हिंसायां (*kṛ hiṃsāyāṃ*), *luṅ* in the passive) and the natural form (*prakṛtibhāva*) of पुर ऋतोः (*pura ṛtoḥ*).

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Varuṇa—O Varuṇa; Mat—from me; Raśanāmiva—binding like a rope; Āgaḥ—the sin consisting in the omission of the observance; Viśrathaya—destroy; Te—given by you; Ṛtasya—full of water; Khāṃ—the river; Ṛdhyāma—may we obtain; Dhiyaṃ—the rite; Vayataḥ—of one weaving; Me—my; Tantuḥ—the thread in the form of the rite; Mā chedi—may it not be cut; Ṛtoḥ—than the time of completion; Purā—before; Apasaḥ—of the rite; Mātrā—the body; Mā śāri—do not cause to be broken.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Varuṇa, remove from me the sin, consisting in the omission of the observance, that binds like a rope. May we obtain the river that is given by you and is full of water. May my thread of rite, while I weave the cloth of the rite, not be cut. Before the time of its completion, do not cause the body of my rite to be broken.

**Printed English translation**

Cast off from me sin, Varuna, as if it were a rope: may we obtain from you a channel (filled) with water : cut not the thread of me (engaged id [sic]) weaving pious works: blight not the elements of holy rites before the season (of their maturity).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

रशनामिन आगः वि श्रथय (*raśanāmina āgaḥ vi śrathaya*)—The word आगः (*āgaḥ*) is explained by Yāska as "from the verb *gam* with the prefix *ā*" (Ni. 11 22, as printed). The word is formed by the prefix *ā* joined with the root *gam*. "It surely comes to the doer, the one who has performed the rite" (अवश्यमेवैतदागच्छति कर्तारं (*avaśyamevaitadāgacchati kartāraṃ*)): the meaning "that which comes to him" is thus settled.

### Printed p. 113

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 10, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 113.*

*Raśanā* means a thing fit for tying, so that clothes and the like stand firm when worn; usually a rope (cord) and the like. Since here it destroys freedom, the *raśanā* is said to be binding. For the word *agas* Sāyaṇa gives the meaning विहिताननुष्ठानजनितं पापं (*vihitānanuṣṭhānajanitaṃ pāpaṃ*), the sin that may arise from abandoning an obligatory duty. This sin lies in the rite of the doer of the rite, so that even if the rite is performed it does not yield its proper fruit; it turns into a fault, the sin of not having performed the rite in the prescribed way, and binds. So the sacrificer's prayer is expressed that these sins, binding like a rope (cord), be destroyed for us by Varuṇa's grace.

धियं वयतः मे तंतुः मा छेदि (*dhiyaṃ vayataḥ me taṃtuḥ mā chedi*)—"May the continuity of my rite, while I carry the rite on, not be cut" (कर्म संतन्वतः मम कर्मसंततिः छिन्नो मा भूत् (*karma saṃtanvataḥ mama karmasaṃtatiḥ chinno mā bhūt*)). The word *dhī* is given among the names of rite (Ni. 3-2) and among the names of knowledge (Ni. 3-13); here it is used in the sense of rite. The word *vayataḥ*, formed from the root वेञ् (*veñ*) (*tantusantāne*, stretching threads), means weaving cloth. If the threads of one weaving cloth and the like break while he is at his work, his weaving does not come out right. In the same way the absence of interruption (*nicchitti* as supplied) in the sacrificers' rites is here prayed for. As a body is complete when joined with its limbs, so a rite is joined with its limbs (the rites to be performed in the middle). Whether the limb is lost, or the rite stops before completion, the rite is broken; the sacrificer's prayer is expressed that neither kind of breaking of the rite occur.

#### Ṛk 6

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अपो॒ सु म्य॑क्ष वरुण भि॒यसं॒ मत्सम्रा॒ळ्कृता॒वोऽनु॑ मा गृभाय।
> दामे॑व व॒त्साद्वि मु॑मु॒ग्धंहो॑ न॒हि त्वदा॒रे नि॒मिष॑श्च॒नेशे॑ ॥ ६ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अपो इति । सु । म्यक्ष । वरुण । भियसं । मत् । संराट् । यत्नवः । अनु । मा ।
> गृभाय ।
> दामिइव । वत्सात् । वि । मुमुग्नि । अंहः । नहि । त्वत् । आरे । निमिषः । चन । ईशे ॥

> *apo iti | su | myakṣa | varuṇa | bhiyasaṃ | mat | saṃrāṭ | yatnavaḥ | anu | mā |*
> *gṛbhāya |*
> *dāmiiva | vatsāt | vi | mumugni | aṃhaḥ | nahi | tvat | āre | nimiṣaḥ | cana | īśe ‖*

(The Pada as supplied reads संराट् । यत्नवः (*saṃrāṭ | yatnavaḥ*) and मुमुग्नि (*mumugni*) against Saṃhitā मत्सम्राळ्कृतावोऽनु (*matsamrāḷkṛtāvo 'nu*) and वि मुमुग्धंहो (*vi mumugdhaṃho*), and अपो इति । सु (*apo iti | su*) against the Saṃhitā अपो सु (*apo su*); the Pada's closing Ṛk number is missing in the supplied text, which ends the page with a stray "8", removed. Not altered.) The remainder of Ṛk 6 follows on the next page.

### Printed p. 114

*Running head: Maṃ. 2, A. 3, Sū. 28, Sāyaṇabhāṣya-sahitā, p. 114.*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे वरुण भियसं भयं मत् मत्तः सु सुष्टु अपो म्यक्ष । अपगमयॆ ।
> हे सम्राट् समग्राजमान हे ऋतावः सत्यवन् यज्ञवन्वा वरुण मा मामनुगृ-
> भाय । अनुगृहाण ॥ छंदसि शायजपीति त्वः शायजादेशः । हृगृहोर्भ इति भत्वं ॥
> अपि च दामेव वत्सात् यथा दोग्धा वत्ससकाशाद्बंधनीं रज्जुं विमुंचति तथांहः
> पापं मत्सकाशाद्विमुमुग्नि । विमुंच ॥ छांदसः शपः श्लुः ॥ पृथक्कुरु । त्वदारे
> त्वत्तो दूरदेशे वर्तमानः । त्वदन्य इत्यर्थः । त्वत्तोऽन्यः कश्चित् निमिषश्चन
> निमेषस्यापि अक्षिपक्षचलनस्यापि नहीशे । नेष्टे । त्वमेवेश्वर इत्यर्थः ॥ लोपस्त
> आत्मनेपदेष्टिति तलोपः ॥

*Gist:* "O Varuṇa, drive fear away from me and favour me; as a milker looses the tether from a calf, loosen sin from me; no one apart from you is master even for the twinkling of an eye." The grammatical tail explains the Vedic forms (the substitution *śāyaj* and the change to *bh* in *gṛbhāya*; the *śap*-elision in *mumugni*; the loss of *ta* in *īśe*).

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Varuṇa—O Varuṇa; Bhiyasaṃ—fear; Mat—from me; Su—completely; Apo mṛkṣa [sic, as supplied; the Saṃhitā and Pada read म्यक्ष (*myakṣa*)]—remove; Samrāṭ—you who shine completely; Ṛtāvaḥ—you who are truthful, O Varuṇa; Mā—me; Anu gṛbhāya—favour; Vatsāt—from a calf (eager to drink milk), from its neck; Dāmeva—as one looses a rope; Aṃhaḥ—sin; Vi mumugni—release (from me); Tvadāre—whatever person is far from you; Nimiṣaścana—even for the time of a twinkling; Nahi īśe—it is not possible to be independent.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Varuṇa, remove fear from me. O Varuṇa, who shine completely and are truthful, favour me. As the rope is loosed from the neck of a calf eager to drink milk, release sin from me. No person who is far from you can be independent even for the time of a twinkling.

**Printed English translation**

Keep off all danger from me, Varuna : supreme monarch, endowed with truth, bestow your favour upon me: cast off (frem [sic] me) sin, like a tether from a calf: no one rules for the twinkling of an eye, apart fram [sic] you.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

Addressing Varuṇa, भियसं मत् सु आपो म्यक्ष (*bhiyasaṃ mat su āpo myakṣa*): the sacrificers pray for Varuṇa's favour, which is the state in which fear is wholly removed, so that fear arises from nobody and in no way.

### Printed p. 115

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 10, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 115.*

दामेव वत्सात् अंहः वि मुमुग्नि (*dāmeva vatsāt aṃhaḥ vi mumugni*)—as a milker looses the tether-rope from a calf, so loosen sin from me. The word *aṃhas* is explained by Yāska: "*aṃhati*, *aṃhas* and *aṃhu* are derived from the root *han*, with an irregular (nirūḍha) infix" (Ni. 4-25); that is, the words *aṃhati*, *aṃhas*, *aṃhu* are formed from the root *han*, and the meaning "sin" is understood from them. The prayer is expressed for Varuṇa's favour that the sacrificer be freed from sins, as one milking the cow looses the calf from the rope. This is stated here by way of a simile. In त्वदारे (*tvadāre*), the word *āre* is read among the names for "far" (Ni. 3-20). Sāyaṇa interprets त्वत्तो दूरदेशे वर्तमानस्त्वदन्यः (*tvatto dūradeśe vartamānastvadanyaḥ*) as: one who is in a place far from Varuṇa (that is, one who does not regard Varuṇa as protector; "other than Varuṇa" means one who is not a recipient of Varuṇa's favour). Such a person, whoever he may be, cannot be independent even for निमिषश्चन (*nimiṣaścana*), the subtle time in which the eyelid moves; and Varuṇa's power is declared, that he alone favours all so that they may be independent.

#### Ṛk 7

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> मा नो॑ व॒धैर्व॑रुण॒ ये त॑ इ॒ष्टावेनः॑ कृ॒ण्वंत॑मसुर भ्री॒णंति॑ ।
> मा ज्योति॑षः प्रवस॒थानि॑ गन्म॒ वि षू मृधः॑ शिश्रथो जी॒वसे॑ नः ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> मा । नः । वधैः । वरुण । ये । ते । इष्टौ । एनः । कृण्वंतं । असुर । भ्रीणंति ।
> मा । ज्योतिषः । प्रवसथानि । गन्म । वि । सु । मृधः । शिश्रथः । जीवसे । नः ॥ ७ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे वरुण नोऽस्मान् वधैर्हननसाधनैरायुधैर्मा हिंसीरिति शेषः । हे
> असुर पापकृतां निरसितः ते त्वदीय इष्टौ यज्ञ एनो विहिताननुष्ठानेन जनितं
> पापं कृण्वंतं कुर्वंतं पुरुषं ये वधा भ्रीणंति हिंसंति शैरिति पूर्वत्र
> संबंधः ॥ भ्रीष् हिंसायां क्रैयादिकः ॥ अपि च ज्योतिषो गृहे वर्तमानादग्नेः

> *he varuṇa no 'smān vadhairhananasādhanairāyudhairmā hiṃsīriti śeṣaḥ | he*
> *asura pāpakṛtāṃ nirasitaḥ te tvadīya iṣṭau yajña eno vihitānanuṣṭhānena janitaṃ*
> *pāpaṃ kṛṇvaṃtaṃ kurvaṃtaṃ puruṣaṃ ye vadhā bhrīṇaṃti hiṃsaṃti śairiti pūrvatra*
> *saṃbaṃdhaḥ ‖ bhrīṣ hiṃsāyāṃ kraiyādikaḥ ‖ api ca jyotiṣo gṛhe vartamānādagneḥ*

### Printed p. 116

*Running head: Maṃ. 2, A. 3, Sū. 28, Sāyaṇabhāṣya-sahitā, p. 116.*

> सौरप्रकाशाद्वा प्रवसथानि प्रवासान्मा गन्म । मा गच्छाम । सर्वदा गृहे वर्तमानः
> सूर्यं पश्यंश्चिरकालं जीवेयमित्यर्थः ॥ गमेर्लुजि मंत्रे घसेति चेर्लुक् ॥
> तथा नोऽस्माकं जीवसे जीवनाय मृधो हिंसकान् सुष्टु विशिश्रिथः । विश्लेषय ।
> बाधस्वेत्यर्थः ॥

*Gist:* "O Varuṇa, do not harm us with the weapons that slay the sinner at your sacrifice; may we not go away from the sacrificial fire; scatter the harmful, that we may live long, seeing the sun in our own home." The tail gives *luk* forms of *gam* (*gan-ma*) in the Veda.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Asura—you who drive out the sinful; Varuṇa—O Varuṇa; Te—directed to you; Iṣṭau—in the sacrifice; Enaḥ—sin (born of omission of the rite); Kṛṇvantaṃ—the man who commits; Ye—whichever weapons, instruments of slaying; Bhrīṇanti—injure (such); Vadhaiḥ—with weapons; Naḥ—us; Mā—do not injure; Jyotiṣaḥ—from the sacrificial fire; Pravasathāni—dwelling in another place; Mā ganma—may we not reach; Naḥ—our; Jīvase—for a (happy) life; Mṛdhaḥ—the harmful enemies; Vi śiśrathaḥ—destroy.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Varuṇa, who drive out the sinful, do not injure us with those weapons, instruments of slaying, which injure the man who, in the sacrifice directed to you, commits sin born of omission of the rite. May we not dwell in a place without the sacrificial fire. Destroy the harmful enemies so that we may obtain a happy life.

**Printed English translation**

Harm us not, Varuna, with those destructive (weapons), which, repeller (of foes), demolish him who does evil at your sacrifice : let us not depart (before our time) from the regions of light : scatter the malevolent, that we may live.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

असुर—पापकृतां निरसितः (*asura—pāpakṛtāṃ nirasitaḥ*). The word *asura* is stated among the names of cloud (Si. 2-22, as printed). It is explained: "*asurāḥ*: from the places (*sthāna*), or because they are *asu*-givers [as supplied]; 'from *asu* he created the *asuras*, hence the *asura*-ness of the asuras'" (Ni. 3-8). Sāyaṇa explains that Varuṇa is addressed by this word because he destroys sinners. And वधैः एनः कृण्वंतं ये भ्रीणंति नः मा (*vadhaiḥ enaḥ kṛṇvaṃtaṃ ye bhrīṇaṃti naḥ mā*)—"with weapons (instruments of slaying) which injure the man who commits sin born of omission of the prescribed observance, do not injure us" (as printed, with the misprint आयुध्यैः (*āyudhyaiḥ*) and आस्मान् (*āsmān*)): protection is prayed for, that Varuṇa not harm us, the sacrificers, with the weapons that destroy those who do harm and those who neglect duty in the sacrifices performed for Varuṇa.

### Printed p. 117

> ज्योतिषः प्रवसथानि मा गन्म-गृहे वर्तमानादग्नेः सौरप्रकाशाद्वा
> प्रवासान् मा गच्छाम। सर्वदा गृहेवर्तमानः सूर्यं पश्यन् चिरकालं जीवेयं।

> *jyotiṣaḥ pravasathāni mā ganma-gṛhe vartamānādagneḥ sauraprakāśādvā*
> *pravāsān mā gacchāma| sarvadā gṛhevartamānaḥ sūryaṃ paśyan cirakālaṃ jīveyaṃ|*

"...always, by the fire's light, from the sacrificial hall pervaded by it, or from a region joined with the sun's light, a state of no separate dwelling arises; that is, the sacrificer, seeing the fire kindled for the sacrifices and the sun, would dwell at home for a long time: such a state is the favour of Varuṇa that is prayed for here."

मृधः—हिंसकान् (*mṛdhaḥ—hiṃsakān*)—"the harmful ones." The word मृधः (*mṛdhaḥ*) is stated among the names of battle (Ni. 3-9). And in the Ṛk

> वि न इंद्र मृधो जहि नीचा यच्छ पृतन्यतः ।
> यो अस्मा अभिदासत्यधरं गमया तमः ॥

> *vi na iṃdra mṛdho jahi nīcā yaccha pṛtanyataḥ |*
> *yo asmā abhidāsatyadharaṃ gamayā tamaḥ ‖*

(Pu [sic] Saṃ. 10-152-4), the meaning is explained as मृधः-मृधकर्त्यन् शत्रून् (*mṛdhaḥ-mṛdhakartyan śatrūn*) (Si. 7-2) ("enemies, doers of harm" [as printed]).

#### Ṛk 8

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> नमः॑ पु॒रा ते॑ वरुणो॒त नू॒नमु॒ताप॒रं तु॑विजात ब्रवाम ।
> त्वे हि कं॒ पर्व॑ते॒ न श्रि॒तान्यप्र॑च्युतानि दूळभ व्र॒तानि॑ ॥ ८ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> नमः । पुरा । ते । वरुण । उत । नूनं । उत । अपरं । तुविजात । ब्रवाम ।
> त्वे इति । हि । कं । पर्वते । न । श्रितानि । अप्रच्युतानि । दुःसदभ । व्रतानि ॥ ८ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे वरुण पुरा पूर्वस्मिन्काले ते शुभ्यं नमो नमस्कारप्रतिपादकं नम इति
> शब्दमवादिस्म । उत अपि च नूनमद्यापि ब्रवाम । उत अपि च हे तुविजात बहुषु
> प्रदेशेषु प्रादुर्भूत वरुणापरमागामिन्यपि काले नमःशब्दं ब्रवाम । उच्चार-
> याम । कस्मादिति चेदुच्यते । दूळभ दुर्दभ दंभितुं हिंसितुमशक्य ॥ दुरो
> दाशनाशध्यादभेषु । पा. ६. ३. १०९. ५. । इति रेफस्योत्वमुत्तरपदादेः ष्टुत्वं च ।

> *he varuṇa purā pūrvasminkāle te śubhyaṃ namo namaskārapratipādakaṃ nama iti*
> *śabdamavādisma | uta api ca nūnamadyāpi bravāma | uta api ca he tuvijāta bahuṣu*
> *pradeśeṣu prādurbhūta varuṇāparamāgāminyapi kāle namaḥśabdaṃ bravāma | uccāra-*
> *yāma | kasmāditi ceducyate | dūḷabha durdabha daṃbhituṃ hiṃsitumaśakya ‖ duro*
> *dāśanāśadhyādabheṣu | pā. 6. 3. 109. 5. | iti rephasyotvamuttarapadādeḥ ṣṭutvaṃ ca |*

### Printed p. 118

> कमित्येतत्पादपूरणं ॥ हि यस्मात् त्वे त्वयि पर्वते न शिलोच्चय इवाप्रच्युता-
> न्यनैः प्रच्यावयितुमशक्यानि व्रतानि कर्माणि विधरणानि श्रितानि । आश्रितानि ।
> तस्मादित्यर्थः ॥

> *kamityetatpādapūraṇaṃ ‖ hi yasmāt tve tvayi parvate na śiloccaya ivāpracyutā-*
> *nyanaiḥ pracyāvayitumaśakyāni vratāni karmāṇi vidharaṇāni śritāni | āśritāni |*
> *tasmādityarthaḥ ‖*

Gist: Sāyaṇa says that, since Varuṇa's undertakings (*vrata*) rest in him unmoved like a rock, the worshippers bow to him; the tail of the bhāṣya gives the Vedic changes of *dūḷabha* (reference Pā. 6-3-109-5) and the filler *kam*.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Dūḷabha—O unobstructed one; Varuṇa—O Varuṇa; Hi—because; Te—in you; Parvate na—as seen in mountains; Apracyutāni—not movable by others; Vratāni—powers of support; Śritāni—have taken refuge (therefore); Purā—in former time; Te—directed to you; Namaḥ—worship with obeisance was offered; Uta—and; Nūnaṃ—now also; Bravāma—we recite praise with obeisance; Uta—moreover; Tuvijāta—Varuṇa, born in many regions; Aparaṃ—in future times also (we shall offer you worship of this same kind).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O unobstructed Varuṇa, because powers of support, immovable by others as in mountains, have taken refuge in you, we have offered worship with obeisance to you in former time and now also. O Varuṇa, born in many regions, in the same way we shall recite praises with obeisance in the future also. (The printed text has "ಅಪ್ರತಿಹನಾದ" for "ಅಪ್ರತಿಹತನಾದ" in the first line, as supplied.)

**Printed English translation**

As we have offered adoration to you, Varuua [sic], of old, in like manner as we offer it now so may we offer it in future (to you), you are everywhere present: for in you, who are difficult of attainment, as in an (immoveable) mountain, all holy acts are assembled, and are not to be dissevered.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

Varuṇa is addressed by the word तुविजात (*tuvijāta*). Since his authority extends over many regions, he is described as born in many regions accordingly, and as दूळभ (*dūḷabha*)—one who cannot be harmed by anyone. त्वे—पर्वते न अप्रच्युतानि व्रतानि श्रितानि (*tve—parvate na apracyutāni vratāni śritāni*)—त्वयि शिलोच्चय इव अन्यैः प्रच्यावयितुमशक्यानि आश्रितानि (*tvayi śiloccaya iva anyaiḥ pracyāvayitumaśakyāni āśritāni*): Varuṇa's power of support cannot be destroyed by anyone, like mountains, rocks, earth and trees; so his might is described. Then, addressing Varuṇa of such abundant might, the fixed practice of the obeisance-worship and the like, offered by sacrificers in the three times, past, present and future, and being offered, is stated.

### Printed p. 119

#### Ṛk 9

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> पर॑ ऋ॒णा सा॑वी॒रध॒ मत्कृ॑तानि॒ माहं रा॑जन्न॒न्यकृ॑तेन भोजं ।
> अव्यु॑ष्टा॒ इन्नु भूय॑सीरु॒षास॒ आ नो॑ जी॒वान्व॑रुण॒ तासु॑ शाधि ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> परा । ऋणा । सावीः । अध । मत्कृतानि । मा । अहं । राजन् । अन्यकृतेन ।
> भोजं ।
> अविउष्टाः । इत् । नु । भूयसीः । उषसः । आ । नः । जीवान् । वरुण । तासु ।
> शाधि ॥ ९ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे वरुण ऋणा ऋणानि पित्रादिभिः कृतान्यस्माभिर्देयानि परा सावीः । परा
> सुव । पराचीनं प्रेरय ॥ षू प्रेरणे । छांदसो लुज् ॥ अध आधुना मत्कृतानि
> मया निष्पादितानि ऋणानि परा सुव । अपि च हे राजन् स्वामिन् वरुण आहमन्य-
> कृतेनान्यैरर्जितेन धनेन मा भोजं । भोगं मा लभेयं । किं कारणमिति
> चेदुच्यते । भूयसीर्भूयस्यो बहुतरा उषसोऽव्युष्टा इन्नु॥ सत्यमव्युष्टा
> एव । अपररात्रेषूत्थाय ऋणानि चिंतयतो जाग्रतो मम व्युष्टा अप्यव्युष्टकल्पा
> आसन् । हे वरुण तासूषिसु नोऽस्मान् जीवान् जीवनवत आशाधि । आ समंताद-
> नुशिष्टान् कुरु । ऋणान्यपकृत्य भोगपर्याप्तं धनं प्रयच्छेत्यर्थः ॥

> *he varuṇa ṛṇā ṛṇāni pitrādibhiḥ kṛtānyasmābhirdeyāni parā sāvīḥ | parā*
> *suva | parācīnaṃ preraya ‖ ṣū preraṇe | chāṃdaso luj ‖ adha ādhunā matkṛtāni*
> *mayā niṣpāditāni ṛṇāni parā suva | api ca he rājan svāmin varuṇa āhamanya-*
> *kṛtenānyairarjitena dhanena mā bhojaṃ | bhogaṃ mā labheyaṃ | kiṃ kāraṇamiti*
> *ceducyate | bhūyasīrbhūyasyo bahutarā uṣaso 'vyuṣṭā innu‖ satyamavyuṣṭā*
> *eva | apararātreṣūtthāya ṛṇāni ciṃtayato jāgrato mama vyuṣṭā apyavyuṣṭakalpā*
> *āsan | he varuṇa tāsūṣisu no 'smān jīvān jīvanavata āśādhi | ā samaṃtāda-*
> *nuśiṣṭān kuru | ṛṇānyapakṛtya bhogaparyāptaṃ dhanaṃ prayacchetyarthaḥ ‖*

Gist: Sāyaṇa takes the prayer to be that Varuṇa discharge the debts incurred by forefathers and by the speaker, so that he enjoy no wealth earned by others, and be given means of livelihood in the many dawns that have passed in sleeplessness over debts; the tail gives the root *sū* (to urge) with the Vedic *luṅ* form.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Varuṇa—O Varuṇa; Ṛṇā—the debts incurred by our forefathers; Parā sāvīḥ—discharge; Adha—now; Matkṛtāni—discharge also the debts incurred by me; Rājan—O Varuṇa, the lord; Ahaṃ—I; Anyakṛtena—with wealth earned by others; Mā bhojaṃ—do not make me enjoy; Bhūyasīḥ—many times; Uṣasaḥ—the shining dawns; Avyuṣṭā innu—(because of the burden of debt) have become as if unrisen, like darkness; Tāsu—in such dawn-times; Naḥ—us; Jīvān—that we may have a peaceful life; Ā śādhi—make us free of debt.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Varuṇa, discharge the debts incurred by our forefathers and the debts now incurred by me. O Varuṇa, the lord, do not let me enjoy pleasure with wealth earned by others [the supplied text of the Bhāvārtha ends here, in mid-sentence, at the end of this window].

### Printed p. 120

(Continuation of the Bhāvārtha of Ṛk 9 from p. 119:) ... do not do so. Because I am under the pressure of debt, many shining dawns have, as it were, not dawned, and have become like darkness. In such dawn-times make us free of debt, so that we may have a peaceful life.

**Printed English translation**

Discharge, Varuna, the debts (contracted) by my progenitors, and those now (contracted) by me; and may I not royal Varuna be dependent (on the debts contacted [sic]) by another: many are the mornings that have as it were, indeed not dawned : make us, Varuna, alive in them.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this Ṛk the word ऋण (*ṛṇa*) (debt) and the verb परासावीः (*parāsāvīḥ*) are used only once. But since the words अध मत्कृतानि (*adha matkṛtāni*) are also used, Sāyaṇa repeats the object-word and the verb in explaining the sense: the debts incurred by the sacrificer's forefathers and the debts incurred by the sacrificer himself, both kinds, have to be paid by the sacrificer; hence the prayer is stated that Varuṇa grant that both kinds of debt be discharged.

On भूयसीः उषसः अव्युष्टा इन्नु (*bhūyasīḥ uṣasaḥ avyuṣṭā innu*): the word इत् (*it*) is given the sense of combination (samuccaya), and नु (*nu*) the sense of comparison. Sāyaṇa's words: भूयस्यः उषसः अपररात्रेषूत्ताय ऋणानि चिंतयतो जाग्रतो मम व्युष्टाअपि अव्युष्टकल्पाः आर्स (*bhūyasyaḥ uṣasaḥ apararātreṣūttāya ṛṇāni ciṃtayato jāgrato mama vyuṣṭāapi avyuṣṭakalpāḥ ārsa*) (as supplied; the last word, आर्स (*ārsa*), is doubtful [?]). That is: even when one is awake in the last watch of the night, thinking how to repay the debts, the dawns that have arrived and have passed one after another remain, as it were, as if they had not passed; thus the sacrificer's anxiety is shown to be abundant. And तासु नः जीवान् आशाधि (*tāsu naḥ jīvān āśādhi*): addressing Varuṇa, the prayer is made that, for the sacrificer who thinks much in those dawn-times, the debts be discharged (ऋणान्यपकृत्य (*ṛṇānyapakṛtya*)) and wealth sufficient for enjoyment be given (भोगपर्याप्तं धनं प्रयच्छ (*bhogaparyāptaṃ dhanaṃ prayaccha*)).

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> यो मे॑ राज॒न्यज्यो॑ वा॒ सखा॑ वा॒ स्वप्ने॑ भ॒यं भी॒रवे॒ मह्य॒-॑
> माह ।
> स्ते॒नो वा॒ यो दिप्स॑ति नो॒ वृको॑ वा॒ त्वं तस्मा॑द्वरुण पाह्य॒स्मान् ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> यः । मे । राजन् । युज्यः । वा । सखा । वा । स्वप्ने । भयं । भीरवे । मह्यं ।
> आह ।
> स्तेनः । वा । यः । दिप्सति । नः । वृकः । वा । त्वं । तस्मात् । वरुण । पाहि । आस्मान्॥१०॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे राजन् वरुण मे मदीयो युज्यो योजनसमर्थः पित्रादिर्वा सखा
> समानख्यानोऽन्यो बांधवो वा स्वप्ने दृष्टं त्वद्विषयं भयं भीरवे भयशी-
> लाय मह्यं य आह ब्रूते । तथा स्तेनश्चौरो वा वृकोऽरण्यश्वा वा यो
> नोऽस्मान् दिप्सति । जिघांसति ॥ सनीवंतर्धोति विकल्पनादिडभावः । दंभ इच्छेतीत्वं ॥
> हे वरुण त्वं तस्मात्सर्वस्मादस्मान्पाहि । रक्ष ॥

> *he rājan varuṇa me madīyo yujyo yojanasamarthaḥ pitrādirvā sakhā*
> *samānakhyāno 'nyo bāṃdhavo vā svapne dṛṣṭaṃ tvadviṣayaṃ bhayaṃ bhīrave bhayaśī-*
> *lāya mahyaṃ ya āha brūte | tathā stenaścauro vā vṛko 'raṇyaśvā vā yo*
> *no 'smān dipsati | jighāṃsati ‖ sanīvaṃtardhoti vikalpanādiḍabhāvaḥ | daṃbha icchetītvaṃ ‖*
> *he varuṇa tvaṃ tasmātsarvasmādasmānpāhi | rakṣa ‖*

Gist: Sāyaṇa takes the Ṛk to ask Varuṇa to protect from the fear that a relative or friend reports from a dream concerning him, and from a thief or wolf that wishes to harm; the tail explains the verb दिप्सति (*dipsati*) (from *dambh*, desiderative) with optional absence of the augment.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Rājan—O lord; Varuṇa—O Varuṇa; Me—my; Yaḥ yujyaḥ—whichever father or other kinsman; Vā—or; Sakhā vā—whichever other relative; Svapne—in a dream (seen with reference to you); Bhayaṃ—the fear; Bhīrave—to the timid; Mahyaṃ—to me; Āha—explains and tells, from that too; Yaḥ stenaḥ vā—whichever thief; Vā—or; Vṛkaḥ—wolf; Naḥ—us; Dipsati [the supplied text has दिप्पति (*dippati*), as supplied]—harms; Tasmāt—from all that fear and that harm; Tvaṃ—you; Asmān—us; Pāhi—protect.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O lord Varuṇa, from whatever fear, seen in a dream with reference to you, that any father or other kinsman of mine, or any other relative, explains and tells to me, who am timid, or from whatever thief or wolf harms us, from all that fear and harm protect us completely.

**Printed English translation**

Protect us, Varuna, against every peril which a kinsman or a friend threatens me with alarmed in sleep; or from a thief or a wolf that attempts to destroy us.

### Printed p. 122

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this Ṛk the sacrificer prays for protection from Varuṇa, with removal of fear; the causes of fear are also stated. First, युज्यः—योजनसमर्थः पित्रादिः (*yujyaḥ—yojanasamarthaḥ pitrādiḥ*), a father or the like able to support; and सखा—समानख्यानः अन्योवा बांधवः (*sakhā—samānakhyānaḥ anyovā bāṃdhavaḥ*), a friend of like understanding with oneself, or a kinsman, indicates to the sacrificer a fear concerning the deity worshipped by the sacrificer; hence protection from this deity is sought.

Second, स्तेनः—चोरः, वृकः—अरण्यश्वा (*stenaḥ—coraḥ, vṛkaḥ—araṇyaśvā*): from creatures that cause harm, the thief and the wicked beasts that kill and eat in the forest (wolves), Varuṇa is prayed to protect.

Although the word स्तेन (*stena*) is not read by Yāska among the names of thieves, it is explained as स्तेनः कस्मात् संस्थानमस्मिन् पापकमिति नैरुक्ताः (*stenaḥ kasmāt saṃsthānamasmin pāpakamiti nairuktāḥ*) (Ni. 3-19) [the supplied text has पापकमिति (*pāpakamiti*), as supplied].

वृकः—अरण्यश्वा (*vṛkaḥ—araṇyaśvā*). The word वृक (*vṛka*) is read among the names of the thunderbolt (Ni. 3-11) and among the names of thieves (Ni. 3-19). It is also explained: वृकश्चंद्रमा भवति । विवृत ज्योतिष्को वा विकृतज्योतिष्टो वा, विक्रांतजोतिष्को वा (*vṛkaścaṃdramā bhavati | vivṛta jyotiṣko vā vikṛtajyotiṣṭo vā, vikrāṃtajotiṣko vā*) (Ni. 5-21); and वृको लांगलं भवति विकर्तनात् (*vṛko lāṃgalaṃ bhavati vikartanāt*) (Ni 6-26) — thus the meaning of the word वृक (*vṛka*) is explained. Sāyaṇa has explained the word as "wild dog (wolf)".

#### Ṛk 11

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> माहं म॒घोनो॑ वरुण प्रि॒यस्य॑ भूरि॒दान्न॒ आ वि॑दं॒ शून॑मा॒पेः ।
> मा रा॒यो रा॑जन्सु॒ यमा॒दव॑ स्थां बृ॒हद्व॑देम वि॒दथे॑ सु॒वीराः॑ ॥

> *māhaṃ maghono varuṇa priyasya bhūridānna ā vidaṃ śūnamāpeḥ |*
> *mā rāyo rājansu yamādava sthāṃ bṛhadvadema vidathe suvīrāḥ ‖*

### Printed p. 123

*Running head: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 8, p. 123.*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> मा । अहं । मघोनः । वरुण । प्रियस्य । भूर्इदान्वः । आ । विदं । शूनं । आपेः ।
> मा । रायः । राजन् । सुयमात् । अव । स्थां । बृहत् । वदेम । विदथे । सु-
> वीराः ॥ १७ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे वरुण मघोनो धनवतः प्रियस्यानुकूलस्य भूरिदाव्यो भूरेर्बहुलस्य
> दातुराड्यस्याहं मा आविदं । मावेदयानि । आवेदनं विज्ञापनं मा करवाणि । किं
> तदनावेद्यं । आपेर्ज्ञातेः शूनं शून्यं दारिद्रं ॥ वर्णलोपश्रांदसः ॥ यद्वा
> शूनं गतं प्राप्तं दारिद्रं ॥ टुओश्वि गतिवृद्ध्योः । श्वीदितो निष्ठायामितीट् प्रति-
> षेधः । यजादित्वात्संप्रसारणं । ओदितश्चति नकारः । वृषादित्वादाद्युदात्तत्वं ॥
> एतदुक्तं भवति । अस्मदीयाः पुत्रादयः क्षुधिता इति प्रभुप्रभुसमीपेभिधाय
> याचमानो मा भूवमिति । हे राजन् सुयमात् । सुष्टु नियम्यतेनेन पुरुषो
> गृह इति सुयमः ॥ ईषद्दुःसुष्टिति खलु ॥ तादृशाद्रायो धनान्मावस्थां ।
> अवयुत्य स्थितो मा भूवं । वयं सुनीराः शोभनपुत्रा विदथे यज्ञे गृहे
> वर्तमानाः संतो बृहत् फौढं स्तोत्रं वदेम । उच्चारयाम ॥

> *he varuṇa maghono dhanavataḥ priyasyānukūlasya bhūridāvyo bhūrerbahulasya*
> *dāturāḍyasyāhaṃ mā āvidaṃ | māvedayāni | āvedanaṃ vijñāpanaṃ mā karavāṇi | kiṃ*
> *tadanāvedyaṃ | āperjñāteḥ śūnaṃ śūnyaṃ dāridraṃ ‖ varṇalopaśrāṃdasaḥ ‖ yadvā*
> *śūnaṃ gataṃ prāptaṃ dāridraṃ ‖ ṭuośvi gativṛddhyoḥ | śvīdito niṣṭhāyāmitīṭ prati-*
> *ṣedhaḥ | yajāditvātsaṃprasāraṇaṃ | oditaścati nakāraḥ | vṛṣāditvādādyudāttatvaṃ ‖*
> *etaduktaṃ bhavati | asmadīyāḥ putrādayaḥ kṣudhitā iti prabhuprabhusamīpebhidhāya*
> *yācamāno mā bhūvamiti | he rājan suyamāt | suṣṭu niyamyatenena puruṣo*
> *gṛha iti suyamaḥ ‖ īṣadduḥsuṣṭiti khalu ‖ tādṛśādrāyo dhanānmāvasthāṃ |*
> *avayutya sthito mā bhūvaṃ | vayaṃ sunīrāḥ śobhanaputrā vidathe yajñe gṛhe*
> *vartamānāḥ saṃto bṛhat phauḍhaṃ stotraṃ vadema | uccārayāma ‖*

*Gist:* Sāyaṇa takes the Ṛk as a prayer that the sacrificer may never have to go to a rich, kind and generous patron and plead his kinsman's poverty, nor be emptied of well-regulated wealth; the grammatical tail explains शूनम् (*śūnam*) from *śvi* (to go, to grow), with elision of a letter, and सुयमात् (*suyamāt*).

*(Readings as supplied: the Pada reads भूर्इदान्वः for the Saṃhitā's भूरिदान्नः; the bhāṣya and Pratipadārtha have भूरिदाव्यः; the bhāṣya has सुनीराः where the Saṃhitā and Pada have सुवीराः; the bhāṣya tail has an odd sequence "ಟುಓಶ್ವಿ". All left as supplied.)*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Varuṇa—O Varuṇa; Maghonaḥ—of the wealthy; Priyasya—of the favourable one; Bhūridāvyaḥ—of him who gives abundantly, that is, of the rich man, in front of him; Ahaṃ—I; Āpeḥ—of my near kinsman; Śūnaṃ—the poverty; Mā ā vidaṃ—may I not (have to) tell it; Rājan—O lord Varuṇa; Suyamāt—from what is gathered under restraint (regulation); Rāyaḥ—from wealth; Mā ava sthāṃ—may I not become empty; (Vayaṃ—we); Suvīrāḥ—together with excellent sons; Vidathe—in the sacrificial house; Bṛhat—a mature hymn of praise; Vadema—we recite.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Varuṇa, may I never, before a rich, favourable and abundantly generous man, say that my sons and other near kinsmen are suffering in poverty, and so ask him for wealth. May I never be emptied of the wealth gathered under restraint. Together with excellent sons we shall recite a mature hymn of praise in the sacrificial house.

### Printed p. 124

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 3 [as printed; elsewhere 2], Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 28, p. 124.*

**Printed English translation**

May I never (have to) represent, Varuna, the destitution of a (once) opulent, dear, and munificent kinsman : may I never, royal Varuna, be devoid of well-regulated riches : (and) may we, blessed with excellent descendants, worthily glorify you at this sacrifice.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

By the word वरुण (*varuṇa*) the Āditya is here addressed; it has been explained earlier that Varuṇa is one of the twelve Ādityas.

मघोनः—धनवतः (*maghonaḥ—dhanavataḥ*). The word मघ (*magha*) is read among the names of wealth by Yāska (Ni. 3-9); the meaning "possessed of wealth" is explained.

प्रियस्य भूरिदान्वः आपेः शुनं मा आ विदं (*priyasya bhūridānvaḥ āpeḥ śunaṃ mā ā vidaṃ*) [the supplied text has शुनं (*śunaṃ*) here, against शूनं (*śūnaṃ*) in the bhāṣya] — बहुलस्य दातुराड्यस्य समीपे अस्मदीयाः पुत्रादयः क्षुधिता इति दारिद्रमभिदाय याचमानो मा भूवं (*bahulasya dāturāḍyasya samīpe asmadīyāḥ putrādayaḥ kṣudhitā iti dāridramabhidāya yācamāno mā bhūvaṃ*): the prayer is explained thus: may we, the sacrificers, not have to go near a wealthy man who is kind and gives abundantly, state that our sons and the rest are in poverty, and beg wealth.

शूनं—शून्यं, यद्वा शूनं गतं प्राप्तं दारिद्रं (*śūnaṃ—śūnyaṃ, yadvā śūnaṃ gataṃ prāptaṃ dāridraṃ*): for the word शून (*śūna*) the meaning is explained as the state of having no kind of thing at all, or poverty (ownership) fit to bring about a state of emptiness.

राजन् सुयमात् रायः मा अवस्थाम्—सुष्टु नियम्यते अनेन पुरुषो गृहे इति सुयमः (*rājan suyamāt rāyaḥ mā avasthām—suṣṭu niyamyate anena puruṣo gṛhe iti suyamaḥ*): Varuṇa is addressed by the word राजन् (*rājan*), and it is prayed that by his favour the wealth gathered by us, the sacrificers, may never perish.

बृहद्वदेम विदथे सुवीराः (*bṛhadvadema vidathe suvīrāḥ*): it is prayed that we, the sacrificers, reciting hymns in the sacrifices together with our excellent sons, may be granted favour.

### Printed p. 125

*Running head: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 11, p. 125.*

## Sūkta 29 (Maṇḍala 2)

*The twenty-ninth sūkta.*

**Sāyaṇa's introduction to the sūkta** (Sanskrit, transcribed; see the Anuvāda below for the sense):

> धृतव्रता इति सप्तर्चं सप्तमं सूक्तं गार्त्समदस्य कूर्मस्य गृत्समदस्य
> वार्षं त्रैष्टुभं वैश्वदेवं । तथा चानुक्रांतं । धृतव्रताः सप्त वैश्वदेवमिति ।
> गतो विनियोगः ॥

*Gist:* *dhṛtavratāḥ* is the seventh sūkta of Anuvāka 3, of seven ṛks, by Kūrma Gārtsamada or Gṛtsamada, in *triṣṭubh* metre, to the Viśvedevas; the Anukramaṇī is quoted; the application is stated as already given.

**Anuvāda (Kannada).** The sūkta *dhṛtavratāḥ* is the seventh sūkta in the third anuvāka. It has seven ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Kūrma, son of Gṛtsamada, or Gṛtsamada himself; the deity is the Viśvedevas; the metre is *triṣṭubh*. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said धृतव्रताः सप्त वैश्वदेवं (*dhṛtavratāḥ sapta vaiśvadevaṃ*) (the seven *dhṛtavratāḥ* are for the Viśvedevas). The application is *laiṅgika*.

**Sūkta—29**

Maṇḍala 2 ‖ Anuvāka 3 ‖ Sūkta 29 ‖
Aṣṭaka 2 ‖ Adhyāya 7 ‖ Varga 11 ‖
Number of ṛks in the Sūkta: 7 ·
Ṛṣi: as supplied "ಕರ್ಮೋ ಗಾರ್ತಮದೋ ಗೃತ್ಸಮದೋ ವಾ" (Kūrma Gārtsamada, or Gṛtsamada; the first word and the second are garbled as supplied, left as they are) · Devatā: the Viśvedevas · Chandas: *triṣṭubh*.

#### Ṛk 1

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> धृत॑व्रता॒ आदि॑त्या॒ इषि॑रा आ॒रे मत्क॑र्त रह॒सूरि॒वागः॑ ।
> शृ॒ण्व॒तो वो॒ वरु॑ण॒ मित्र॒ देवा॑ भ॒द्रस्य॑ वि॒द्वा अव॑से हुवे वः ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> धृतव्रताः । आदित्याः । इषिराः । आरे । मत् । कर्त । रहसूः इव । आगः ।
> शृण्वतः । वः । वरुण । मित्र । देवाः । भद्रस्य । विद्वान् । आवसे । हुवे । वः ॥ १ ॥

> *dhṛtavratāḥ | ādityāḥ | iṣirāḥ | āre | mat | karta | rahasūḥ iva | āgaḥ |*
> *śṛṇvataḥ | vaḥ | varuṇa | mitra | devāḥ | bhadrasya | vidvān | āvase | huve | vaḥ ‖ 1 ‖*

### Printed p. 126

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 29, p. 126.*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे धृतव्रता धृतकर्माण आदित्या आदितेः पुत्रा इषीरा गमनशीलाः सर्वैरभ्ये-
> षणीयाः प्रार्थनीया वा हे विश्वे देवा मदारे मत्तो दूरदेश आगो विहिता-
> ननुष्ठानादिजनितं पापं कर्त । कुरुत ॥ करोतेश्चांदसो विकरणस्य लुक् । तप्तन-
> स्तनथनाश्लेति तबादेशः ॥ तत्र दृष्टांतः । रहसूरिव । रहस्यनैरज्ञाते प्रदेशे
> सूयत इति रहसूर्यभिचारिणी । सा यथा गर्भं पातयित्वा दूरदेशे परित्यजति
> तद्वत् । हे वरुण मित्र देवाश्च वो युष्मदीयस्य भद्रस्य युष्माभिः करणीयं
> श्रेयो विद्वान् जानन्नहं शृण्वतो मत्कृतानि स्तोत्राणि श्रोत्रेण जानतो वो
> युष्मान् अवसे रक्षणार्थं हुवे । आह्वयामि ॥

*Gist:* O Ādityas, sons of Aditi, upholders of rites, seekers of favour: cast my sin of faulty performance far away, as a secretly delivering woman casts away her child; knowing the good you bestow, I call you for protection.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

*dhṛtavratāḥ*—those who hold to their rites; *iṣirāḥ*—desired by all; *ādityāḥ*—O Ādityas; *rahasūḥ iva*—like a woman who has given birth in secret and throws the child far away so that no one sees it; *āgaḥ*—my sin, consisting of lapse in performance; *mat*—from me; *āre*—in a place very far away; *karta*—cast away and make it unseen; *varuṇa*—O Varuṇa; *mitra*—O Mitra; *devāḥ*—O Viśvedevas; *vaḥ*—to be obtained from you; *bhadrasya*—of the auspicious nature; *vidvān*—I who know; *śṛṇvataḥ*—you who hear my praise; *vaḥ*—you; *avase*—for protection; *huve*—I call.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Ādityas, who hold to your rites and are prayed to by all: as a woman who has given birth in secret throws the child far away where no one sees it, so throw my sin of lapse in performance far from me, out of sight. O Varuṇa, Mitra and Viśvedevas, I who know the auspicious good that comes from you call you, who hear my praise, for protection.

**English translation (as printed)**

Adityas, upholders of pious works, and who are to be sought by all, remove sin far from me, like, a woman delivered in secret: knowing, Mitra, Varuna, and (universal) gods, the good that follows from your hearing our prayers, I invoke you for (our) protection.

### Printed p. 127

*Running head: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 11, p. 127.*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

इषिराः—गमनशीलाः सर्वैरभ्येषणीयाः प्रार्थनीया वा (*iṣirāḥ—gamanaśīlāḥ sarvairabhyeṣaṇīyāḥ prārthanīyā vā*): Sāyaṇa explains the word as "of a moving nature; to be sought, or prayed to, by all", applying it as an epithet of the Ādityas: those of a roving nature, desired by all, worthy of being prayed to by all that their wishes be fulfilled. Yāska explains the word (Nirukta 4-7) as इषिरेण—ईषणेन ना एषणेन ना ऋषणेन ना (*iṣireṇa—īṣaṇena nā eṣaṇena nā ṛṣaṇena nā*). The Viśvedevas, sons of Aditi, are always of a moving nature; they are desired by all for the fulfilment of their tasks and wishes, and prayed to by all. Therefore may these Ādityas keep far away, that is, forgive or destroy, the offence in the form of a deficiency arising from variation in the performance of sacrifices and other rites by the sacrificer. Thus the Viśvedevas are addressed and taken to be sons of the goddess Aditi. The plural आदित्याः (*ādityāḥ*) is used in this ṛk; Sāyaṇa explains it as हे आदित्याः अदितिपुत्राः हे विश्वे देवाः (*he ādityāḥ aditiputrāḥ he viśve devāḥ*). Yāska explains विश्वे देवाः—सर्वॆ देवाः (*viśve devāḥ—sarvè devāḥ*) (Nirukta 12-40).

रहसूः इव—रहसि अन्यैरज्ञाते प्रदेशे सूयत इति रहसूः व्यभिचारिणी । सा यथा गर्भं पातयित्वा दूरदेशे परित्यजति तद्वत् (*rahasūḥ iva—rahasi anyairajñāte pradeśe sūyata iti rahasūḥ vyabhicāriṇī | sā yathā garbhaṃ pātayitvā dūradeśe parityajati tadvat*): since she gives birth in a place unknown to others and without human traffic, the unchaste woman is here designated by the word रहसूः (*rahasūḥ*).

As this woman throws far away the child she has borne, so may the offences of the sacrificer, the deficiencies consisting of non-performance of rites, be removed by the favour of these Ādityas or Viśvedevas; thus the prayer.

Addressing the sons of Aditi as Mitra, Varuṇa and Devāḥ, the poet says भद्रस्य विद्वान्—युष्माभिः करणीयं श्रेयः जानन् (*bhadrasya vidvān—yuṣmābhiḥ karaṇīyaṃ śreyaḥ jānan*): that he knows the greatness of the good which these gods bestow on the sacrificer; and शृण्वतः (*śṛṇvataḥ*): because these gods hear the hymns recited by the sacrificer, he will call them for his protection.

भद्रस्य (*bhadrasya*): in explaining this word Yāska says भद्रं भगेन व्याख्यातं (*bhadraṃ bhagena vyākhyātaṃ*) and then भजनीयं, भूतानां अभिद्रवणीयं, भवद्रमयतीति वा, भाजनवद्वा (*bhajanīyaṃ, bhūtānāṃ abhidravaṇīyaṃ, bhavadramayatīti vā, bhājanavadvā*) (Nirukta 4-9). Its meaning has been explained before.

### Printed p. 128

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 29, p. 128.*

#### Ṛk 2

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> यू॒यं दे॑वाः॒ प्रम॑तियूर्यू॒यमोजो॑ यू॒यं द्वेषां॑सि॒॑
> सनु॒तर्यु॑योत ।
> अ॒भि॒क्ष॒त्तारो॑ अ॒भि च॒ क्षम॑ध्वम॒द्या च॑ नो मृ॒ळय॑ताप॒रं च॑ ॥ २ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> यूयं । देवाः । प्रमतिः । यूयं । ओजः । यूयं । द्वेषांसि । सनुतः ।
> युयोत ।
> अभिक्षत्तारः । अभि । च । क्षमध्वं । आद्य । च । नः । मृळयत । अपरं । च ॥ २ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे देवाः प्रमतिः प्रकृष्टा मतिर्यस्य प्रित्रादेस्तादृशा अपि यूयमेव ।
> तथौजो बलमपि यूयमेव । आपि च यूयं द्वेषांसि द्वेष्ट्यणि रक्षःपिशाचा-
> दीनि । सनुतरित्यव्ययं । अंतर्हितनामैतत् । अंतर्हिते प्रदेशे युयोत ।
> पृथक्कुरुत ॥ यु मिश्रणामिश्रणयोः । छांदसः शपः श्लुः । पूर्ववत्तस्य तबादेशः ॥
> अभिक्षत्तारोऽभितः शत्रूणां हिंसितारो यूयमभि च क्षमध्वं । अभिभवत शत्रून् ।
> अद्यास्मिन्निने नोऽस्मान्मृळयत । सुखयत । अपरं च अपरमपि कालं । आगामिन्यपि
> कालेऽस्मान् मृळयत ॥

*Gist:* O gods, you are wisdom and strength; drive our enemies, the demons and the like, to a hidden place; overcome our foes, and make us happy today and hereafter.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

*devāḥ*—O gods; *pramatiḥ*—excellent understanding; *yūyam*—you yourselves are; *ojaḥ*—strength; *yūyam*—you also; *yūyam*—you; *dveṣāṃsi*—the demons and others who hate us; *sanutaḥ*—to a hidden, distant place; *yuyota*—drive away; *abhikṣattāraḥ*—you who strike enemies on every side; *abhi ca kṣamadhvam*—defeat them completely; *adya ca*—today also; *naḥ*—us; *mṛḷayata*—make happy; *aparaṃ ca*—on another day also (make us happy).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O gods, you yourselves are excellent understanding and strength. Drive the demons and others who hate us to a hidden, distant place. You who strike enemies on every side, defeat them completely. Make us happy today, and in the same way hereafter.

### Printed p. 129

*Running head: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 11, p. 129.*

**English Translation**

You, gods, are intelligence, you are vigour: drive away the malevolent subduers (of enemies), overcome them entirely, and grant us felicity both now and in future.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

देवाः (*devāḥ*): Yāska explains the word deva as देवो दानाद्वा, दीपनाद्वा द्योतनाद्वा, द्युस्थानो भवतीति वा (*devo dānādvā, dīpanādvā dyotanādvā, dyusthāno bhavatīti vā*) (Nirukta 6-15; the text as supplied reads "ಸಿ." for "ನಿ.").

ओजः (*ojaḥ*): This word is read by Yāska among the names of water (Nirukta 2-24) and the names of strength (Nirukta 3-9), and explained as ओज ओजतेर्वा उज्जतेर्वा (*oja ojatervā ujjatervā*) (Nirukta 6-8). Sāyaṇa takes the meaning here to be "strength".

The gods are addressed. In regard to the sacrificer these gods are प्रमतिः—प्रकृष्टा मतिर्यस्य पित्रादेः (*pramatiḥ—prakṛṣṭā matiryasya pitrādeḥ*): protectors such as a father possessed of excellent understanding; and they are strength itself, able to carry out for the sacrificer the whole business of this world. The verse says that these gods should keep far away the demons, pisācas and the like who harm the sacrificer.

These gods are अभिक्षत्तारः—अभितः शत्रून् हिंसितारः (*abhikṣattāraḥ—abhitaḥ śatrūn hiṃsitāraḥ*), destroyers of enemies on every side; therefore, having destroyed the sacrificer's enemies, may they protect or make us happy अद्य अपरं च कालं मृळयत (*adya aparaṃ ca kālaṃ mṛḷayata*), both now and in the time to come. Thus the prayer is made.

#### Ṛk 3

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> किमू॒ नु वः॑ कृणवा॒माप॑रेण॒ किं सने॑न वसव॒ आप्ये॑न ।
> यू॒यं नो॑ मित्रावरुणादिते च स्व॒स्तिमिं॑द्रामरुतो दधात ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> किं । ऊं इति । नु । वः । कृणवाम । अपरेण । किं । सनेन । वसवः । अप्येन ।
> यूयं । नः । मित्रावरुणा । अदिते । च । स्वस्तिं । इंद्रामरुतः । दधात ॥ ३ ॥

> *kiṃ | ūṃ iti | nu | vaḥ | kṛṇavāma | apareṇa | kiṃ | sanena | vasavaḥ | apyena |*
> *yūyaṃ | naḥ | mitrāvaruṇā | adite | ca | svastiṃ | iṃdrāmarutaḥ | dadhāta ‖ 3 ‖*

### Printed p. 130

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 29, p. 130.*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे देवा वो युष्माकं नु अद्येदानीं किमु कृणवाम । किं खलु कर्तु०
> शक्नवाम । न किमपीत्यर्थः । तथापरेणापरकालभाविना कर्मणा किं कर्तु० शक्ताः ।
> तथा हे वसवो वासयितारो देवाः सनेन सनातनेनाप्येनाप्तव्येन कर्मणा
> युष्माकं किं कर्तु० शक्ताः । सर्वदापि युष्मदनुगुणं परिचरणं कर्तु० न शक्ता
> इत्यर्थः । हे मित्रावरुणौ हे अदिते हे इंद्रामरुतः ॥ देवताद्वंद्वे चेति
> पूर्वपदस्यानज् ॥ ते च सर्वॆ यूयं स्वस्तिमविनाशं नोऽस्मभ्यं दधात । धत्त ।
> प्रयच्छत ॥ लोटि तस्य तबादेशे, जित्वा भावेनातोलोपाभावः ॥

*Gist:* What can we do for you, gods, now or later, or with an ancient, attainable rite? We cannot serve you adequately; grant us imperishable well-being. The grammatical tail (a rule on the dvandva of deity names, and the ending of the imperative) is not explained here.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

*devāḥ*—O gods; *vaḥ*—to you; *nu*—now; *kimu kṛṇavāma*—what can we do? (how are we able to offer worship in a fitting manner?); *apareṇa*—at another time; *kiṃ*—in what manner can we serve fittingly?; *vasavaḥ*—O gods; *sanena*—with an ancient; *āpyena*—practicable rite, how can we worship you?; *aditē*—O Aditi; *indrāmarutaḥ*—O Indra and Maruts; *yūyam*—you; *svastiṃ*—imperishable welfare; *naḥ*—to us; *dadhāta*—bring about.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O gods, whether now or at another time, how are we able to offer you worship in a fitting manner? O gods, with a rite that is ancient and practicable, how can we worship you? O Aditi, O Indra and Maruts, bring about for us imperishable welfare.

**English Translation**

What may we do for you, gods, either now, or in future: what may we do, Vasus, by perpetual and practicable (acts of devotion): Do you, Mitra, Aditi, Indra, and the Maruts, maintain our well-being.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

वसवः—वासयितारः (*vasavaḥ—vāsayitāraḥ*): Yāska uses the word vasu in the senses of food (Nirukta 5-19), wealth (Nirukta 3-9) and ray (Nirukta 2-15). Here it is explained as "those who make one dwell in comfort".

### Printed p. 131

*Running head: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 11, p. 131.*

The gods are addressed. These gods always make all beings dwell in comfort; the sacrificer says that even in the present time, the time of performing the sacrifice, he is unable to render them suitable service (worship and the like), and likewise in the past and future times and at all times; having thus declared his incapacity, he prays that the gods, forgiving this fault of the sacrificer, grant us imperishable protection.

स्वस्तिं-अविनाशं (*svastiṃ-avināśaṃ*): Yāska explains स्वस्तीत्यविनाशिनाम । अस्तिरभिपूजितः सु अस्तीति (*svastītyavināśināma | astirabhipūjitaḥ su astīti*) (Nirukta 2-21). Sāyaṇa gives the meaning "without destruction".

#### Ṛk 4

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> ह॒ये दे॑वा यू॒यमिदा॒पयः॑ स्थ॒ते मृ॑ळत॒ नाध॑मानाय॒ मह्यं॑ ।
> मा वो॒ रथो॑ मध्यम॒वाळ्कृ॒ते भू॒म्ना यु॒ष्माव॑त्स्वा॒पिषु॑ श्रमिष्म ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> हये । देवाः । यूयं । इत् । अपयः । स्थ । ते । मृळत । नाधमानाय । मह्यं ।
> मा । वः । रथः । मध्यमवाट् । ऋते । भूत् । मा । युष्मावत्सु । आपिषु ।
> श्रमिष्म ॥ ४ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हये देवाः ॥ छांदसो वर्णोपजनः ॥ हे देवाः यूयमित् यूय-
> मेवापयो धनस्य प्रापयितारो बंधवः स्थ । भवथ । ते यूयं नाधमानाया-
> भिमतं फलं याचमानाय मह्यं स्तोत्रे मृळत । सुखयत । तथा वो युष्माकं
> रथ ऋतेऽस्मदीये यज्ञे निमित्तभूते मध्यमवाट् मा भूत् । मध्यमेन
> मंदगमनेन वाहको मा भूत् । शीघ्रं युष्मान्वहत्वित्यर्थः । वयं युष्मावत्तु
> युष्मत्सदृशेश्वापिषु बंधुषु सत्सु मा श्रमिष्म । श्रांता मा भूम ॥ वतुप्पकरणे
> युष्मदस्मद्भ्यां छंदसि सादृश्य उपसंख्यानमिति वतुप् । अ सर्वनाम्न इत्यात्वं ॥

*Gist:* O gods, you alone are our kinsmen who bring wealth; make me, the petitioner, happy; let not your chariot be slow on account of our sacrifice; may we not be wearied while we have friends like you. The grammatical tail concerns the suffix vatup after yuṣmad and the substitution of ā.

### Printed p. 132

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2 [sic, as supplied], Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 29, p. 132.*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

हये देवाः (*haye devāḥ*)—O gods; यूयमित् (*yūyamit*)—you indeed; आपयः (*āpayaḥ*)—as kinsmen, givers of wealth; स्थॆ (*sthè*) [sic: as supplied]—you are; ते (*te*)—such you; नाधमानस्य (*nādhamānasya*) [as supplied; the Saṃhitā has नाधमानाय (*nādhamānāya*)]—to one who asks for the desired object; मध्यं (*madhyaṃ*) [as supplied; the Saṃhitā has मह्यं (*mahyaṃ*)]—to me; मृळत (*mṛḷata*)—bring about happiness; वः (*vaḥ*)—your; रथः (*rathaḥ*)—chariot; ऋते (*ṛte*)—(when coming) for our sacrifice; मध्यमवाट् मा भूत् (*madhyamavāṭ mā bhūt*)—let it not be slow-moving; युष्मावत्तु (*yuṣmāvattu*) [as supplied; the Saṃhitā has युष्मावत्सु (*yuṣmāvatsu*)]—such as you; आपिषु (*āpiṣu*)—while there are kinsmen; मा श्रमिष्म (*mā śramiṣma*)—may we never be wearied.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O gods, you yourselves are our kinsmen, givers of wealth. Being such, bring happiness to me, who ask for the desired object. When your chariot comes for our sacrifice, let it not be slow-moving but come swiftly. While we have kinsmen such as you, may we never suffer weariness.

**English Translation**

O gods, you, verily, are our kinsmen : being so, grant felicity to me your suppliant; let not your chariot be tardy in coming to the sacrifice ; let us never be weary of relatives such as you are.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

हये देवाः यूयमित् आपयः स्थ (*haye devāḥ yūyamit āpayaḥ stha*)—यूयमेव धनस्य प्रापयितारो बंधवो भवथ (*yūyameva dhanasya prāpayitāro baṃdhavo bhavatha*): here Sāyaṇa gives the particle इत् (*it*) the emphatic sense of एव (*eva*) (an upasarga, as the text calls it), and explains आपयः (*āpayaḥ*) as kinsmen who give wealth. हये (*haye*) is a particle of address. The verse addresses the gods, saying the sacrificers know the gods to be their kinsmen who bring wealth; the wish expressed in नाधमानाय मह्यं मृळत (*nādhamānāya mahyaṃ mṛḷata*) is that the gods, being kinsmen of the sacrificer, grant happiness to him who asks for desired benefits and praises them.

वः रथः ऋते मध्यमवाट् माभूत् (*vaḥ rathaḥ ṛte madhyamavāṭ mābhūt*)—युष्माकं रथः अस्मदीये यज्ञे मध्यमेन गमनेन वाहको मा भूत् । शीघ्रं युष्मान् वहतु (*yuṣmākaṃ rathaḥ asmadīye yajñe madhyamena gamanena vāhako mā bhūt | śīghraṃ yuṣmān vahatu*): the prayer is that the gods' chariot, without slow movement, bring the gods quickly for the sacrificer's sacrifice.

युष्मावत्सु आपिषु मा श्रमिष्म (*yuṣmāvatsu āpiṣu mā śramiṣma*)—युष्मत्सदृशेषु बंधुषु सत्तु श्रांता मा भूम (*yuṣmatsadṛśeṣu baṃdhuṣu sattu śrāṃtā mā bhūma*): the state prayed for is that we sacrificers, while we have kinsmen like the gods, may be without any weariness at all.

### Printed p. 133

*Running head: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 11, p. 133.*

#### Ṛk 5

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> प्र व॒ एको॑ मिमय॒ भूर्यागो॒ यन्मा॑ पि॒तेव॑ कित॒वं श॑शा॒स ।
> आ॒रे पाशा॑ आ॒रे अ॒घानि॑ देवा॒ मा माधि॑ पु॒त्रे विमि॑व ग्रभीष्ट ॥ ५ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> प्र । वः । एकः । मिमय । भूरि । आगः । यत् । मा । पिताइव । कितवं । शशास ।
> आरे । पाशाः । आरे । अघानि । देवाः । मा । मा । अधि । पुत्रे । विंइव । ग्रभीष्ट ॥ ५॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे देवा वो युष्माकं मध्येऽहमेकः सन् भूरि बहुलवागः पापं
> प्र मिमय । अहं प्रकर्षणाहिंसिषं । कथमस्माकं मध्ये त्वमेव इति चेदुच्यते।
> यद्यस्मान्मां पितेव कितवं यथापथप्रवृत्तं पुत्रं पितानुशास्ति तथा शशास । अनु-
> शिष्टं चकृढ्वे ॥ शासु अनुशिष्टावित्यस्माल्लिटि मध्यमस्य रूपं ॥ अपि च हे देवा
> युष्मदीयाः पाशा आरेऽस्मत्तो दूरदेशे संतु । अघानि पापानि चारे संतु ।
> मा माधि पुत्रे ॥ अधिः सप्तम्यर्थानुवादी ॥ पुत्रे पश्यति सति पितरं संतं विमिव
> पक्षिणमिव व्याधो मा ग्रभीष्ट । मा गृह्णिध्वं ॥ माजि लुजि हृग्रहोर्भ इति
> भत्वं ॥

> *he devā vo yuṣmākaṃ madhye 'hamekaḥ san bhūri bahulavāgaḥ pāpaṃ*
> *pra mimaya | ahaṃ prakarṣaṇāhiṃsiṣaṃ | kathamasmākaṃ madhye tvameva iti ceducyate|*
> *yadyasmānmāṃ piteva kitavaṃ yathāpathapravṛttaṃ putraṃ pitānuśāsti tathā śaśāsa | anu-*
> *śiṣṭaṃ cakṛḍhve ‖ śāsu anuśiṣṭāvityasmālliṭi madhyamasya rūpaṃ ‖ api ca he devā*
> *yuṣmadīyāḥ pāśā āre 'smatto dūradeśe saṃtu | aghāni pāpāni cāre saṃtu |*
> *mā mādhi putre ‖ adhiḥ saptamyarthānuvādī ‖ putre paśyati sati pitaraṃ saṃtaṃ vimiva*
> *pakṣiṇamiva vyādho mā grabhīṣṭa | mā gṛhṇidhvaṃ ‖ māji luji hṛgrahorbha iti*
> *bhatvaṃ ‖*

*Gist:* O gods, alone among you I have committed much sin; correct me as a father corrects a straying son; let your snares and sins be far from me; do not seize me, your son, as a fowler seizes a bird. The tail explains the perfect of शासु (*śāsu*) and the भ (*bha*) substitution in the aorist of ग्रह् (*grah*).

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

देवाः (*devāḥ*)—O gods; वः (*vaḥ*)—in your regard; एकः (*ekaḥ*)—being alone; भूरि (*bhūri*)—abundant; आगः (*āgaḥ*)—sin; प्र मिमय (*pra mimaya*)—I have committed; यत् (*yat*)—with which freedom and affection; पिता (*pitā*)—a father; कितवं इव (*kitavaṃ iva*)—as he corrects the fault of a son on a bad path and urges him onto the good path (so you also); मा (*mā*)—me; शशास (*śaśāsa*)—correct properly and direct to behave on the good path; पाशाः (*pāśāḥ*)—your snares; आरे (*āre*)—be far from us; अघानि (*aghāni*)—sins; आरे (*āre*)—be far; विमिव (*vimiva*)—(as a fowler) catches birds; पुत्रे (*putre*)—in me, who am as a son to you; मा मा अधि ग्रेभिष्ट (*mā mā adhi grebhiṣṭa*) [as supplied; the Saṃhitā has ग्रभीष्ट (*grabhīṣṭa*)]—do not cast a binding snare and bind me.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O gods, being alone, I have committed abundant sin against you. With the freedom and pride with which a father corrects the fault of a son on a bad path and urges him onto the good path, so do you also correct me properly and direct me to behave on the good path.

### Printed p. 134

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2 [sic, as supplied], Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 29, p. 134.*

(The Bhāvārtha continues from p. 133.) Let your snares be far from us, and let sins be far. As a fowler catches birds, do not cast a binding snare on me, who am as a son to you, and bind me.

**English Translation**

Alone among you, I have committed many offences, (correct them) as a father corrects a naughty(son): far from me, gods, be bonds; far from me be sins: seize not upon me (your) son as (a fowler) catches a bird.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

The meaning that Sāyaṇa gives to the first half of this Ṛk does not fit well. वः एकः भूरि आगः प्रमिमय (*vaḥ ekaḥ bhūri āgaḥ pramimaya*)—युष्माकं मध्यॆ अहमेकस्सन् बहुलं पापं अहं प्रकर्षेण अहिंसिषं (*yuṣmākaṃ madhyè ahamekassan bahulaṃ pāpaṃ ahaṃ prakarṣeṇa ahiṃsiṣaṃ*): addressing the gods, "I alone have committed abundant sins against you." यत् मां पितेव कितवं शशास (*yat māṃ piteva kitavaṃ śaśāsa*)—यथा अपथप्रवृत्तं पुत्रं पिता अनुशास्ति तथा अनुशिष्यं चकृड्वे (*yathā apathapravṛttaṃ putraṃ pitā anuśāsti tathā anuśiṣyaṃ cakṛḍve*) [as supplied]: as a father corrects a son on a bad path and punishes him so that he is on the right path, so you should instruct me, who have committed many sins; this is Sāyaṇa's rendering. The text remarks that this sense is not quite fitting; it might perhaps fit if the word मध्यॆ (*madhyè*) in Sāyaṇa's युष्माकं मध्यॆ (*yuṣmākaṃ madhyè*) / अस्माकं मध्यॆ (*asmākaṃ madhyè*) were omitted.

मा माधि पुत्रे विमिव (*mā mādhi putre vimiva*)—पुत्रे पश्यति सति पितरं संतं पक्षिणमिव व्याधः मा गृह्णीध्वं (*putre paśyati sati pitaraṃ saṃtaṃ pakṣiṇamiva vyādhaḥ mā gṛhṇīdhvaṃ*): as a fowler catches a bird, do not cast a snare on me, who am like your son, and bind me; and अघानि आरे संतु (*aghāni āre saṃtu*): the sins that bind us are also to be far from us. The gods are addressed with this prayer.

#### Ṛk 6

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अ॒र्वांचो॑ अ॒द्या भ॑वता यजत्रा॒ आ वो॒ हार्दि॒ भय॑मानो
> व्ययेयं ।
> त्राध्वं॑ नो देवा नि॒जुरो॒ वृक॑स्य॒ त्राध्वं॑ क॒र्ताद॑व॒पदो॑ यजत्राः॥

> *arvāṃco adyā bhavatā yajatrā ā vo hārdi bhayamāno*
> *vyayeyaṃ |*
> *trādhvaṃ no devā nijuro vṛkasya trādhvaṃ kartādavapado yajatrāḥ‖*

### Printed p. 135

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 11] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 135.*

(Ṛk 6 continues; its Saṃhitā was given on p. 134.)

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अर्वांचः । अद्य । भवत । यजत्राः । आ । वः । हार्दि । भयमानः । व्ययेयं ।
> त्राध्वं । नः । देवाः । निजुरः । वृकस्य । त्राध्वं । कर्तात् । अव्यपदः । यजत्राः ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे यजत्रा यष्टव्या देवा अद्येदानीमर्वांचोऽस्मदभिमुखा भवत ।
> वो युष्माकं हार्दि हृद्यवस्थितं रक्षणं भयमानो बिभ्यदहमाव्ययेयं ।
> आभिमुख्येन प्राप्नुयां ॥ व्यय गत् भौवादिकः ॥ हे देवा वृकस्य तस्करस्यारण्य-
> शुनो वा संबंधिनो निजुरो निहननात्॥ जूरी हिंसार्थः । अस्मात्संपदादिलक्षणो
> भावे क्विप् । छांदसो ह्रस्वः ॥ नोऽस्मान् त्राध्वं । त्रायध्वं ॥ तथा हे यजत्रा
> अवपद आपदः कर्तात्कर्तुः सकाशात् त्राध्वं । त्रायध्वं ॥

> *he yajatrā yaṣṭavyā devā adyedānīmarvāṃco 'smadabhimukhā bhavata |*
> *vo yuṣmākaṃ hārdi hṛdyavasthitaṃ rakṣaṇaṃ bhayamāno bibhyadahamāvyayeyaṃ |*
> *ābhimukhyena prāpnuyāṃ ‖ vyaya gat bhauvādikaḥ ‖ he devā vṛkasya taskarasyāraṇya-*
> *śuno vā saṃbaṃdhino nijuro nihananāt‖ jūrī hiṃsārthaḥ | asmātsaṃpadādilakṣaṇo*
> *bhāve kvip | chāṃdaso hrasvaḥ ‖ no 'smān trādhvaṃ | trāyadhvaṃ ‖ tathā he yajatrā*
> *avapada āpadaḥ kartātkartuḥ sakāśāt trādhvaṃ | trāyadhvaṃ ‖*

*Gist:* O adorable gods, turn to us today; fearing, may I obtain your heartfelt protection; protect us from the wolf's (or thief's) violence and from the one who causes calamity. The tail explains व्यय (*vyaya*) (root *vyay*, bhū-class), निजुरः (*nijuraḥ*) (from the root *jūr*, with a Vedic shortening) and त्राध्वं (*trādhvaṃ*) = त्रायध्वं (*trāyadhvaṃ*).

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

यजत्राः (*yajatrāḥ*)—O gods worthy of sacrifice; अद्य (*adya*)—at this time of the sacrifice; अर्वांचः (*arvāṃcaḥ*)—facing us; भवत (*bhavata*)—be; वः (*vaḥ*)—your; हार्दि (*hārdi*)—protection given with affection; भयमानः (*bhayamānaḥ*)—I, who am afraid; आ व्ययेयं (*ā vyayeyaṃ*)—may I obtain fully; देवाः (*devāḥ*)—O gods; वृकस्य (*vṛkasya*)—of the thief or the wolf; निजुरः (*nijuraḥ*)—from the violence; नः (*naḥ*)—us; त्राध्वं (*trādhvaṃ*)—protect; यजत्राः (*yajatrāḥ*)—O revered gods; अवपदः (*avapadaḥ*)—calamities; कर्तात् (*kartāt*)—from one who causes; त्राध्वं (*trādhvaṃ*)—protect.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O gods worthy of sacrifice, be facing us at this time of the sacrifice. May I, being afraid, obtain fully your protection given with affection. O revered gods, protect us from the violence of the thief. Protect us also from those who cause calamities.

**English Translation**

Adorable (deities), be present to-day, that, apprehensive of danger, I may feel sure of your hearty (support): protect us, gods, from the rapacity of the wolf: protect us, adorable (deities), from him who would work us misfortune.

### Printed p. 136

*Running head: [136] Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṇḍala 2 as supplied, Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 29].*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

यजत्राः (*yajatrāḥ*)—the word has been explained as "worthy of sacrifice". By it the gods worthy of sacrifice are addressed: because the sacrificers are fearful, the gods' presence at the times of sacrifice is prayed for, so that the sacrificers may receive the gods' wholehearted protection. After the prayer for protection from fear caused by the gods, protection for the sacrificers is prayed for here from the visible harmful creatures, the thief and the wolf, and from wicked people who cause calamity.

वृकस्य (*vṛkasya*)—तस्करस्य अरण्यतुनो वा (*taskarasya araṇyatuno vā*) [sic, as supplied, probably for *araṇyaśuno*]. The many meanings of the word *vṛka* were explained earlier. Here the meaning given is the thief or the wolf (wild dog) and other creatures that do violence.

अवपदः (*avapadaḥ*)—आपदः (*āpadaḥ*). The word *avapadaḥ* is explained as calamities.

यजत्राः अद्य अर्वांचः भवत (*yajatrāḥ adya arvāṃcaḥ bhavata*)—हे यष्टव्या देवा इदानीमस्मदभिमुखा भवत (*he yaṣṭavyā devā idānīmasmadabhimukhā bhavata*). O gods worthy of sacrifice, that is, worshipped in the sacrifice; "now", that is, at this time while the sacrifice is going on: it is prayed that you come facing us (in front of us) and be seated.

वः हार्दि भयमानः आ व्यवेयं (*vaḥ hārdi bhayamānaḥ ā vyaveyaṃ*) [as supplied; the Saṃhitā and bhāṣya have व्ययेयं (*vyayeyaṃ*)]—हे देवा युष्माकं हृद्यवस्थितं रक्षणं बिभ्यदहमाभिमुख्येन प्राप्नुयां (*he devā yuṣmākaṃ hṛdyavasthitaṃ rakṣaṇaṃ bibhyadahamābhimukhyena prāpnuyāṃ*). O gods, it is in your minds to remove the fear I have and protect me; may I obtain that protection. What is the fear that has come upon me?—यजत्राः निजुरः वृकस्य अवपदः कर्तात् त्राध्वं (*yajatrāḥ nijuraḥ vṛkasya avapadaḥ kartāt trādhvaṃ*): O gods worthy of sacrifice, remove the fear that may come from the violent wolf, the thief, and wicked people who cause calamities, and protect me; thus the gods are prayed to.

#### Ṛk 7

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> माहं म॒घोनो॑ वरुण प्रि॒यस्य॑ भूरि॒दान्न॒ आ वि॑दं॒ शून॑मा॒पेः।
> मा रा॒यो रा॑जन्तु॒यमा॒दव॑ स्थां बृ॒हद्व॑देम वि॒दथे॑ सु॒वीराः॑॥

> *māhaṃ maghono varuṇa priyasya bhūridānna ā vidaṃ śūnamāpeḥ|*
> *mā rāyo rājantuyamādava sthāṃ bṛhadvadema vidathe suvīrāḥ‖*

### Printed p. 137

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 11] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 137.*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> मा । अहं । मघोनः । वरुण । प्रियस्य । भूर्इदान्वः । आ । विदं । शूनं । आपेः ।
> मा । रायः । राजन् । सुयमात् । अव । स्थां । बृहत् । वदेम । विदथे । सु-
> वीराः ॥ ७ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे वरुण मघोनो धनवतः प्रियस्यानुकूलस्य भूरिदाव्यो भूरेर्बहुलस्य
> दातुराड्यस्याहं मा आविदं । मावेदयानि । आवेदनं विज्ञापनं मा करवाणि । किं
> तदनावेद्यं । आपेर्ज्ञातेः शूनं शून्यं दारिद्रं ॥ वर्णलोपश्कांदसः ॥ यद्वा
> शूनं गतं प्राप्तं दारिद्रं ॥ टुओश्वि गतिवृद्ध्योः । श्विदितो निष्ठायामितीट् प्रति-
> षेधः । यजादित्वात्संप्रसारणं । ओदितश्चति नकारः । वृषादित्वादाद्युदात्तत्वं ॥
> एतदुक्तं भवति । अस्मदीयाः पुत्रादयः क्षुधिता इति प्रभुप्रभुसमीपेऽभिधाय
> याचमानो मा भूवमिति । हे राजन् सुयमात् । सुष्टु नियम्यतेऽनेन पुरुषो
> गृह इति सुयमः ॥ ईषद्दुःसुष्टिति खलु ॥ ताद्बशाद्रायो धनान्मावस्थां ।
> अवयुत्य स्थितो मा भूवं । वयं सुवीराः शोभनपुत्रा विदथे यज्ञॆ गृहे
> वर्तमानाः संतो बृहत् फौढं स्तोत्रं वदेम । उच्चारयाम ॥

> *he varuṇa maghono dhanavataḥ priyasyānukūlasya bhūridāvyo bhūrerbahulasya*
> *dāturāḍyasyāhaṃ mā āvidaṃ | māvedayāni | āvedanaṃ vijñāpanaṃ mā karavāṇi | kiṃ*
> *tadanāvedyaṃ | āperjñāteḥ śūnaṃ śūnyaṃ dāridraṃ ‖ varṇalopaśkāṃdasaḥ ‖ yadvā*
> *śūnaṃ gataṃ prāptaṃ dāridraṃ ‖ ṭuośvi gativṛddhyoḥ | śvidito niṣṭhāyāmitīṭ prati-*
> *ṣedhaḥ | yajāditvātsaṃprasāraṇaṃ | oditaścati nakāraḥ | vṛṣāditvādādyudāttatvaṃ ‖*
> *etaduktaṃ bhavati | asmadīyāḥ putrādayaḥ kṣudhitā iti prabhuprabhusamīpe 'bhidhāya*
> *yācamāno mā bhūvamiti | he rājan suyamāt | suṣṭu niyamyate 'nena puruṣo*
> *gṛha iti suyamaḥ ‖ īṣadduḥsuṣṭiti khalu ‖ tādbaśādrāyo dhanānmāvasthāṃ |*
> *avayutya sthito mā bhūvaṃ | vayaṃ suvīrāḥ śobhanaputrā vidathe yajñè gṛhe*
> *vartamānāḥ saṃto bṛhat phauḍhaṃ stotraṃ vadema | uccārayāma ‖*

*Gist:* O Varuṇa, may I not have to report to a wealthy, generous patron the poverty of my kinsmen, nor be left bereft of wealth; with good sons we shall recite a sublime hymn in the sacrificial house. The tail explains शूनं (*śūnaṃ*) (loss of a letter, Vedic; or from the root *śvi*, with sūtras on the augment and contraction, numbers not legible in this note) and सुयमात् (*suyamāt*).

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

वरुण (*varuṇa*)—O Varuṇa; मघोनः (*maghonaḥ*)—of the wealthy, प्रियस्य (*priyasya*)—the favourable, भूरिदाव्यः (*bhūridāvyaḥ*)—the lavish giver, the rich man (before him); अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I; आपेः (*āpeḥ*)—of my near kinsman; शूनं (*śūnaṃ*)—the poverty; मा आ विदं (*mā ā vidaṃ*)—may I not have to state; राजन् (*rājan*)—O Varuṇa, lord; सुयमात् (*suyamāt*)—from [wealth] accumulated in due order; रायः (*rāyaḥ*)—from wealth; मा अव स्थां (*mā ava sthāṃ*)—may I not be left empty; (वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we); सुवीराः (*suvīrāḥ*)—together with excellent sons; विदथे (*vidathe*)—in the sacrificial house; बृहत् (*bṛhat*)—a sublime hymn; वदेम (*vadema*)—we recite.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Varuṇa, may I not have to go before the rich man, who is wealthy, favourable and a lavish giver, and say that my sons and other near kinsmen suffer from poverty, asking him for wealth. May I not be left empty of wealth accumulated in due order. With excellent sons we shall recite a sublime hymn in the sacrificial house.

*Note on readings: the Saṃhitā has भूरिदान्न (*bhūridānna*), the Pada भूर्इदान्वः (*bhūridānvaḥ*) and the bhāṣya भूरिदाव्यो (*bhūridāvyo*), as supplied; the Pada's भूर्इदान्वः (*bhūridānvaḥ*) and the Pratipadārtha's भूरिदाव्यः (*bhūridāvyaḥ*) differ. The Pada ends "॥ ೭ ॥" as printed.*

### Printed p. 138

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala "3" [sic, as supplied; elsewhere 2], Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 29, p. 138.*

**English Translation**

May I never (have to) represent, Varuna, the destitution of a (once)
opulent, dear, and munificent kinsman : may I never, royal Varuna, be
devoid of well-regulated riches : (and) may we, blessed with excellent
descendants, worthily glorify you at this sacrifice.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

By the word वरुण (*varuṇa*) the Āditya is addressed here. That Varuṇa too is one of the twelve Ādityas has been explained earlier.

मघोनः (*maghonaḥ*)—धनवतः (*dhanavataḥ*) (of the wealthy). The word *magha* is read among the names for wealth by Yāska (Ni. 3-9). The meaning is explained as "one who has wealth".

प्रियस्य भूरिदान्वः आपेः शुनं मा आ विदं (*priyasya bhūridānvaḥ āpeḥ śunaṃ mā ā vidaṃ*) (as supplied, with शुनं (*śunaṃ*))—the bhāṣya says, in Sanskrit, that near a rich man who gives abundantly, "our sons and others are hungry", stating their poverty, I should not become a supplicant. In Kannada: the prayer is explained thus: "may we, the sacrificers, not have to go to a wealthy man who is benevolent and gives liberally, say that our sons and others are fasting from poverty, and beg wealth".

शूनं (*śūnaṃ*)—the bhāṣya gives शून्यं (*śūnyaṃ*) or शूनं गतं प्राप्तं दारिद्र्यं (*śūnaṃ gataṃ prāptaṃ dāridryaṃ*). The meaning given for the word *śūna* is: the absence of any kind of thing, or the poverty (destitution) that brings one to a state of emptiness.

राजन् सुयमात् रायः मा अवस्थाम् (*rājan suyamāt rāyaḥ mā avasthām*)—the explanation सुष्टु नियम्यते अनेन पुरुषो गृहे इति सुयमः (*suṣṭu niyamyate anena puruṣo gṛhe iti suyamaḥ*) is given. Addressing Varuṇa by the word *rājan*, it is prayed that by this Varuṇa's favour the wealth accumulated by us, the sacrificers, may never be destroyed.

बृहद्वदेम विदथे सुवीराः (*bṛhadvadema vidathe suvīrāḥ*)—"we, the sacrificers, recite hymns in the sacrifices together with our excellent sons; therefore cause favour to be shown to us": thus the prayer is made.

### Printed p. 139

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 12, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 139.*

## Sūkta 30 (Maṇḍala 2)

*The thirtieth sūkta.*

**Sāyaṇa's introduction to the sūkta** (Sanskrit, transcribed; see the Anuvāda below for the sense):

> ऋतं देवायेत्येकादशर्चमष्टमं सूक्तं गार्त्समदं । अंत्या जगती
> शिष्टास्त्रिष्टुभः । प्र हि क्रतुमित्येषेंद्रासोमदेवताका । सरस्वति त्वमस्मानित्यर्धर्चः
> सारस्वतः । यो नः सनुत्य इत्येषा बार्हस्पत्या । अंत्या मारुती । शिष्टा ऐंद्र्यः ।
> तथा चानुक्रांतं । ऋतमेकादश जगत्त्यंतं षष्ठ्यैंद्रासोमी सरस्वति त्वमिति
> सारस्वतोऽर्धर्चो यो नो बार्हस्पत्या तं वो मारुतीति ॥ गतो विनियोगः ॥

*Gist:* *ṛtaṃ devāya* is the eighth sūkta of Anuvāka 3, of eleven ṛks, of the Gārtsamada seer; the last ṛk is *jagatī*, the rest *triṣṭubh*; the deities are named ṛk by ṛk (Indra; Indra and Soma; Sarasvatī; Bṛhaspati; the Maruts); the Anukramaṇī is quoted; the application is stated as already given.

**Anuvāda (Kannada).** The sūkta ऋतं देवाय (*ṛtaṃ devāya*) is the eighth sūkta in the third anuvāka. It has eleven ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Gṛtsamada; the last ṛk of the sūkta is of *jagatī* metre; the remaining ṛks are of *triṣṭubh* metre. In this sūkta the ṛk प्र हि क्रतुं (*pra hi kratuṃ*) (the 6th ṛk) has Indra and Soma as deity; the first half of the ṛk सरस्वति त्वमस्मान् (*sarasvati tvamasmān*) (the 8th ṛk) has Sarasvatī as deity; the ṛk यो नः सनुत्य (*yo naḥ sanutya*) (the 9th ṛk) has Bṛhaspati as deity; the last ṛk (the 11th ṛk) has the Maruts as deity. The remaining ṛks (1-5, 7, the latter half of 8, and 10) have Indra as deity. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said ऋतमेकादश जगत्तंतं षष्ठैंद्रासोमी सरस्वति त्वमिति सारस्वतोऽर्धर्चो यो नो बार्हस्पत्या तं वो मारुतीति (*ṛtamekādaśa jagattaṃtaṃ ṣaṣṭhaiṃdrāsomī sarasvati tvamiti sārasvato 'rdharco yo no bārhaspatyā taṃ vo mārutīti*). The application has been stated earlier.

**Sūkta—30**

Maṇḍala 2 ‖ Anuvāka 3 ‖ Sūkta 30 ‖
Aṣṭaka 2 ‖ Adhyāya 7 ‖ Varga 12, 13 ‖
Number of ṛks in the Sūkta: 11 ·
Ṛṣi: Gṛtsamada · Devatā: 1-5, 7, the latter half of 8, 10, Indra; 6, Indra and Soma; the first half of 8, Sarasvatī; 9, Bṛhaspati; 11, the Maruts · Chandas: 1-10 *triṣṭubh*; 11 *jagatī*.

### Printed p. 140

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 30, p. 140.*

#### Ṛk 1

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> ऋ॒तं दे॒वाय॑ कृण्व॒ते स॑वि॒त्र इंद्रा॑याहि॒घ्ने न र॑मंत॒ आपः॑ !
> अह॑रहर्यात्य॒क्तुर॒पां किया॒त्या प्र॑थ॒मः सर्ग॑ आसां ॥ १ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> ऋतं । देवाय । कृण्वते । सवित्रे । इंद्राय । अहिघ्ने । न । रमंते । आपः ।
> अहः।अहः । याति । अक्तुः । अपां । कियति । आ । प्रथमः । सर्गः । आसां ॥ १ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> ऋतमुदकं वृष्टिलक्षणं कृण्वते कुर्वते देवाय, द्योतमानाय सवित्रे
> सर्वस्य प्रेरकायाहिघ्नेऽहिं वृत्रं हतवत इंद्राय यागार्थमापो न रमंते ।
> तस्माद्भेतोर्भित्या नोपरता भवंति ॥ तस्माद्वृत्ययेन चतुर्थी ॥ अपि तु शासा-
> मपामक्तुः सेचकं स्तोतोऽहरहः प्रत्यहं याति । गच्छति । आसां प्रथमः सर्गः
> प्राथमिकं जन्म कियात्या ! कियति काले निष्पन्नं । आकारः प्रश्ने । किंशब्द आक्षेपे ।
> यावति काले ता अजायंत स कालः कियान् । किं परिमाणं तस्य कालस्य । न किमपि ।
> नित्या एव हि ता इत्यर्थः ॥

*Gist:* For Indra's sacrifice the waters never rest; their stream flows every day; the question "in how long a time was their first creation?" implies that they are eternal.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

ऋतं (*ṛtaṃ*)—the water in the form of rain; कृण्वते (*kṛṇvate*)—to the maker; देवाय (*devāya*)—to the divine one; सवित्रे (*savitre*)—to the impeller of all affairs; अहिघ्ने (*ahighne*)—to the slayer of Vṛtra; इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—for the sacrifice directed to Indra; आपः (*āpaḥ*)—the waters; न रमंते (*na ramaṃte*)—never find rest; अपां (*apāṃ*)—of (such) waters; अक्तुः (*aktuḥ*)—the flow; अहरहः (*aharahaḥ*)—every day; याति (*yāti*)—flows; आसां (*āsāṃ*)—of them; प्रथमः (*prathamaḥ*)—the first; सर्गः (*sargaḥ*)—creation; कियात्या (*kiyātyā*)—at what time did it happen?

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The waters never find rest for the sake of the sacrifice directed to Indra, who makes the water in the form of rain, who is divine, who is the impeller of all affairs, and the slayer of Vṛtra. The flow of such waters flows every day. At what time did their first creation happen? That is to say, they are without beginning.

### Printed p. 141

*Running head: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 12, p. 141.*

**Printed English translation**

The waters cease not (to flow in libations) to the divine Indra, the sender of rain, the animator (of all), the slayer of Ahi; day by day proceeds the current of the waters; at what period of time was their first creation.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

ऋतं-वृष्टिलक्षणं उदकं (*ṛtaṃ-vṛṣṭilakṣaṇaṃ udakaṃ*): Although the word *ṛta* has many meanings (truth, sacrifice, water), here the sense is given as rain-water.

देवाय— द्योतमानाय (*devāya— dyotamānāya*): Since the word *deva* is said to have the grounds of usage dāna (giving), dīpana (illumining), dyotana (shining) and dyusthāna-sthiti (dwelling in heaven), the sense is explained, among the various etymologies, as "the one who shines".

सवित्रे—सर्वस्य प्रेरकाय (*savitre—sarvasya prerakāya*): Yāska has explained the sense as सविता—सर्वस्य प्रसविता (*savitā—sarvasya prasavitā*) (Si. 10-32); it is described as the one who impels everything.

आहिघ्ने—वृत्रं हतवते (*āhighne—vṛtraṃ hatavate*): The word *ahi* is stated among the names of cloud (Ni. 2-21) and among the names of water (Ni. 2-24), and is explained as अहिरयनात् (*ahirayanāt*) (Ni. 10-43). Here the sense "the demon Vṛtra" is given, and he is described as the one who destroyed Vṛtra.

इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*): The etymology of the word *indra* (Ni. 10-8) is also stated in many ways. It is explained that for the sacrifice performed in honour of Indra, who creates rain-water, who shines always, who impels everything in its own way, and who destroyed the demon Vṛtra, the waters flow in fear; and that this current of waters flows forward every day without the slightest hindrance.

आसां सर्गः प्रथमः कियत्या (*āsāṃ sargaḥ prathamaḥ kiyatyā*): The question is asked: "the first creation of these waters, कियति काले निष्पन्नं (*kiyati kāle niṣpannaṃ*), took place when?" Then it is considered: सः कालः कियान्, किं परिमाणं तस्य कालस्य (*saḥ kālaḥ kiyān, kiṃ parimāṇaṃ tasya kālasya*), what is the measure of that time? The answer is given: न किमपि (*na kimapi*), the measure of that time cannot be known by anyone; and it is established that नित्या एव हि ताः (*nityā eva hi tāḥ*), those currents of water are eternal.

### Printed p. 142

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 3, Sūkta 30, p. 142.*

#### Ṛk 2

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> यो वृ॒त्राय॒ सिन॒मत्राभ॑रिष्य॒त्प्रतं जनि॑त्रि वि॒दुष॑ उवाच ।
> प॒थो रदं॑ती॒रनु॒ जोष॑मस्मै दि॒वेदि॑वे॒ धुन॑यो यं॒त्यर्थ॑० ॥ २ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> यः । वृत्राय । सिनं । अत्र । अभरिष्यत् । प्र । तं । जनित्री । विदुषे । उवाच ।
> पथः । रदंतीः । अनु । जोषं । अस्मै । दिवेऽदिवे । धुनयः । यंति । अर्थ० ॥ २ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> यः पुरुषो वृत्रायासुराय सिनमन्नमत्र पाकस्थान आहर्तुमभरिष्यत्
> संपादितवान् तं पुरुषं जनित्रि जनयित्रींद्रस्य मातादितिर्विदुषेऽभिज्ञायेंद्राय
> प्रोवाच । प्रोक्तवती । प्रवचनस्य हननार्थत्वात् तं वृत्रं हतवानिति शेषः । अपि
> चास्मा अस्येंद्रस्यानुजोषमनुकाममिच्छामनुसृत्य पथो मार्गान् रदंतीर्विलि-
> खंत्यो धुनयो नद्यो दिवेदिवे प्रतिदिवसमर्थमर्तव्यं गंतव्यं समुद्रं प्रति
> यंति । गच्छंति ॥

*Gist:* The man who offered food to Vṛtra was pointed out by Indra's mother Aditi to Indra, who slew him; for Indra's pleasure the rivers cut their paths and flow daily to the ocean.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

यः (*yaḥ*)—the man who; वृत्राय (*vṛtrāya*)—to the demon; सिनं (*sinaṃ*)—food; अत्र (*atra*)—in this place of cooking, to offer; अभरिष्यत् (*abhariṣyat*)—procured (food); तं (*taṃ*)—that man; जनित्रि (*janitri*)—Aditi, Indra's mother; विदुषे (*viduṣe*)—to the wise Indra; प्र उवाच (*pra uvāca*)—declared; अस्मै (*asmai*)—of this Indra; अनु जोषं (*anu joṣaṃ*)—following the wish; पथः (*pathaḥ*)—paths; रदंतीः (*radaṃtīḥ*)—digging out; धुनयः (*dhunayaḥ*)—the rivers; दिवेदिवे (*divedive*)—every day; अर्थं (*arthaṃ*)—to their destination, the ocean; यंति (*yaṃti*)—go and join.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The man who procured food in this place of cooking in order to offer it to the demon, that man Aditi, Indra's mother, declared to the wise Indra. Following the wish of this Indra, the rivers, digging out their paths of their own accord, go and join every day the ocean, their destination.

### Printed p. 143

*Running head: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 12, p. 143.*

**Printed English translation**

His mother, (Aditi), declared to him the man who had offered (sacrificial) food to Vritra; obedient to his pleasure, the rivers, tracing out their paths. flow day by day to their object, (the ocean).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

वृत्राय—असुराय (*vṛtrāya—asurāya*): The meaning of the word *vṛtra* is stated by Yāska (Ni. 2-16); it is also read among the names of cloud (Si. 2-22) [sic as supplied] and stated among the names of wealth (Ni. 3-9). Here the sense "demons" is given.

सिनं— अन्नं (*sinaṃ— annaṃ*): The word *sina* is read among the names of food (Ni. 3-9) and is explained as सिनमन्नं भवति सिनाति भूतानि (*sinamannaṃ bhavati sināti bhūtāni*) (Ni. 5-5).
